# SOURCE NOTES FOR: Liquidity: buy-side, sell-side and the draw

Each block below is one concept section from study notes of a specific ICT video. Blocks are ordered 2022 Mentorship → 2024 Mentorship → 2026 Lectures, so you can see how the teaching evolved. Timestamp links inside are real.


######## 2022 MENTORSHIP ########

=== [2022 Ep1 — ICT Mentorship 2022 Introduction (ID kt6V4ai60fI)] — Draw on liquidity — study prompts, not trade setups  (aliases ICT uses: "where price will likely draw to next")
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** He will draw on charts beforehand where he expects price to draw to next; this is for study, not a trade setup and not an enticement to take a live trade. Students must not grow dependent on him calling moves; independent decision-making is the goal so results (good or bad) are owned by the student.
- **How to identify:** Levels he marks before the fact.
- **How to use:** Anticipate on your own, then check yourself against what he reveals or reviews.
- **Example in this video:** n/a.
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:22](https://youtu.be/kt6V4ai60fI?t=442) — not setups, study where price will draw; [0:09:43](https://youtu.be/kt6V4ai60fI?t=583) — avoid codependency.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** none.

=== [2022 Ep1 — ICT Mentorship 2022 Introduction (ID kt6V4ai60fI)] — Buy-side / sell-side liquidity (liquidity matrix)  (aliases ICT uses: "buy stops", "liquidity matrix")
- **Kind:** definition (brief)
- **What ICT says:** Markets work within a liquidity matrix: buy side and sell side. His charts stay simple: a line for a level he wants to see breached, and a line for a level he wants price to reach up into. Buy stops sit above a high.
- **How to identify:** Highs (buy stops above) and lows (sell stops below) marked with simple lines; avoid "chart graffiti".
- **How to use:** Target the resting liquidity (e.g. buy stops above a high for a long).
- **Example in this video:** ES 2m long targeting buy stops above a prior high (see OTE concept).
- **Timestamps:** [0:24:11](https://youtu.be/kt6V4ai60fI?t=1451) — above this high is buy stops, targeting into that; [0:26:33](https://youtu.be/kt6V4ai60fI?t=1593) — simple lines; liquidity matrix of buy side and sell side.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** To be taught in detail later in the series.

=== [2022 Ep2 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 2 (ID tmeCWULSTHc)] — Draw on liquidity (DOL)  (aliases ICT uses: "where it's drawing to", "paperclip and magnet")
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Price is like a paperclip; price levels and seasonality act like a magnet pulling it toward certain levels. Price draws to one of two things: stops (liquidity) or an imbalance. Above old highs = buy stops; below old lows = sell stops. Speed and magnitude of the move to a level is learned by experience ("a feel"), not transferable.
- **How to identify:** On the daily chart, mark swing highs/lows (where liquidity sits) and imbalances. Most of the draw on liquidity "is predominantly found on" the daily timeframe, so most analysis should be linked to it.
- **How to use:** Define the weekly direction, then use the daily chart to see where you are within the weekly range expansion and which short-term lows/highs (stops) are the next draw.
- **Example in this video:** NQ daily: in a weak market, each short-term low has sell stops beneath it; the old daily low is the larger objective.
- **Timestamps:** [0:14:45](https://youtu.be/tmeCWULSTHc?t=885) — paperclip/magnet analogy; [0:16:20](https://youtu.be/tmeCWULSTHc?t=980) — daily chart: swing highs/lows, draw on liquidity found mostly on daily; [0:17:08](https://youtu.be/tmeCWULSTHc?t=1028) — draws to stops or an imbalance.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He explicitly says this is not auction theory and his boxes are not supply and demand.

=== [2022 Ep2 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 2 (ID tmeCWULSTHc)] — Buy-side / sell-side liquidity and the stop hunt before a big move  (aliases ICT uses: "buy stops", "sell stops", "sucker play", "engineered liquidity", "inducing shorts")
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** Before any significant price move lower, always anticipate some measure of a stop hunt on buy stops / a short-term high taken out; when expecting higher prices, generally a short-term low and its sell stops get taken before a pronounced rally. Says this occurs almost daily. When buy stops are run, they become market orders flooding in, giving smart money (who want to sell high) willing buyers as counterparty.
- **How to identify:** Consolidation forming a short-term high and short-term low; relative equal highs (retail "resistance" with stops above); traders who were short trail stops just above the recent high.
- **How to use:** In a bearish week, wait for the run above the old high (buy stops), then look for the entry signature on lower timeframes. Do not sell simply because highs were taken.
- **Example in this video:** NQ hourly/15m, Friday (last day of the week): bearish all week, sell-off Thursday, overnight consolidation. Friday: price first trades down below the consolidation low (sell stops), inducing breakout shorts ("sucker play"), then runs up through relative equal highs/old highs (buy stops above where shorts trailed their stops). That run is the setup context.
- **Timestamps:** [0:20:19](https://youtu.be/tmeCWULSTHc?t=1219) — consolidation; sell stops taken first to induce shorts; [0:22:01](https://youtu.be/tmeCWULSTHc?t=1321) — buy stops as counterparty for smart money; note: anticipate a stop hunt before a significant move; [0:23:39](https://youtu.be/tmeCWULSTHc?t=1419) — relative equal highs, retail resistance; [0:25:18](https://youtu.be/tmeCWULSTHc?t=1518) — larger pool above in sync with downtrend.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He says "algorithms" attack the buy-stop pool; he rejects the buying/selling pressure explanation in favor of liquidity.

=== [2022 Ep2 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 2 (ID tmeCWULSTHc)] — Relative equal highs/lows  (aliases ICT uses: "cleanly delivered", "relative equal highs")
- **Kind:** definition (applied)
- **What ICT says:** Two highs at basically the same level are seen by retail as resistance; textbooks say place a buy stop just above clear resistance when short. These levels hold briefly, but most of the time the market runs them before a meaningful move.
- **How to identify:** Two or more highs (or lows) at nearly the same price.
- **How to use:** Treat as an engineered liquidity pool and a likely target before the real move.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 15m Friday: high right before the drop matches an earlier high; price runs through them.
- **Timestamps:** [0:23:39](https://youtu.be/tmeCWULSTHc?t=1419); [0:24:29](https://youtu.be/tmeCWULSTHc?t=1469).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** none.

=== [2022 Ep2 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 2 (ID tmeCWULSTHc)] — Premium / discount / equilibrium (dealing range split at 50%)  (aliases ICT uses: "split the range", "50 level", "liquidity matrix")
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:** Take the current range (low of day to high of day so far) and split it with a Fibonacci 50% level. Above 50% is a premium (expensive, "algorithmic stance"); below 50% is a discount. Markets can stay in premium a while. When short, check where you are in the range: selling an FVG in premium, the algorithm will seek discount; it reprices lower regardless of buyers (buyers get "squeezed"; "bear squeeze/bull squeeze" are excuses for not understanding the algorithm).
- **How to identify:** Fib from high to low (or low to high) of the day's range; 50% level.
- **How to use:** Sell in premium, target liquidity/imbalance in discount (below 50%) — sell stops below an old low and nearby imbalances.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 2m Friday: FVG short formed in the premium of the day's range; price drove below the 50% level, raided sell stops under a low and filled the buy-side imbalance there.
- **Timestamps:** [0:34:49](https://youtu.be/tmeCWULSTHc?t=2089) — liquidity matrix; range low/high of day; [0:35:36](https://youtu.be/tmeCWULSTHc?t=2136) — 50% Fib, premium vs discount; [0:36:26](https://youtu.be/tmeCWULSTHc?t=2186) — algorithm in a sell program reprices lower; [0:37:17](https://youtu.be/tmeCWULSTHc?t=2237) — sell stops below 50% as counterparty to cover.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The day's range here is "low of the day and high of the day thus far".

=== [2022 Ep3 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 3 (ID nQfHZ2DEJ8c)] — Buy-side / sell-side liquidity (marking the pools)  (aliases ICT uses: "buy stops", "sell stops", "relative equal highs", "retail support/resistance")
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** Old low → sell stops below. Relative equal highs → buy stops above. If equal highs exist and they are higher than an older single high, he uses the equal highs as the reference. Liquidity location is pure logic: retail places stops beyond obvious support/resistance. Buying/selling "pressure" has no bearing on where price goes. Highs left just below a level "building up more interest" engineer liquidity for a later run.
- **How to identify:** Mark the 15m buy-side and sell-side liquidity pools, then drop to 3m/2m/1m.
- **How to use:** The run into these pools is the precondition for the MSS. When the market runs buy stops, smart money that bought lower sells to "high-seeking buyers".
- **Example in this video:** NQ 15m: shaded sell-stop area below ~14,600; relative equal highs above.
- **Timestamps:** [0:01:04](https://youtu.be/nQfHZ2DEJ8c?t=64) — old low and relative equal highs; [0:12:56](https://youtu.be/nQfHZ2DEJ8c?t=776) — sell-stop zone below 14,600; [0:28:03](https://youtu.be/nQfHZ2DEJ8c?t=1683) — highs building interest = engineering liquidity.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** none.

=== [2022 Ep3 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 3 (ID nQfHZ2DEJ8c)] — Internal range liquidity (IRL)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** IRL = short-term lows or highs inside a price leg that price is retracing back into; i.e., a short-term high or low with stops above/below it, or an imbalance in that same range.
- **How to identify:** Within the current leg, swing points and FVGs.
- **How to use:** Targets/partials when retracing within a leg.
- **Example in this video:** Sell stops below internal short-term lows in the bearish NQ leg.
- **Timestamps:** [0:34:28](https://youtu.be/nQfHZ2DEJ8c?t=2068); [0:35:15](https://youtu.be/nQfHZ2DEJ8c?t=2115).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** External range liquidity not defined here.

=== [2022 Ep5 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 5 (ID N29ZJ-o31xs)] — Buy program / spooling  (aliases ICT uses: "buy program", "sell program", "spooling")
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** A buy program is when the algorithms continuously offer higher prices (spooling), regardless of volume; sometimes volume is good, sometimes not — that inconsistency is the signature of manipulation.
- **How to identify:** After a stop hunt, persistent repricing in one direction with small retracements.
- **How to use:** Get long after the stop hunt and hold rather than scalping small moves.
- **Example in this video:** ES afternoon rally after the 1:30-area swing-low raid.
- **Timestamps:** [0:33:22](https://youtu.be/N29ZJ-o31xs?t=2002); [0:34:10](https://youtu.be/N29ZJ-o31xs?t=2050).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** none.

=== [2022 Ep6 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 6 (ID Bkt8B3kLATQ)] — Internal vs external range liquidity (IRL / ERL) for partials
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:** Take the price run (low to high) and put a Fib on it: 50% is equilibrium; below is discount. An FVG in the discount, inside the range, is internal range liquidity — first target for partials. The stops below the range's lows are external range liquidity — the final target. "Partials internal range, external range your trade."
- **How to identify:** Range low to range high; FVG inside below 50%; old lows beyond the range.
- **How to use:** Scale out at IRL, take the rest at ERL.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Feb 3: bullish FVG below 50% = IRL partial at 14,675; sell stops under equal lows = ERL exit at 14,647.
- **Timestamps:** [0:29:29](https://youtu.be/Bkt8B3kLATQ?t=1769) — exit strategy using external range liquidity; [0:45:00](https://youtu.be/Bkt8B3kLATQ?t=2700) — FVG in discount = IRL; [0:45:49](https://youtu.be/Bkt8B3kLATQ?t=2749) — stops below lows = ERL.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Extends Ep. 3's IRL definition with its counterpart ERL.

=== [2022 Ep7 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 7 (ID G8-z91acgG4)] — 8:30 a.m. reference line ("crosshairs") and looking left for liquidity
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Draw a vertical line at 8:30 a.m. New York time. That's the point you start looking back to the left from.
  - Mark the first low and the first high to the left of 8:30. If a swing point sits inside another candle's range, use the extreme (the candle that holds the outermost price), because that's where the liquidity rests.
  - Relative equal highs further left are buy-side liquidity. Retail traders see them as "strong resistance" because price failed there twice, and the market then runs right through it.
  - Use a first target (the nearest liquidity) and a longer-term intraday target further left.
  - "Algorithms run on time and price, not price and time." Refer to time first (8:30), then find the first low to its left.
- **How to identify:** Use the 15m chart. Draw the 8:30 vertical, find the first swing low and high to the left, then look further left for relative equal highs and lows.
- **How to use:** Expect a raid on the liquidity on one side, then a run to the opposing pool.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 15m on 8 Feb 2022. Price traded below the first low to the left of 8:30, reversed, and took out the short-term high (relative equal highs further left). ICT took that trade and exited at that high instead of holding for the longer-term target.
- **Timestamps:** [0:11:24](https://youtu.be/G8-z91acgG4?t=684) — 8:30 crosshairs, first low, use the extreme · [0:12:14](https://youtu.be/G8-z91acgG4?t=734) — relative equal highs as "resistance" retail sees · [0:28:20](https://youtu.be/G8-z91acgG4?t=1700) — time first, then price

=== [2022 Ep7 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 7 (ID G8-z91acgG4)] — Targeting: range midpoint plus liquidity
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** He exited at the short-term high rather than holding for the longer-term one. The midpoint of the larger range (high down to the low) was just above that short-term high, and there was a small imbalance there. In this mentorship he looks for "about the midpoint and some kind of liquidity" (buy stops above it) as the objective.
- **How to use:** Take profit where the range midpoint lines up with resting liquidity. Don't hold for the extended target just to show off a perfect trade.
- **Timestamps:** [0:13:02](https://youtu.be/G8-z91acgG4?t=782) — why he didn't hold: midpoint plus imbalance plus buy stops
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He deliberately doesn't hold for big runs on the public account, so he doesn't present unrealistic results.

=== [2022 Ep7 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 7 (ID G8-z91acgG4)] — Turtle soup (ICT's version)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** A fake break below an old low that then rallies, or a fake break above an old high that then drops. ICT calls this turtle soup. It's his own simplified interpretation, not the pattern from Linda Raschke and Larry Connors' *Street Smarts* (which he recommends as a book). He looks for runs above old highs to set up shorts, and the mirror for longs.
- **Timestamps:** [0:25:09](https://youtu.be/G8-z91acgG4?t=1509)

=== [2022 Ep9 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 9 (ID iZLXnNiZm_s)] — Engineering liquidity (relative equal lows as stop pools)
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Back-and-forth AM price action builds "suspect" relative equal lows with sell stops below them. Anyone long overnight will "jam their stop loss right underneath" once price starts rising.
  - It's even better when the high above hasn't been taken yet.
  - Price runs up, consolidates, creates a low, starts rallying; retail moves stops under that low. That's the setup he wants, the liquidity he expects to be run.
  - After the overnight stops have been run, the market doesn't need to go back down there. It gets "sneakier" and won't give stopped-out traders another chance at the same price.
- **Timestamps:** [0:14:33](https://youtu.be/iZLXnNiZm_s?t=873) — relative equal lows, overnight longs' stops · [0:17:45](https://youtu.be/iZLXnNiZm_s?t=1065) — "that's engineering liquidity" · [0:19:21](https://youtu.be/iZLXnNiZm_s?t=1161) — post-lunch, they won't let stopped traders back in

- **Kind:** example only
- **What ICT says:** The relative equal lows below were sell-side liquidity, the low candle at 14,381.00 ("go look at your own chart"). That was the draw for the session's short.

=== [2022 Ep13 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 13 (ID tpPtItWqmlg)] — Buy-side liquidity (BSL) at relative equal highs / draw on liquidity
- **Kind:** rule / example
- **What ICT says:** Relative equal highs look like "resistance / a ceiling" to retail traders; buy stops rest above them. The algorithm, unwilling to let price go lower, goes after profitable shorts' stops. The ideal exit for longs is where willing buyers (buy stops of shorts) are — when price trades above the level, buy stops become market buy orders, flooding liquidity so longs can sell at a higher price. Don't exit 6 or 10 points higher arbitrarily.
- **How to identify:** Two highs at roughly the same level; mark the level just above them.
- **How to use:** That level is the profit objective of the trade.
- **Example in this video:** MNQ, ~14,110 above the relative equal highs (red level).
- **Timestamps:** [0:12:51](https://youtu.be/tpPtItWqmlg?t=771) — "just going to call it 14,110." [0:16:49](https://youtu.be/tpPtItWqmlg?t=1009) — retail sees ceiling; price should go over. [0:20:49](https://youtu.be/tpPtItWqmlg?t=1249) — why exits sit at buy stops.

=== [2022 Ep14 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 14 (ID NUdu1n-ML98)] — Buy-side liquidity vs retail "resistance"
- **Kind:** rule (applied)
- **What ICT says:** Buy-side liquidity sits around 14,160. Price will often consolidate there with a small retracement that gets people thinking it's time to short; retail sees 14,160 as resistance because price fell from there before. He expects it to "bust through the door" and run to 14,180.
- **Timestamps:** [0:02:22](https://youtu.be/NUdu1n-ML98?t=142).

=== [2022 Ep16 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 16 (ID EpGQnhjXBq8)] — PM session buy-side liquidity pool (Friday reversal / position squaring)  (new coinage: "PM session buy-side liquidity pool")
- **Kind:** new coinage / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - The pink-box zone above the **most energetic high of the morning session** — the high that launched price down to the daily target.
  - Because it's Friday, the daily objective has been hit, price is in a discount and has traded to the lower end of the week, expect a retracement back into the weekly range: position squaring / short covering by smart money that sold all morning. Shorts holding over the weekend put stops above that high → liquidity.
  - Retail sees relative equal lows broken ("support broken") and sells when price returns to them; smart money buys from those sellers — "engineering buy-side liquidity on the basis of sell-side flow."
  - Premium for the PM: take the morning high (the 8:30/9:30 rotation high — either works) down to the low; above 50% = premium; the PM draw is into that premium.
- **How to use:** Look for longs in the PM targeting the buy stops above the morning's energetic high.
- **Example in this video:** ES Friday PM: after tagging the daily OB, price repriced from discount to premium into the pink buy-side pool.
- **Timestamps:** [0:28:05](https://youtu.be/EpGQnhjXBq8?t=1685) — defines the pink rectangle. [0:28:52](https://youtu.be/EpGQnhjXBq8?t=1732) — Friday, weekly range retracement, weekend-hold stops. [0:29:42](https://youtu.be/EpGQnhjXBq8?t=1782) — 50% of the morning high-to-low = premium. [0:32:49](https://youtu.be/EpGQnhjXBq8?t=1969) — short covering; engineering BSL on sell-side flow.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He personally won't hold index positions over the weekend (gap risk).

=== [2022 Ep16 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 16 (ID EpGQnhjXBq8)] — Old high as support (old highs become a discount array)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** If price trades above an old high, that old high becomes a discount array — broken old highs can act as support ("not all the time"). What makes them real support/resistance is the narrative: when you know price is drawing up to buy stops, a retest of a broken old high that also fills an imbalance is a buy.
- **Example in this video:** ES PM: price traded back down, hit an old high to the left and filled an imbalance, then ran up to the pink BSL area.
- **Timestamps:** [0:35:09](https://youtu.be/EpGQnhjXBq8?t=2109), [0:35:58](https://youtu.be/EpGQnhjXBq8?t=2158) — "something for your notes."

=== [2022 Ep17 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 17 (ID 5WIqHJDQ_p4)] — Daily bias from liquidity (BSL taken → SSL next; "unfinished business")
- **Kind:** rule (applied)
- **What ICT says:** Once price runs above relative equal highs (buy stops taken) and turns lower, the next draw is the sell stops below. Two areas of SSL: relative equal lows ("two little devils") to the left, and a further low. Monday's candle low had not yet taken out the prior low → "unfinished business" below. After three consecutive down-close daily candles, the bias for the next candle is bearish.
- **Example in this video:** EURUSD daily: run above relative equal highs, three down-close days, Monday's low not yet below the relative equal lows; Tuesday expected lower.
- **Timestamps:** [0:01:03](https://youtu.be/5WIqHJDQ_p4?t=63) — run above relative equal highs. [0:01:51](https://youtu.be/5WIqHJDQ_p4?t=111) — relative equal lows; unfinished business; bearish bias. [0:03:28](https://youtu.be/5WIqHJDQ_p4?t=208) — why buy stops above highs feed smart-money shorts.

=== [2022 Ep17 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 17 (ID 5WIqHJDQ_p4)] — Buy-side liquidity pool (hourly) absorbed before the decline
- **Kind:** example
- **What ICT says:** On the hourly, price traded up into the buy-side liquidity pool level, "absorbing all of that liquidity," broke down taking several short-term lows, retraced, broke lower.
- **Timestamps:** [0:06:41](https://youtu.be/5WIqHJDQ_p4?t=401).

=== [2022 Ep17 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 17 (ID 5WIqHJDQ_p4)] — News drivers (ISM Services PMI) as volatility injection / stop raid
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** He doesn't care about the data, expectation, or what the number means — only that the algorithm uses the injection of volatility at or around that time to reprice and facilitate trades ("it's a rigged game" for those not in the know). High-impact news is the "wild card": it's designed to stop out traders trailing stops above recent swing highs, jamming price above the opening price so their buy stops become counterparty to smart-money shorts. You may avoid news if you don't know what you're doing, "but don't be afraid of them."
- **Example in this video:** EURUSD: 10:00 a.m. ET ISM Services PMI; price dropped after the 7:00-hour OB tap, then retraced up into the 10:00 release, back above the midnight open and into the bearish OB body, then fell.
- **Timestamps:** [0:12:15](https://youtu.be/5WIqHJDQ_p4?t=735) — ISM Services PMI at 10; volatility only. [0:16:13](https://youtu.be/5WIqHJDQ_p4?t=973) — news as stop-raid wild card. [0:24:54](https://youtu.be/5WIqHJDQ_p4?t=1494) — don't be afraid of news.

=== [2022 Ep18 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 18 (ID eai0nHhAC8w)] — Previous day's high & low as intraday framework (old highs/lows, last 3 days)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** The daily draw on liquidity (the old low) is not your intraday trade target — it may not happen in one day. Instead frame each day with the previous day's high (premium level) and low (target). There's lots of liquidity around daily highs and lows; "institutional mindset traders" use them and high-frequency algorithms attack them. His first lesson on BabyPips (2009–2010): mark the previous day's highs and lows for the last 3 days — "you're never going to run out of trades."
- **How to use:** Bearish day: expect the open to rally just above the previous day's high (Judas swing) back into the FVG, then sell targeting the previous day's low.
- **Example in this video:** EURUSD: Wednesday's high marked red (premium), Wednesday's low marked as target. Thursday opened, rallied, popped just above the short-term high into the daily FVG, then sold off toward Wednesday's low. Range of opportunity roughly 60 pips (he reads it as from ~…935 to ~…874 — figures caption unclear).
- **Timestamps:** [0:16:45](https://youtu.be/eai0nHhAC8w?t=1005) — targets; mark the FVG-day low. [0:17:32](https://youtu.be/eai0nHhAC8w?t=1052) — daily DOL isn't the intraday target. [0:18:20](https://youtu.be/eai0nHhAC8w?t=1100) — Thursday Judas into FVG; HFT attacks previous day highs/lows. [0:19:08](https://youtu.be/eai0nHhAC8w?t=1148) — last 3 days' highs/lows.

=== [2022 Ep18 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 18 (ID eai0nHhAC8w)] — Relative equal highs (hourly) + midnight open — higher first, then lower
- **Kind:** rule (applied)
- **What ICT says:** On the hourly, the daily FVG also shows, with relative equal highs below/at it (buy-side liquidity pool). On a bearish day, the market wants to "go higher first to go lower": after the midnight open, rally above the relative equal highs into the FVG, then displacement down breaking a swing low and creating an FVG.
- **Example in this video:** EURUSD: 6 April low mapped; new day 7 April at midnight NY. At ~5:25 a.m. NY (London) price swept the previous day's (6 April) low first; he ignored London because it didn't give the rally into his level. The rally into the premium level/daily FVG came at **9:30 a.m. NY**, above the relative equal highs, then displacement lower.
- **Timestamps:** [0:24:44](https://youtu.be/eai0nHhAC8w?t=1484) — hourly relative equal highs. [0:30:20](https://youtu.be/eai0nHhAC8w?t=1820) — rallied above them; displacement; swing low broken; FVG. [0:45:21](https://youtu.be/eai0nHhAC8w?t=2721) — 5:25 a.m. sweep of 6 April low; rally at 9:30.

=== [2022 Ep19 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 19 (ID IEa1N0rTtbc)] — Narrative after a liquidity run (sell-side taken → seek buy-side)  (aliases: draw on liquidity, short-term oversold)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** Once an old daily low is taken, the market is "short-term oversold on the daily"; sell stops/sell-side liquidity have been removed, so the market now looks for buy-side liquidity. An energetic displacement off that low that leaves a FVG is the algorithmic tell; a trade back down into the gap is a buy even though newer traders fear "it'll go below these equal lows."
- **How to use:** Buy the retrace into the displacement FVG; target the short-term high / above the bodies / 62% retracement.
- **Timestamps:** [0:53:15](https://youtu.be/IEa1N0rTtbc?t=3195) — EURUSD 15m clean FVG off old daily low; [0:54:02](https://youtu.be/IEa1N0rTtbc?t=3242) — narrative: SSL taken, now BSL.

=== [2022 Ep19 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 19 (ID IEa1N0rTtbc)] — Relative equal highs/lows as engineered liquidity  (aliases: relative equal highs, REH, engineered buy stops, "too clean" support/resistance)
- **Kind:** rule / example
- **What ICT says:** Two level highs = relative equal highs; they "engineer" buy stops because retail sees double-top resistance, shorts there with stops above, and treats the sideways drift as a bear flag (projecting a measured move lower). When it "looks too perfect, too pristine, too classic support/resistance," expect it to be run. Old highs/lows and relative equal highs/lows are the easiest, most visual targeting method; that logic will never change because stops always rest above old highs and below old lows.
- **Example in this video:** ES hourly/15m: two level highs above the consolidation = BSL; this became the day's target.
- **Timestamps:** [1:26:33](https://youtu.be/IEa1N0rTtbc?t=5193) — relative equal highs engineer buy stops; [1:27:20](https://youtu.be/IEa1N0rTtbc?t=5240) — retail sees resistance / bear flag / measured move; [1:48:49](https://youtu.be/IEa1N0rTtbc?t=6529) — Monday clip: "too perfect, too pristine"; [1:49:37](https://youtu.be/IEa1N0rTtbc?t=6577) — old highs/lows + REH/REL = easiest targeting.

Up-close candles in that decline were just opportunities to short. This "reaching for that low" is what he'll do in the live streams on intraday charts.

=== [2022 Ep21 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 21 (ID kmVXVJE08eQ)] — Draw on liquidity — daily relative equal lows ("heaviness objective")  (aliases: DOL, relative equal lows, sell-side)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** With the daily draw being relative equal lows below, each daily candle should expand lower toward them. Prices keep going lower because they gravitate toward those lows; "the algorithm is marching every minute, every hour, every day to that level." He doesn't know how it will trade once there (violent acceleration vs sharp retracement) and doesn't care yet. While price is above those daily REL, any imbalance is a reason to get short, not long.
- **Example in this video:** ES: rallied slightly above Monday's high, failed, accelerated lower, falling just short of the REL.
- **Timestamps:** [0:08:52](https://youtu.be/kmVXVJE08eQ?t=532) — each daily candle should expand lower; rally above Monday's high then failure; [0:27:59](https://youtu.be/kmVXVJE08eQ?t=1679) — imbalances above daily REL = shorts, algorithm marching to the level.

=== [2022 Ep22 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 22 (ID QKc_i90chVg)] — Premium/discount relative to old highs/lows  (aliases: short-term premium, short-term discount)
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:** Any time the market trades *above an old high* it is in a short-term premium (it's going into liquidity); any time it trades *below an old low* it's in a discount. Trading above the 8:30 opening price also puts it in a premium on a bearish day. Like overbought/oversold, premium can still go higher and discount can keep going lower — so it must be read through the narrative.
- **Timestamps:** [0:10:25](https://youtu.be/QKc_i90chVg?t=625) — 8:30 open traded above = premium, above old high = premium; [0:11:16](https://youtu.be/QKc_i90chVg?t=676) — definition, viewer question answered.

=== [2022 Ep23 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 23 (ID TV9TfwHXuqs)] — "Running" vs "Sweeping" liquidity  (aliases: run on liquidity, sweep, expansion vs reversal)
- **Kind:** definition (vocabulary)
- **What ICT says:** He picks these words deliberately. *Sweeping* a high (or low) = price goes just a little above (below) it — a shallow run — and reverses back into the range. *Running* a high (old high or relative equal highs) = price goes right over the top of it and continues without looking back (expansion).
- **Example in this video:** Before/at FOMC price made a shallow move above Tuesday's high (red line) = a sweep, then came down. After the drop into the blue zone, price ran Tuesday's high "much more meaningfully" — straight through and higher.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:03](https://youtu.be/TV9TfwHXuqs?t=243) — "running the Tuesday high, not sweeping"; [0:04:50](https://youtu.be/TV9TfwHXuqs?t=290) — sweep = shallow run and reverse, run = right over top and doesn't look back; [0:05:38](https://youtu.be/TV9TfwHXuqs?t=338) — sweep and reverse into range vs expansion move.

=== [2022 Ep24 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 24 (ID nv0ey5UyOmY)] — 2022 Mentorship model — where setups form (bearish version via relative equal highs)
- **Kind:** entry model / rule
- **What ICT says:** Setup forms after price runs above an old high or relative equal highs. As soon as price trades above the relative equal highs, scan back through the run-up and find the **nearest short-term low** — that is the trigger. It is not enough that price goes above the highs and simply drifts back down below them; price must go below that short-term low **with displacement** ("energetic move"). That displacement leg is "the foundation of the setup." Only then go into that leg and look for a fair value gap to short into.
- **How to identify:** (1) relative equal highs / old high level; (2) price runs through it; (3) identify the nearest short-term low formed during the run-up; (4) displacement takes out that short-term low = market structure shift; (5) FVG inside the displacement leg.
- **How to use:** Sell when price retraces up into the FVG in the displacement leg. Until the short-term low is taken out, "there's no trade there, there's no setup there." Reverse everything for longs (run below relative equal lows/old low, displacement above nearest short-term high, buy the FVG).
- **Example in this video:** Diagram drawn: green line = relative equal highs; price approaches, small correction, approaches again, then runs through; trigger = nearest short-term low in that run; displacement through it; FVG in the leg.
- **Timestamps:** [0:31:41](https://youtu.be/nv0ey5UyOmY?t=1901) — "where do setups form" journal subtitle; [0:33:16](https://youtu.be/nv0ey5UyOmY?t=1996) — nearest short-term low is the trigger; [0:34:03](https://youtu.be/nv0ey5UyOmY?t=2043) — no displacement through the low = no setup.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He calls this the filter that removes low-probability trades — the displacement requirement is what separates a real shift from a simple dip back below the highs.

=== [2022 Ep24 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 24 (ID nv0ey5UyOmY)] — Relative equal highs ("smooth criminal highs") as draw / setup location
- **Kind:** example only
- **What ICT says:** Community post told viewers to drop a horizontal line across the "smooth criminal highs" (relative equal highs) on NQ and ES 15m charts and study how they're treated ahead of NFP Friday. Bearish framework on NQ 1h: look for a run above those relative equal highs, then a washout lower that creates the model.
- **How to identify:** Relative equal highs on 15m/1h index charts.
- **How to use:** As a liquidity target to be run before a reversal (setup location).
- **Example in this video:** E-mini NQ June 2022 contract, daily and 1h chart; he felt price could stab above the relative equal highs and then run lower. Price never ran them; both scenarios (drop into FVG then run the highs; run the highs then reverse) failed to develop.

=== [2022 Ep25 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 25 (ID CnTXwAuDi9Y)] — Smart money liquidity cycle (sell to buy stops, buy back from sell stops)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Smart money sells short to buy stops resting above old/relative equal highs, leaving them net short; to get out they must buy back cheaper, from sell stops resting below old lows (sell-side liquidity). Large fund traders place stops in similar places to retail — the difference is only size. This is the "market efficiency paradigm": liquidity resting or engineered where it's wanted — not support/resistance, not supply/demand. The algorithm goes to the liquidity "whether there is sufficient volume or not" — not because of buying pressure.
- **Timestamps:** [0:14:19](https://youtu.be/CnTXwAuDi9Y?t=859) — net short, buy back from sell stops; [0:15:06](https://youtu.be/CnTXwAuDi9Y?t=906) — funds and retail stops in same areas; [0:49:58](https://youtu.be/CnTXwAuDi9Y?t=2998) — algorithm goes there regardless of volume.

=== [2022 Ep25 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 25 (ID CnTXwAuDi9Y)] — Order of liquidity runs defines bias (buy-side first = bearish)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** With a bearish bias, the market goes up to a premium for one of two reasons: run old high(s) for buy-side liquidity so smart money can sell to them, then seek sell-side liquidity below to offset. Running buy-side (relative equal highs) at 9:30 *without* first taking the previous low is bearish. If price had first taken the previous low and then rallied, "that to me is not bearish."
- **Example in this video:** ES 1h/15m: relative equal highs above, old low (Friday's low) above that; at 9:30 price runs the relative equal highs and hits the old low (red line), purging buy-side; then sells to take partials below a short-term low and run Monday's low.
- **Timestamps:** [0:27:01](https://youtu.be/CnTXwAuDi9Y?t=1621) — BSL purged at 9:30; [0:28:38](https://youtu.be/CnTXwAuDi9Y?t=1718) — buy side run first is the bearish tell.

=== [2022 Ep26 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 26 - Example Of Tape Reading Practice (ID k26euOG6zAk)] — Intraday order flow against the daily bias (after sell-side is taken)
- **Kind:** rule / example
- **What ICT says:** He is trading against the daily bias on the basis that sell-side liquidity has already been taken and price has traded into a fair value gap (~4000). Price traded down three times intraday and took out yesterday's low; the lack of follow-through bolsters a counter-trend long. Retail sees the consolidation as a bear flag and expects lower prices.
- **How to identify:** Relative equal lows / previous day low swept; price reached into a discount FVG; repeated failure to continue lower.
- **How to use:** Target the nearest buy-side liquidity (short-term high), not a full reversal. The best case (high of day) is optional — "it doesn't need to do that to be profitable."
- **Example in this video:** Likely ES (levels ~4000; instrument not named in captions), date not stated. Sell-side below relative equal lows run; entry long around the FVG near 4000 (fill read as ~4006.25/4006.50, caption unclear); draw = buy-side above a short-term high (≈4036 area); stretch = 4051.25 (high of day, buy stops).
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:05](https://youtu.be/k26euOG6zAk?t=5) — against daily bias, sell stops taken, FVG near 4000; [0:01:44](https://youtu.be/k26euOG6zAk?t=104) — SSL taken, 4051 stretch; [0:04:06](https://youtu.be/k26euOG6zAk?t=246) — three drives down, yesterday's low taken, lack of follow-through.

=== [2022 Ep27 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 27 - Counter Trend Ideas (ID gLK4Qm6jte8)] — Draw on liquidity doesn't need to be reached
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** If you believe price will draw toward an objective (today or into next week), look for a setup that lets you participate in the expansion toward it; the DOL "does not need to be traded to" to have a profitable framework. Think of it as a magnet.

With the large sell-side pool taken and price back above the relative equal lows, the clear draw is buy-side above the relative equal highs. Little imbalance remained in between because that area was already rebalanced, so he expected price to "punch through" and take the buy stops.

=== [2022 Ep27 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 27 - Counter Trend Ideas (ID gLK4Qm6jte8)] — Lunch high and the 1:30 PM afternoon liquidity search
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** The high formed at the 12:10 candle — lunchtime "usually creates a retracement of some sort or consolidation." Around 1:30 PM the algorithm starts seeking liquidity again; if it's going to continue higher, it first seeks sell-side. Relative equal lows existed, but they weren't in a discount yet — the afternoon retracement must reach equilibrium of the day's range (low of day to high of day; purple dashed line = Fib 50%) or below.
- **How to use:** On a bullish day, wait for the afternoon retracement to drop to/below the day's equilibrium into an FVG, then buy for continuation.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 5m: price dropped through equilibrium into an FVG at ~2:00 PM (2:05–2:25 candles), then displaced higher — an MSS within a likely bullish day.
- **Timestamps:** [0:05:49](https://youtu.be/gLK4Qm6jte8?t=349) — buy discount FVG at/below equilibrium; 12:10 lunch high; [0:06:37](https://youtu.be/gLK4Qm6jte8?t=397) — 1:30 liquidity search, equilibrium; [0:07:24](https://youtu.be/gLK4Qm6jte8?t=444) — drop into FVG at 2 PM, displacement.

=== [2022 Ep31 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 31 (ID OYNpIeu9czw)] — Pointing to liquidity (scout / sniper framing)

When price is above and he draws attention to an old low (the May 16 low), he means it's the target for sell-side liquidity — "we're aiming for liquidity." He's the scout; students are snipers who must do the scoping (entry, stop, limit orders), which he has already taught.

=== [2022 Ep32 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 32 (ID hzoMJQlEv5M)] — Outside day (down close) → choppy next day if old low isn't reached
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:** An outside day trades above the previous day's high and below the previous day's low (the whole prior range is inside it); here it closed down. The following day, if price trades down toward an old low but falls short of it (the "double bottom" that feels safe), it tends to be choppy — especially after a down-close outside day.
- **Example in this video:** Old daily low at 3855; day's low 3856 (one handle short), day low area 3872.25; old highs at 3933.25. Price then chopped.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:54](https://youtu.be/hzoMJQlEv5M?t=54) — outside day defined; [0:01:40](https://youtu.be/hzoMJQlEv5M?t=100) — falling short of old low → choppy; [0:03:17](https://youtu.be/hzoMJQlEv5M?t=197) — levels 3872.25, 3855, 3856.

=== [2022 Ep32 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 32 (ID hzoMJQlEv5M)] — Protective buy stops as a "rushing liquidity wave"
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Buy orders resting above relative equal highs are short sellers' protective stops. When price trades above, those stops become "a rushing liquidity wave" of willing buyers at the market — which smart money sells into. The run also induces breakout longs, building sell-side liquidity below (their stops) for later.
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:26](https://youtu.be/hzoMJQlEv5M?t=446) — run to squeeze shorts and induce longs; [0:16:20](https://youtu.be/hzoMJQlEv5M?t=980) — rushing liquidity wave.

=== [2022 Ep36 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 36 (ID pWnMpdN_g98)] — Sell-side clearing with no model entry = "tipping its hand"
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** Before 9:30 price ran up into a premium; then it sank rapidly around the 9:30 opening, taking relative equal lows (sell-side), bounced briefly, then took another low — knocking out longs who tried to pick the bottom. There was no model short entry on that drop: "that's telling, it's tipping its hand" — it was a rush to clear sell-side liquidity, not a genuine bearish move. Retail rarely re-enters after being stopped like that.
- **How to identify:** Fast drop through obvious lows without a displacement/FVG setup that your model would short.
- **How to use:** Treat it as a liquidity sweep in a bullish context; look for the subsequent MSS + FVG long.
- **Timestamps:** [0:01:47](https://youtu.be/pWnMpdN_g98?t=107) — run to premium then slide through relative equal lows; [0:05:42](https://youtu.be/pWnMpdN_g98?t=342) — no model entry, tipping its hand.

=== [2022 Ep37 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 37 (ID HuZurY0ghDI)] — PM session: lunch low stop run then old high taken
- **Kind:** example only

=== [2022 Ep38 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 38 (ID 36184dDAqtM)] — Narrative / Draw on liquidity  (aliases: where the market is likely to go next)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:**
  - Narrative is knowing what price should do, why, and what it will run into along the way that proves the assumed narrative is actually underway.
  - He says he is not teaching entry patterns where a precise entry price is what matters most. He is teaching how to work out where the market is likely to go next (the DOL).
  - The DOL is set algorithmically, not by "buying/selling pressure". Read price and the logic behind it instead of basing trades on indicators (overbought/oversold, line crossovers).
  - Once you can read price, entries are "the easiest thing to do".
- **How to identify:** First mark the higher-timeframe objective (here the daily FVG, prior-day high, relative equal highs/lows). Then watch for events at key times that confirm or reject it.
- **How to use:** The narrative alone is not an entry. Even after he believed price would run to the prior-day high, he says buying right away could have stopped him out by being early. He waited for displacement, measured premium/discount, and only bought in a discount.
- **Example in this video:** ES 5m. Price traded down into the 15m FVG and rallied from it. That told him the algorithm was "priming itself" for an afternoon run toward the previous day's high (4168.25). At 10:35 ET he tweeted "note previous day's high".
- **Timestamps:** [0:06:18](https://youtu.be/36184dDAqtM?t=378) — what he teaches: where price goes, not entry patterns; [0:07:54](https://youtu.be/36184dDAqtM?t=474) — definition of narrative; [0:28:22](https://youtu.be/36184dDAqtM?t=1702) — 15m FVG retrace leads to the afternoon narrative; [0:29:09](https://youtu.be/36184dDAqtM?t=1749) — narrative alone is not a buy signal.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He stresses he does not need to be right. Being wrong about 4070 cost nothing because there was no setup and no trade.

=== [2022 Ep39 — 2022 ICT Mentorship Episode 39 (ID yFpHbBnsK_c)] — Conditional narrative, not "always sweep equal lows and reverse"
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - The forecast was conditional. If price went below the daily relative equal lows, it would not "go down to go up". It would keep drawing into the daily FVG below.
  - That implies the volatility would be large enough for a large-range day.
  - Others teaching his concepts (or their own twists on them) claim price always hits relative equal lows and then reverses. "That's not the case." Narrative must be understood, and narrative requires experience, which he is lending.
- **How to use:** When he names an objective (4070), study what sits at that level yourself (old lows, relative equal lows, FVG). He will not spoon-feed it.
- **Example in this video:** ES. Pre-market (before 9:30) DOL was 4070, below the daily relative equal lows. Beyond that, the target was the daily FVG. Price ran through 4070, then to about 4040 (caption says "440"), and closed inside the larger daily FVG.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:58](https://youtu.be/yFpHbBnsK_c?t=58) — 4070 DOL below relative equal lows; [0:02:35](https://youtu.be/yFpHbBnsK_c?t=155) — below the lows means draw into the FVG, large range day; [0:05:45](https://youtu.be/yFpHbBnsK_c?t=345) — "not always equal lows then reverse".

=== [2022 Ep41 — 2022 ICT Mentorship - Episode 41 & Final (ID 2XhDi5GoNUI)] — NY lunch: don't initiate into lunch; prefer the lunch stop run; wait for 1:30
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Once price broke lower going into lunch, he did not want to short, because it might consolidate and "take forever". He does not hold anything new into lunch.
  - He prefers to see a stop run during the lunch hour. Here price ran below the lows into an FVG in a deep discount (of the low–high range) during lunch, and made the session low at about 1:00 when lunch ended.
  - The NY index PM session is 1:30–4:00 PM NY. He likes to wait until 1:30 because the price action is cleaner.
- **Timestamps:** [0:06:23](https://youtu.be/2XhDi5GoNUI?t=383) — 1:30–4:00 PM session; [0:07:10](https://youtu.be/2XhDi5GoNUI?t=430) — no new trades into lunch; prefer the lunch stop run; [0:07:59](https://youtu.be/2XhDi5GoNUI?t=479) — wait until 1:30, cleaner price action.


######## 2024 MENTORSHIP ########

=== [2024 #1 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #1  August 5, 2024 (ID GKeLVR3dPuI)] — Daily bias from smooth relative equal highs/lows (the "secret weapon")  (aliases: smooth vs. jagged, "where it's smooth it's going", contrasting one side of the market against the other)
- **Kind:** entry model / rule (beginner bias framework)
- **What ICT says:**
  - Sit down at a key window: **7:00–7:30, or 8:00–8:30, or 9:00–9:30 ET**. That gives you three opportunities every morning. You don't need the afternoon or London.
  - Look to the left of the window on the **15m, 5m and 1m** charts for **relative equal highs or relative equal lows**. Whichever side has them is your bias, because price will draw to it.
  - **If there are relative equal highs above AND relative equal lows below:** wait for the first one to be disrupted, then work toward the other one. Defer and wait.
  - Each window that passes without the run makes it more likely, not less. Example: the relative equal high formed at 7:00, price didn't go there in 8:00–8:30, so 9:00–9:30 "chances are that's the run".
  - "Key takeaway": the framework starts at 7:00, so look for the relative equal highs to form from 7:00 on, "not before". Relative equal highs/lows from before 7:00 (the London session) may work, but expecting London levels to be run is often wrong, so starting at 7:00 acts as a filter.
  - He calls this a secret weapon he had never given out, even to his private mentorship: "a flamethrower" that "makes every playing field even". The market can't hide it.
- **How to identify:** Smooth means two swing points close together that make a flat, obvious level. Jagged means choppy swings where stops have already been run ("the work has been done").
- **How to use:** Trade toward the smooth side. Use PD arrays (breakers, FVGs, inversion FVGs) as stepping stones toward it. A level or inefficiency visible on all three timeframes is "three timeframes telling you pay attention to me". You don't need to trade every day. Trade when it's obvious, and accept that sometimes you'll miss the move.
- **Example in this video:** NQ, Mon Aug 5 2024. After a multi-thousand-handle selloff there was a big NWOG overhead and the market had dropped early, around 7:00. On the 5m, relative equal highs (each high slightly above the next one to its right) put liquidity at **17,820.75**, and the first line he drew was there. Below, the lows were jagged, so they had already been raided. Price formed a bullish breaker, made a 9:30 Judas-swing drop into it, then ran up through intermediate FVGs and took the 17,820.75 highs.
- **Timestamps:** [1:43:12](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=6192) — 7:00–7:30 window, relative equal highs/lows formed prior · [1:47:59](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=6479) — "secret weapon": contrast one side of the market against the other · [1:53:31](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=6811) — whichever side has equal highs/lows is the bias; if both, wait for the first to break · [2:05:29](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=7529) — three windows, likelihood builds; start at 7:00, not before
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:**
  - A higher-timeframe draw is "not necessary". He used only 15/5/1 and time.
  - The level does not have to be hit in that session. That is the risk you take on every trade.
  - Manual intervention (news, geopolitics) can always break it.
  - The wording on the 7:00 window is ambiguous. At 1:43 he says look for relative equal highs/lows "that have happened prior to 7 o'clock". At 2:05 he says start looking for them to form at 7:00, "not before". Most likely the AM framework begins at 7:00 and pre-7:00 (London) levels are the weaker filter (partly caption unclear).
  - He admits relative equal highs/lows aren't new teaching, but says students haven't actually traded this way.

=== [2024 #1 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #1  August 5, 2024 (ID GKeLVR3dPuI)] — Smooth vs. jagged price (shark / still-water analogy)  (aliases: "still waters invite rocks", "shark with no teeth")
- **Kind:** new coinage (principle)
- **What ICT says:**
  - Smooth relative equal levels are like a shark with no teeth, or still water: they invite disruption.
  - Jagged price is where a shark attack has already happened. You can see the wounds, and the "cannibals" have already eaten. Don't trade into a level where the frenzy has already taken place.
  - The market goes to areas of smoothness to disrupt the orders resting above or below them. This holds on every timeframe, from sub-minute charts up to quarterly.
  - His closing rule: "Where it's smooth it's going; where it's jagged it's going to move away from."
- **How to identify:** Compare the two sides of the current range. Flat, clean highs or lows are smooth. Choppy swings with stop runs are jagged.
- **How to use:** Your target side is the smooth side. A jagged run that then rips the other way tells you buy-side liquidity is next (shorts trapped on a breakout).
- **Example in this video:** NQ: the lows had been disrupted and were jagged after the 7:00 drop and the 9:30 Judas swing. The highs at 17,820.75 were smooth, and price went to them.
- **Timestamps:** [1:09:15](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=4155) — the two-sharks analogy · [1:10:05](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=4205) — the market goes to smoothness on any timeframe · [1:54:18](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=6858) — jagged then ripped higher means look for buy stops · [2:14:17](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=8057) — closing rule
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He ties the jaggedness to "fingerprints" left by consistent winners, using the World Series of Poker final-table players as the comparison.

=== [2024 #1 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #1  August 5, 2024 (ID GKeLVR3dPuI)] — Draw on liquidity (DOL)  (aliases: draw, "low-hanging fruit")
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** The draw on liquidity is just an initial reasonable assumption about a directional move that could unfold. It is not the be-all, end-all target or the end point of the run. Price can retrace or even make a lower low first, though he says that is highly unlikely. The New Week Opening Gap (Friday settlement to the Sunday 6:00 pm ET open) is a draw on liquidity, but price doesn't need to reach it. For a short-term bias, use the low-hanging-fruit objective, such as nearby relative equal highs.
- **How to use:** It gives you something to focus on, and a way to judge each candle's behavior as it forms against your expectation.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Aug 5: a huge NWOG overhead after a multi-thousand-handle decline. The short-term draw was the relative equal highs instead.
- **Timestamps:** [0:48:19](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=2899) — relative equal highs and NWOG as draws · [0:49:08](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=2948) — low-hanging fruit for short-term bias · [1:06:50](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=4010) — DOL is not the terminus

=== [2024 #1 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #1  August 5, 2024 (ID GKeLVR3dPuI)] — Equilibrium measured from the relative equal lows, not the lowest low
- **Kind:** refinement
- **What ICT says:** For the midpoint of the expected leg, draw a fib from slightly above the relative equal highs (the target; no special logic to the exact price) down to the relative equal lows, not the lowest low. The lowest low is the manipulation. The FVG near that equilibrium is the entry area. An FVG slightly above equilibrium is still usable because the relative equal highs haven't been taken yet.
- **Example in this video:** NQ: the fib from just above the 17,820.75 highs to the relative equal lows put the midpoint close to an FVG. He said "watch this fair value gap" in real time, and price took off to the highs.
- **Timestamps:** [1:59:55](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=7195) — the midpoint between where price came from and where it's going · [2:00:43](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=7243) — use the relative equal lows, not the lowest low · [2:01:30](https://youtu.be/GKeLVR3dPuI?t=7290) — the called FVG launches the run

=== [2024 #2 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #2   August 6, 2024 (ID pkI4AgC4OPI)] — Hourly hunt for relative equal highs/lows (7:00, 8:00, 9:00)  (aliases: three windows of opportunity)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - At 7:00, scan the 15m, then the 5m, then the 1m for relative equal highs or relative equal lows that form **after** 7:00.
  - At 8:00 and again at 9:00 you start the whole process over. At 8:00 you may look back before 8:00, but never before 7:00, because you're working inside the morning-session range. Same at 9:00: anything back to 7:00 is fair.
  - Morning session = 7:00–11:00 (technically to noon). ICT likes to be done by 10:15–10:30.
  - **Never annotate or anticipate a pool that hasn't formed.** "Don't predict the setup … wait for the setup." Only take a mental note of a *potential* pair. ICT can anticipate (for example, calling iFVGs before they form), but beginners can't.
  - If relative equal highs and lows are both present (a consolidation), wait for one side to be taken. It usually then attacks the other side.
  - If no obvious pair exists on any of the three timeframes, move to annotating inefficiencies (see the FVG sections).
  - The relative equal high/low is this model's draw on liquidity. Pick the target, then re-measure each hour.
- **How to use:** Get a short-term bias by watching how price attacks or is attracted to a relative equal low or high. Once the bias is set, "stick with it even if it turns against you" during observation.
- **Example in this video:** Aug 6, 2024 (index futures; the instrument isn't named, but he measures in "handles"). On the 15m at 7:00 there's only a single high, so no relative equal highs yet. The two lows visible are before 7:00, so they're ignored. On the 5m, a potential relative equal low pair exists *if* price rallies, but only as a mental note. On the 1m, relative equal highs formed after 7:00 and were then run.
- **Timestamps:** [0:54:24](https://youtu.be/pkI4AgC4OPI?t=3264) 15m scan at 7:00. [0:55:13](https://youtu.be/pkI4AgC4OPI?t=3313) "never lead your expectations with something that has not formed". [1:38:39](https://youtu.be/pkI4AgC4OPI?t=5919) restart at 8:00/9:00, morning session 7–11. [1:56:38](https://youtu.be/pkI4AgC4OPI?t=6998) relative equal highs/lows are the target for this model.

=== [2024 #2 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #2   August 6, 2024 (ID pkI4AgC4OPI)] — High-probability relative equal high
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** A relative equal high is high probability when the right-side high is **slightly lower** than the left one: the market failed to get back to the first high. The algorithm will reprice to it because it "wants to disrupt" that pool. By symmetry, the right-side low would be slightly higher for lows. He only states the highs case explicitly.
- **How to identify:** Two highs at roughly the same level, where the second is a bit lower. It may only look equal when the chart is compressed.
- **Timestamps:** [1:12:51](https://youtu.be/pkI4AgC4OPI?t=4371) "did I teach you yesterday…" (the right one slightly lower). [2:54:59](https://youtu.be/pkI4AgC4OPI?t=10499) restated on the afternoon run to the day's high.

=== [2024 #2 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #2   August 6, 2024 (ID pkI4AgC4OPI)] — Smooth vs. jagged (liquidity engagement)
- **Kind:** definition (continued from Lecture #1)
- **What ICT says:** Price delivery has two simple characteristics. When price takes an area that was smooth (resting equal highs or lows) and makes it **jagged**, that was an engagement for liquidity. Expect the opposite direction to follow, especially when other factors (breaker, iFVG) support it. After a run of buy-side liquidity, go back through that price leg for setups.
- **How to identify:** Zoom out or compress the chart. A Larry Williams tip: if you're losing touch, zoom out, go up a timeframe, or look from across the room. Jaggedness that's invisible zoomed in becomes obvious.
- **Example:** Aug 6: relative equal highs after 7:00 were run ("sharp frenzy … blood in the water up here"), so don't trade up into that area and look lower. Later, the lows were made jagged, so look for buy side, a run on the high (the 7:00 high).
- **Timestamps:** [0:58:25](https://youtu.be/pkI4AgC4OPI?t=3505) smooth made jagged = engagement for liquidity. [1:00:50](https://youtu.be/pkI4AgC4OPI?t=3650) "blood in the water". [2:01:25](https://youtu.be/pkI4AgC4OPI?t=7285) zoom out and jagged lows (Larry Williams). [2:02:12](https://youtu.be/pkI4AgC4OPI?t=7332) look for buy side next.

=== [2024 #3 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #3   August 7, 2024 (ID ZkLc4-adodg)] — Engineering liquidity (predicting REH/REL before they form)  (aliases: engineered liquidity, design of liquidity)
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - Midnight NY is the start of the "true day". The algorithm refers back to midnight and everything after it for liquidity and inefficiencies, and beyond that to NDOG/NWOG.
  - Starting at **7:00 AM** (then 8:00, 9:00), look for relative equal highs/lows or "smooth" price that forms after the session changeover. In the **30 minutes after each top of hour**, anticipate a move in the opposing direction (this continues from Lecture 2).
  - Because a known NDOG/NWOG draw gives you a "magnetized reference point", you can predict **where relative equal highs/lows will be built before they form**. That, he says, is exactly what engineering liquidity means.
  - Example rule: with an NDOG below and REH forming after 7:00, **never short right away** on the first drop. Let price run those highs first (a **Judas swing**), and don't chase.
- **How to identify / REH-REL quality filter:** Two swings close together, and the right-hand swing must sit inside the left one. For relative equal **lows**, the right low has to be **higher** than the previous low. Then it is a high-probability REL to draw to. The NDOG below acts as "rocket fuel" on top of that.
- **Example in this video:** NQ, Aug 7 2024. In the 7:00 and 8:00 hours, price built REH and REL. The NDOG sat far south, so he expected the highs to run first. Price dropped, and retail chased it short. After 9:00, it rallied and pumped above the highs (one, two, three relative equal highs), made another REH, then broke down. He calls that a Judas swing into 9:00 that chops traders up all morning.
- **Timestamps:** [0:14:57](https://youtu.be/ZkLc4-adodg?t=897) NDOG as underlying narrative; 7/8/9 intervals · [0:50:57](https://youtu.be/ZkLc4-adodg?t=3057) predict REH/REL before they form · [0:52:31](https://youtu.be/ZkLc4-adodg?t=3151) don't chase; Judas swing · [1:28:40](https://youtu.be/ZkLc4-adodg?t=5320) REL right-side rule
- **Nuance:** Once the real draw is lower, the REH above can simply be left alone ("they can stay in play"). He doesn't need them taken before shorting the afternoon.

=== [2024 #4 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #4    August 8, 2024 (ID hdSscxZgE58)] — 8:30 spooling / algorithmic repricing  (aliases: spooling, the market spools, buy program / sell program)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:**
  - At 8:30 ET the algorithm starts "spooling" (running) regardless of news; he never looks at the actual report numbers (CPI, PMI, NFP) — "it's all baked in."
  - Medium/high-impact news "loosens it up" so price can reach further outside the normal band of volatility; the magnitude and speed are bigger. No news → price still moves toward the same levels, just less energetically.
  - Spooling analogy: like fishing line shooting off a reel when cast. When the algorithm enters a buy program it spools higher; a sell program, lower.
  - The big 8:30 run is repricing, not buying pressure — check time & sales / volume: few contracts traded during the run. Historical analogy: open-outcry floor traders "pairing orders" (one contract needed to book a price) to stair-step price.
  - Other scheduled times where price spools: 8:30, 10:00, sometimes 9:45, 10:30 (crude oil inventories), plus macros.
- **How to identify:** Economic calendar (Forex Factory is cleaner; he prefers Econoday — more information, free). The calendar is your "TV guide" for when to watch.
- **How to use:** The 8:30 initial run is essentially untradeable unless already positioned before it; the draw is determined by where price is relative to the gap clusters right before the release.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 15-second chart at 8:30: from 17,986.75 to roughly 18,08x (caption unclear) — "100 handles in just like 15 seconds". On the 1-second chart, the first one-second candle went from 17,986.75 to ~18,037.75 (caption unclear).
- **Timestamps:** [0:09:16](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=556) — algorithm spools at 8:30 even without news; [0:36:13](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=2173) — 15-sec chart ~100 handles in 15 seconds; [1:19:56](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=4796) — fishing reel analogy, buy/sell programs; [1:56:04](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=6964) — 1-second chart of the 8:30 candle.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Warns funded-account traders using 5–15 contracts on a ~$3,000 max loss that gambling these runs guarantees blowing out.

=== [2024 #4 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #4    August 8, 2024 (ID hdSscxZgE58)] — Support & resistance critique / old highs as targets  (aliases: liquidity above old highs)
- **Kind:** rule / mindset
- **What ICT says:** Classical S/R (John Murphy etc.) shows what retail will try to do, which is useful only because it tells you where their stops are. If an old high is resistance, why does price go back to it? To go above it, because shorts' stops are there. Treat old highs/lows as levels "to be attacked". The real S/R is the opening gaps and inefficiencies (NDOG, NWOG, "Event Horizon" — things outside the candles "like dark pools").
- **Timestamps:** [0:24:43](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=1483) — S/R too myopic; [0:55:04](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=3304) — "we're trading old reference points ... to engage and liquidate traders"; [1:26:25](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=5185) — outside-the-candles levels; [2:01:48](https://youtu.be/hdSscxZgE58?t=7308) — attack the levels where orders sit.

=== [2024 #5 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #5    August 9, 2024 (ID ul6x6BwOg6M)] — Turtle Soup (name origin & premise)  (aliases: turtle soup, fake out, fade)
- **Kind:** definition (introduction only)
- **What ICT says:** The name comes from Linda Raschke & Larry Connors' book *Street Smarts* (he paid $175 for it in the mid-90s; the book most influenced his understanding of stop hunts). It snubs Richard Dennis's "Turtles" trend-following system, which bought breakouts of the 20-day high (sold below 20-day low) and had a very low strike rate but caught big trends. Turtle soup does the opposite: when price breaks above a 20-day high and fails back into the range, trade the reversal. ICT says he does not just use a 20-day high/low — "there's other things" — and promised to show those characteristics at the end, but the stream ended before he got to it. He calls turtle soup "my version of a reversal pattern": retail resistance gets pegged, then reverses toward the opposite side (discount, and vice versa).
- **How to identify (as applied in this video):** Price trades above a high and immediately goes right back in — "that's your turtle soup, it's a fake out" — as opposed to a strong "nitrous oxide" push through the level.
- **Timestamps:** [0:05:20](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=320) — announces turtle soup lecture; [0:06:09](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=369) — Richard Dennis, 20-day breakout; [0:07:49](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=469) — Raschke's turtle soup; [0:51:33](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=3093) — failure back inside = turtle soup.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Full turtle soup characteristics not delivered in this lecture (stream cut short). He also teased talking about "wicks" — also not delivered.

=== [2024 #5 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #5    August 9, 2024 (ID ul6x6BwOg6M)] — Initial liquidity levels as real support/resistance overnight; previous day high/low
- **Kind:** example / rule
- **What ICT says:** The initial BSL/SSL levels are what the algorithm keeps referring back to — this is the "real" support/resistance, not lines drawn at random swing points. Retail sees a quick run up and consolidation as a "bull flag" — "that's fake". Most big setups form around the previous day's high and low (what he taught on BabyPips in 2010).
- **Example in this video:** NQ overnight Aug 8–9: the bull-flag-looking consolidation broke down to the old initial BSL level; swept below the NDOG to the NWOG high (next level below); gyrated around the initial SSL; rallied back to the NDOG, then to the previous day high and gyrated around the initial BSL; another bull-flag look at midnight ran down to the NWOG CE; then gyrated in the 12:00–12:30 range, popped through, consolidated around the old initial BSL, hit it, pumped, and at ~6 AM dropped back into the NDOG, bumped the previous day high again; ~7:58 AM it traded up to the initial SSL level and dropped.
- **Timestamps:** [1:09:07](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=4147) — bull flag is fake; [1:11:31](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=4291) — to NWOG high; [1:12:19](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=4339) — previous day high, initial BSL; [1:15:30](https://youtu.be/ul6x6BwOg6M?t=4530) — PDH/PDL setups, initial SSL test.

=== [2024 #6 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #6   August 12, 2024 Begins 9:15am ET (ID woAQ1XJeD7Y)] — Session start-time delineation (7:00 ET) and "wait for the first sweep before annotating"  (aliases: "7 o'clock rule", the initial run, last week's rules)
- **Kind:** rule / refinement (re-statement of Lecture #5 framework, applied live)
- **What ICT says:**
  - If your trading day starts at 7:00 a.m. ET (New York local time), you start watching price at 7:00 and mark the first short-term high/low formed by the 7:00 candle area. The chart clock must be ICT time (New York local). "7 o'clock isn't changing"; the rules are never optimized or "morphed".
  - He does NOT annotate the 7:00-candle high as the initial buy-side liquidity just because it is a high. He waits for the initial run to take the high out (gives it "some allowance"), then waits for a short-term high to form and price to trade lower and take out the low. Only then does he highlight both the high and the low.
  - The first 30 minutes after 7:00 (7:00–7:30) is used to form the initial high and low he delineates.
  - Start time variants: if you start at 8:00, use any relative equal highs/lows formed after 7:00 that have not been pierced; if they were already taken, sit and wait for your own relative equal highs/lows to form after 8:00. If you use the 9:00 hour you have additional factors, e.g. the regular-trading-hours opening range (he had shown Caleb that on Friday).
  - He frames the whole approach as: where is it going, why should it go there, how do I know when it will start, what is the beginning of that move.
- **How to identify:** a defined start time; a marked high/low pair after that time; the first run takes one side; then the other side is raided (see Relative equal highs/lows below).
- **How to use:** it defines which liquidity is "initial" for your session and tells you where to be patient. "Waiting" is the entry condition.
- **Example in this video:** Mon Aug 12, 2024. A high exists to the left of 7:00 and the 7:00 candle also makes a high. Price ran above those (relative equal highs, plus the London-session high), sold off, then broke lower leaving a fair value gap and displacement; the lows below (5:00 a.m. low, 7:00 range low) became the targets.
- **Timestamps:** [0:09:55](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=595) — "I'm not trying to annotate that high" until the run happens and the low is taken; [0:35:27](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=2127) — recap of why the 7:00 candle high is not labeled initial BSL; [0:36:17](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=2177) — what to do if you start at 8:00; [0:48:22](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=2902) — first 30 minutes post-7:00.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Teaching is explicitly a recap of "last week" (Lecture #5). He repeats that the rules do not change and no settings are tuned.

=== [2024 #6 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #6   August 12, 2024 Begins 9:15am ET (ID woAQ1XJeD7Y)] — Seek and Destroy (Monday/pre-news chop)  (aliases: S&D, jagged, "made jagged")
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Seek and Destroy is where the market goes just above a short-term high, then goes back lower to take an old short-term low, then back up to take the short-term high just formed, and so on. It runs you over even when you are right on direction. It is typical on a Monday morning ahead of big news days (here PPI/CPI), because large pools are not positioned and everyone is "chomping at the bit". Price action is "skittish" and "sloppy". He talks about waiting for the market to be "made jagged" (liquidity engineered) versus smooth areas.
- **How to identify:** repeated rotation above a fresh short-term high and below a fresh short-term low.
- **How to use:** be conservative: take partials, protect quickly, do not hunt home runs, and give yourself one shot.
- **Example in this video:** The opening stretch of the stream: his limit short filled, stop was brought down to protect, price rotated back and stopped him out in profit.
- **Timestamps:** [0:26:21](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=1581) — definition; [0:19:56](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=1196) — limit order filled, stop brought down, price came back into the entry area; [0:44:33](https://youtu.be/woAQ1XJeD7Y?t=2673) — back-and-forth before PPI/CPI days.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Describes day-type, not a model.

=== [2024 #6 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #6   August 12, 2024 Begins 9:15am ET (ID woAQ1XJeD7Y)] — Targets, draw on liquidity, and where he expects the end of the move

His interest in a short ends at the midpoint (CE) of the Friday NDOG. He says he would not have held through the minor low because the jagged action there is bait for more shorts; he would not buy and would not try a 200-handle short.
