=== [2024 #7 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #7   August 13, 2024 (ID -WItxLARGpE)] — Higher-timeframe draw on liquidity first (weekly → daily → intraday)  (aliases: where is price going to be drawn; "the first thing you're trying to do")
- **Kind:** rule / process
- **What ICT says:**
  - The first and only initial job is to get a feel for where the market could genuinely end up without much back-and-forth, i.e. the draw on liquidity. You are not trying to find patterns, the best stop location or where to move your stop. "If you don't have [the draw], everything else is going to put you offside."
  - He builds it from the weekly chart down to the daily, then the intraday charts. If the market is going to move, it moves to something on the daily chart.
  - A tradable idea needs range. He wants at least ~20 NQ handles of simple, timeable run, because from 20 handles he can take 10–15.
- **How to identify:** weekly and daily imbalances (SIBI/BISI), candle lows/highs, consequent encroachment of wicks and old highs/lows that price has not yet reached.
- **How to use:** mark the weekly and daily levels on your chart or notepad before the session. Then watch price either reach them or fail. Do not chase once price is already there.
- **Example in this video:**
  - Weekly: the low of the weekly candle of 22 July 2024 at 18883.25 (as captioned). A weekly sell-side imbalance / buy-side inefficiency (SIBI) sits there, and he also notes the consequent encroachment (CE) of that candle's wick. He asks for stiff opposition at the level ("I like to see some kind of stiff opposition to any forward run higher at the 18883¼ level"), meaning he does not expect a clean run through it.
  - Daily: the butt end of a daily candle low about 10 handles away, ~18854 (as captioned).
  - Result: NQ ran about 50 handles up off the Monday-evening/overnight gap area and hit both levels. Weekly and daily objectives were met early, so he saw nothing left to buy.
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:34](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=454) — weekly low 18883.25 and its CE · [0:10:03](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=603) — daily ~854 and weekly ~883/884 written on the notepad · [0:33:32](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=2012) — the draw is the first thing, "almost like meditation" · [3:09:24](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=11364) — if you do not know where the market is likely to draw to, everything else puts you offside
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** prices are as auto-captioned and may be off (e.g. thousands digits). He says "forget the PPI label" and just study the whole run from its origin (the new-day opening gap) to the target to see the PD arrays along the way.

=== [2024 #7 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #7   August 13, 2024 (ID -WItxLARGpE)] — Day-type filter: when to stand aside (grinding / choppy / jagged days)  (aliases: environment filter; "fish out of water"; "swim upstream")
- **Kind:** rule / psychology
- **What ICT says:**
  - He skips days where price just **grinds one way** with no meaningful retracement, or is **muddy, lethargic and jagged**: small candles, back-and-forth, coloring outside the lines, constantly overlapping. On such days PD arrays are not cleanly respected and he cannot reliably say where it will go.
  - He wants an environment with **gyration**: clean runs, obvious gaps, pullbacks into those gaps, and room to be a buyer and a seller in the same day or even hour ("Spider-Man web swinging"). He calls a day where he can buy and sell both sides a "playground".
  - Conditions that make him back off: right after a high-impact news driver, one-sided from the open, and any session where he cannot paint a simple 20-handle run.
  - Pre-set kill criteria: for the morning he said if price made a **higher high above the short-term high after sweeping the relative equal lows**, he was done and would close the stream. Otherwise by about 10:20 ET with no displacement below the low, he was done.
  - If he is in a trade on such a day he takes many more partials, because he cannot hold comfortably. On unpleasant days he may take one contract off and see how he feels, then another.
  - "High probability" is defined here as: it is very difficult to frame the other side of the market going against the trade idea. If both scenarios can be painted, it is not high probability.
- **How to identify:** one-sided grind with no real retracements; overlapping candles and tiny ranges; long consolidation; many small wick violations of arrays; heavy-news day.
- **How to use:** write down the conditions that make you not trade, and a stop-for-the-day trigger, before the session. Log these days and study them afterwards.
- **Timestamps:** [0:49:03](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=2943) — grinding days · [0:55:26](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=3326) — coping by scaling out one contract at a time · [1:25:13](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=5113) — definition of high probability · [1:29:14](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=5354) — lethargic, "swim upstream" days · [3:19:05](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=11945) — wants intraday swing-trader environment
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** he stresses this is personal fit. Some students may thrive on these days. The line he adds: "you have to know your own Kryptonite."

=== [2024 #7 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #7   August 13, 2024 (ID -WItxLARGpE)] — Macros (algorithmic time windows) — 10:50–11:10 ET and every hour  (aliases: macro; the "spool")
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - A macro is a **20-minute window every hour: 10 minutes before the top of the hour to 10 minutes after** (e.g. 9:50–10:10, 10:50–11:10, 11:50–12:10). Label it on your chart "10:50 to 11:10 a.m. Eastern Time".
  - Always use **New York time** regardless of where you live. If you do not use an alarm (e.g. 10:45 for the 10:50 macro), draw vertical lines framing each macro (but be careful they do not hide candle colors).
  - Inside the macro the market "spools" (like a fishing line leaving a reel). It will: (a) run to a **short-term low** (SSL / sell stops), (b) run to a **short-term high** (BSL / buy stops), or (c) trade to an **inefficiency** (FVG) to give smart money an entry **just before** it spools to the liquidity.
  - The macro is "like a supercharger" for entries: tighter fills, immediate response (price moving away and never near your stop is how you know you are on side).
  - Not every macro is equally good; he leaves "which one and when" for the book. He also mentions a **midnight–1:00 am** macro and a **final-hour-of-the-day** macro.
  - Entries outside macros are fine ("I'm not saying … ICT only teaches the first 10 minutes before and after the hour"), but you demand more of yourself.
  - **90-minute cycles:** he says "there is no 90-minute cycle" to copy; 90 minutes is just the smallest window you should give yourself, because it guarantees at least one macro.
  - Timing is "the most critical function in price delivery". The macro tells you when to look; the higher-timeframe draw tells you which direction.
- **How to identify:** clock window (ET). Then ask which of the three the market needs: SSL, BSL, or an FVG/OB entry first.
- **How to use:** be positioned in the first 10 minutes (before the hour) for the entry into the inefficiency, then expect the spool in the last half. At 10:52 (mid-macro) he saw the entry FVG, then the run up to the daily imbalance, then at 11:10 a second entry in the order block open.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 1m, Tue 13 Aug.
  - 10:50–11:10 macro: a bullish FVG (breakaway gap) trades down into at 10:52 → rally to the 18951.50 high and the daily volume imbalance. The OB open is revisited at 11:10 and delivers higher.
  - 11:50–12:10 macro: price dug down at about 11:50 into a sell-side run into the daily inefficiency at discount (mid-macro, ~12:00 per his narration), then rallied to a smaller volume imbalance above and the liquidity above the high ("rock-climbing footholds"). The real runs start inside the window; after 10:50–11:10 "look how shitty it gets".
- **Timestamps:** [2:40:28](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=9628) — 10:50–11:10, every 60 minutes · [2:41:16](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=9676) — the three things price does in a macro · [2:59:00](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=10740) — New York time always, alarms or vertical lines · [3:07:03](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=11223) — spooling analogy and the 11:50 run · [3:02:12](https://youtu.be/-WItxLARGpE?t=10932) — 90-minute cycle comment
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** he calls this his "clinic on macros" and says most other macros circulating are not macros. In this stream he says the 10:00 macro begins at 9:50 (10 minutes before the hour); this lecture makes the "every hour, ±10 minutes" statement explicit.

=== [2024 #8 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #8   August 14, 2024 (ID zU7iAqg4sSA)] — Buy-side delivery vs. sell-side delivery (algorithmic programs) — not buy/sell-side liquidity  (aliases: "sell program / buy program", "buy side of the curve / sell side of the curve")
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:**
  - Sell-side delivery is the algorithm delivering lower prices ("a sell program"), buy-side delivery is delivering higher prices. It is NOT sell-side liquidity (resting sell stops). Do not think buying/selling pressure; it spools in a direction "for the express purpose of delivering" prices.
  - Sell-side delivery means the market is going lower to reprice inefficiencies (FVGs, wick CEs) and then the liquidity below; it matters not how much buying/selling pressure there is.
  - While in a sell program, retracements are short-lived, controlled, "stunted", never protracted to the upside.
  - He ties this to the Market Maker Buy/Sell Models (2017 mentorship): the "buy side of the curve" is the run up; the "sell side of the curve" is the run down. He says everything he trades is part of an MMSM or MMBM even when he does not name it.
- **How to identify:** which way the algorithm is currently offering prices (a sequence of up-closed displacement and old highs being taken = buy-side delivery). See CISD for the switch.
- **How to use:** in a sell program, look for repricing to inefficiencies: bearish FVGs (sell-side imbalance / buy-side inefficiency), wick CEs, order blocks and breakers on the way down, and go to the sell-side liquidity below.
- **Timestamps:** [0:42:17](https://youtu.be/zU7iAqg4sSA?t=2537) — sell-side delivery is not sell-side liquidity · [1:38:03](https://youtu.be/zU7iAqg4sSA?t=5883) — sell program reprices to inefficiencies · [2:21:17](https://youtu.be/zU7iAqg4sSA?t=8477) — MM buy/sell models, buy side of the curve
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** In his words the algorithm "doesn't care how many people are buying or selling". This is his core argument against order flow / depth-of-market.

=== [2024 #8 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #8   August 14, 2024 (ID zU7iAqg4sSA)] — Turtle soup — only two forms: reversal (change in direction) and in-trend entry technique  (aliases: "Turtle Soup", "run on liquidity", "stop run")
- **Kind:** definition / new refinement (explicitly distinguished from the Street Smarts pattern)
- **What ICT says:**
  - Turtle soup = a run on liquidity that is not always above an old high or below an old low (he says the name was borrowed because he liked it from Connors/Raschke's Street Smarts, which taught him the stop-run idea; he has used the concept since 1995).
  - There are only two forms: buying below old lows or selling above old highs, either (1) as a reversal that changes the direction of delivery (CISD, sustained for a session/day or longer), or (2) as an entry mechanism inside an existing, still-unfolding sell or buy program.
  - Form 1: take out the buy stops above old highs (bodies as well as wicks), hit an objective (e.g., a NWOG or NDOG as a magnet), then CISD. The order block / breaker is part of that reversal ("breakers tend to be turtle soups in changing directional sustained price runs").
  - Form 2: every time you enter a short in a bearish FVG you are selling above an old high (the candle high marking the FVG's edge); every long in a bullish FVG is buying below an old low. It is not support and resistance but an absence of buyers who ever were afforded the chance above that candle's high.
  - Street Smarts version (for contrast): fade a break of the 20-day high/low (and "plus one" = one more day), with a very short profit objective; he calls it infantile, says he does not trade it, and recalls the turtle-trader style strike rate as under about 38%.
  - Harder variant: selling into a rally, a bold-faced bullish candle above short-term highs into a premium array. Takes much more experience and complementary PD arrays; do not start here.
- **How to identify:** (reversal) high swept + objective reached + CISD; (entry) retracement into an FVG in a program that already started.
- **How to use:** form 1: sell above the highs only after the objective and after delivery flips; form 2: put the limit order at the old high + 1 tick (see FVG entry section).
- **Example in this video:** NQ Aug 14, 2024: running above short-term highs / relative equal highs into the Jul 28 NWOG, then CISD at the OB open (form 1); after that, shorting into the FVG that formed in the sell leg (form 2). He adds that if price had reached the NWOG on the 8:30 first leg, then poked above again at 9:30 and failed, that second poke would have been the turtle soup.
- **Timestamps:** [1:10:28](https://youtu.be/zU7iAqg4sSA?t=4228) — turtle soup is anticipating turning points, not always above old highs · [2:22:04](https://youtu.be/zU7iAqg4sSA?t=8524) — reversal vs. entry mechanism · [2:24:27](https://youtu.be/zU7iAqg4sSA?t=8667) — Street Smarts 20-day version contrasted · [2:46:05](https://youtu.be/zU7iAqg4sSA?t=9965) — trading an FVG = trading a turtle soup · [3:33:27](https://youtu.be/zU7iAqg4sSA?t=12807) — "only two forms of turtle soup"
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Narrows/clarifies earlier use of the term. He calls the Street Smarts version the "wish version"/"street-smarts version" and says Linda Raschke and Larry Connors' book helped only to introduce the idea of stop runs.

=== [2024 #9 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #9  August 15, 2024 (ID pg5XBNkpotM)] — Trapped longs and where their stops sit (run on liquidity logic)  (aliases: "snare", "holding traders in", "jagged")
- **Kind:** definition / rule of thumb
- **What ICT says:** A one-sided run that keeps printing small wick/body pushes through highs is work to (a) hurt anyone who bought a breakout, (b) get more people long or stop out anyone already short, and (c) build net-long positioning that the larger player can later sell into. Longs trail their stops to just below the latest consolidation/gap/low, so that is where the sell-side liquidity is; relative equal lows from the 8:30 consolidation, the gap, and the consolidation itself all hold stops. Shorts' stops sit above the highs; he expects a final overshoot to clear them ("if I was making the market... I would do that too and then sink it after").
- **How to identify:** Price holds above a gap/low, refuses to rally away convincingly, small candles that do not run; a "three drives"-type pattern higher inside liquidity; each new high is only a few ticks to slightly more.
- **How to use:** Expect a retracement to engage the sell-side. Frame, don't force: "we're looking for signatures to support an idea that we're hunting", not "arm wrestling" the market.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 1m: 8:30 run up created a consolidation with relative equal lows; after the 9:30 open price ran to buy-side above 8:30 high and kept pushing; he sees "one, two, three" drives higher into the liquidity and says the longs' stops are right under the gap/consolidation.
- **Timestamps:** [0:11:22](https://youtu.be/pg5XBNkpotM?t=682) everyone long trails stops under here · [0:16:14](https://youtu.be/pg5XBNkpotM?t=974) three drives pattern higher inside liquidity · [0:22:00](https://youtu.be/pg5XBNkpotM?t=1320) the hold-above is potentially a trap · [0:25:15](https://youtu.be/pg5XBNkpotM?t=1515) stops just below the consolidation to protect the move
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** "If it doesn't rally and start running away, it's more likely just a snare." Contrast: if price does rally away immediately, he is not in it either.

=== [2024 #9 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #9  August 15, 2024 (ID pg5XBNkpotM)] — Stop run overshoot before the reversal  (aliases: "run the high", "10 to 20 handles", "one more gun")
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:** Before the retracement he expects one more run above the high that is large enough to engage stops and trap: not "a tick or two", but "10, 15 handles, maybe 20 handles above it", then peter out, especially when ES is weaker. This gives the "street money" comfort that they were right so they raise stops under the new low (second pool of liquidity) that gets run on the way down.
- **How to identify:** Clean old high/relative equal highs sitting just above; an SMT divergence with ES/YM.
- **How to use:** Don't short the initial poke; wait for the sweep, then the inversion of a gap below/MSS-type confirmation.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 1m about 10:23–10:28 ET: price expected to sweep the high, "it should do it now because we had a gap, it overshot, it should spring up there and clear that high", and does pop on the next candle ("there it is, anyone short here has been canceled out").
- **Timestamps:** [0:58:23](https://youtu.be/pg5XBNkpotM?t=3503) bump the high once more to give comfort · [0:59:10](https://youtu.be/pg5XBNkpotM?t=3550) second pool of liquidity · [1:01:43](https://youtu.be/pg5XBNkpotM?t=3703) 10–20 handles above the high then peter out · [1:04:06](https://youtu.be/pg5XBNkpotM?t=3846) it pops, shorts canceled
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The handle range is explicitly his rough preference, not a fixed rule.

- **Kind:** example only
- **What ICT says:** On Thursday 8/15 he took a short in the lunch macro window: a Turtle Soup, entered about **1.5 handles from the high**, on **15 contracts**, took **one partial of five**, trailed the stop, and was stopped out on the balance. It was linked to his stated afternoon retracement plan; will be in the review.

=== [2024 #10 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #10  August 16, 2024 (ID qoNQfVzVXZQ)] — High-resistance liquidity run and the "maximum carnage" low-of-day sweep
- **Kind:** refinement
- **What ICT says:** An area that price has traded in many times (multiple legs straight down, then reversal) is hard to trade through; a sell-off through it is a **high-resistance liquidity run**. But if price takes the day's low aggressively with speed, taking out everybody, that is "maximum carnage" and not a high-resistance run, especially on a Friday after a huge up week where even a 30% weekly retracement would not alter the bullish backdrop.
- **How to use:** Identify the lows where the day's longs (the longest winners) hold stops; if the lunch macro takes the low with speed, expect continuation lower into the close.
- **Example in this video:** Friday NQ 1m: price was drifting around a prior low area in the late morning (about 11:15–11:20 ET by his clock comments); he wanted any move below it to be quick, not drifting; the relative equal highs above were the alternative.
- **Timestamps:** [1:49:57](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=6597) high-resistance run defined · [1:52:25](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=6745) maximum carnage; speed or not · [1:53:12](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=6792) 30% weekly retrace would be healthy
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Applies the High/Low Resistance Liquidity Run concept to a chop day (previously taught for trending conditions).

=== [2024 #10 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #10  August 16, 2024 (ID qoNQfVzVXZQ)] — Draw on liquidity: which relative equal high is valid, and how he'd engineer the sweep
- **Kind:** example / refinement
- **What ICT says:** He walks through which highs count as valid relative equal highs (each high he points at is higher than the one to its right; audio is hard to follow, caption unclear) and compares the nearby relative equal lows (one is lower than its neighbour, so less clean); he concludes the buy-side is the more probable draw. His hypothetical "if I were running it": push through the "590" level, sweep the "601" level (later "601.50") with a wick only (no body), then slam below and take the low of day, keeping the weekly high in play and sweeping the day-session high. The drop would be fast and furious, not fuzzy, trapping shorts who are profitable and anyone with stops at the low. At the end of the session he again said he would sink it to the low of the day into the close (not a prediction).
- **How to identify:** (levels as captioned: "590", "601", "592", "60150"; leading digits not captioned)
- **How to use:** Treat clean, smooth levels as magnets; spikes through them can be quick.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Fri 8/16, about 11:45 ET (by stream clock): price near the NDOG and the relative equal highs; he asked which is easier, the liquidity above the REH or all the way back down.
- **Timestamps:** [2:19:14](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=8354) which is easier, REH or lows · [2:20:02](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=8402) which REH is valid · [2:21:07](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=8467) how he would run it · [2:46:09](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=9969) sink it to the low of the day into the close
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Strong statement that a market maker rig is "manual intervention".

=== [2024 #10 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #10  August 16, 2024 (ID qoNQfVzVXZQ)] — Don't use ICT's publicly drawn levels as your stop or liquidity  (aliases: "easy target", "pair of orders")
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** Do not put your stop where he said "this is where the stop would be" — with tens of thousands following live, you create an easy target that can be run (open the spreads, push a button). Learn to read it yourself. A level that is too smooth can be run: he imagines paired orders at the smooth level to engage stops just above it.
- **Timestamps:** [2:38:00](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=9480) don't put liquidity at his stop level · [2:42:06](https://youtu.be/qoNQfVzVXZQ?t=9726) too smooth to leave alone
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** A caveat relevant to using his live-stream levels as entries.

=== [2024 #11 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #11  August 19, 2024 Begins 9:15am ET (ID aONkyy-HTqE)] — Caleb's model (the beginner framework / learning ladder)  (aliases: "Caleb's entry mechanism", "Caleb's model", "session draw on liquidity first")
- **Kind:** entry model + curriculum (new coinage)
- **What ICT says:** He is deliberately forcing his son (a beginner who cannot yet determine the draw on liquidity) to use ONE entry mechanism, the fair value gap, inside order-flow context (price, not level 2 / DOM / volume profile). The aim is a baseline understanding of price action. The FVG may later lead him to see order blocks, breakers and inversion FVGs. It "should not necessarily be yours", and ICT says it does not matter which PD array you start with (order block, OTE, etc.) as long as you start and watch price daily.
- **How to identify / the steps as taught:**
  1. Outline liquidity above and below: relative equal highs and lows. Caleb only looks to the right of 7:00 ET, i.e. the last two hours before 9:00-9:30. Advanced students may also use the London range and Power-of-3 context from earlier teaching.
  2. Mark gap references: RTH opening range gap, new week opening gap, the nearest new day opening gap(s).
  3. Do not trade the first 30 minutes (opening range, 9:30-10:00). Watch which side of the liquidity is taken first. ("Water disturbed, jagged" = the taken side; smooth = the untouched side.)
  4. The side taken FIRST is the trap (Judas swing). The second side is the draw. ICT frames it with the Bible line "the first shall be last".
  5. After the first side is taken, wait for price to reclaim a reference (e.g. back above an old NDOG), then enter on a fair value gap using IOFED (see below).
  6. Take partials at an easy, low-threshold target (e.g. lower quadrant of the NWOG).
  7. Graduated later steps: hold through lunch and close at about 1:30 if in profit; eventually frame the full day (open-to-low zone) and then daily bias from weekly/monthly. He says you cannot derive daily bias until you can read the session draw.
- **How to use:** Session draws on liquidity are "dynamic" (exist above and below). Learn session bias first, then daily bias.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Aug 19: relative equal highs sit above, relative equal lows below. Price takes the sell side first after 9:30, sweeps below old lows and an old NDOG, then reclaims and runs to the buy side and NWOG.
- **Timestamps:** [1:32:04](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=5524) session draws are dynamic, learn this before daily bias · [1:46:27](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=6387) only look back two hours from 9:00 to 7:00 · [2:08:13](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=7693) take first side, second comes later · [2:29:47](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=8987) graduated steps: lunch hold, close at 1:30
- **Nuance / caveats:** He says repeatedly that he is not calling a bias in advance ("I'm not calling that, I'm looking for reasons to hunt a setup"). He lays out what he'd want to see and what would invalidate it. He also notes that experienced students can fold in London and pre-7:00 data. Caleb should not.

=== [2024 #11 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #11  August 19, 2024 Begins 9:15am ET (ID aONkyy-HTqE)] — Relative equal highs/lows: validity rule and the 7:00-9:00 look-back  (aliases: relative equal highs, relative equal lows, REH/REL)
- **Kind:** rule + refinement ("from this 2024 mentorship")
- **What ICT says:**
  - A valid relative equal high is a high that has a slightly LOWER high to the right of it. That is where liquidity is most likely tapped. A high slightly higher to the right can still be traded through; if it only wicks above and the bodies stay below, the earlier high is still a valid relative equal high and price will go into the wick. You then need to see price trade above and close with speed and magnitude.
  - Look-back window for Caleb: only from 7:00 ET onward. If you start watching at 9:00, look back just the last two hours (7:00-9:00).
  - Buy stops rest above those highs (shorts protect with buy stops); sell stops below lows. You don't need a book map/DOM to know that.
- **How to use:** Mark the equal highs and lows, wait for one side to be taken; note which was taken first. In this video the early run took the sell side first; the buy side was left intact and was the draw. Equal-lows sweep went to the left-most/lowest of the cluster (use the lowest swing low as the reference).
- **Example in this video:** Equal highs sit above (the 9:00 high is "lower than that one" so it qualifies). After 9:30 price takes the sell side first.
- **Timestamps:** [1:46:27](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=6387) 7:00 start, two-hour look-back · [2:56:01](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=10561) validation rule for equal highs · [3:04:54](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=11094) buy stops above highs

=== [2024 #11 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #11  August 19, 2024 Begins 9:15am ET (ID aONkyy-HTqE)] — Old highs and old lows as PD arrays (after they are broken)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** After price trades above an old high and retraces back down to it, the old high is a discount array: he says "old highs are the highest discount array; old lows are the lowest premium array" (he first misspoke and corrected himself). Every time price breaches a PD array, look back at the range just traveled, map every PD array in it, and check whether any were left untouched ("did you miss a PD array not traded to? did it disregard it? That's a good thing, it means you're on side").
- **Example in this video:** The old relative equal high, after being traded above, was used as a discount array on the retracement.
- **Timestamps:** [3:38:47](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=13127) old highs vs old lows as arrays · [3:39:37](https://youtu.be/aONkyy-HTqE?t=13177) un-traded PD arrays mean you are on the right side

=== [2024 #18 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #18  August 27, 2024 (ID yOZHawAE8QI)] — Liquidity accumulation: why smart money drops price below relative equal lows first  (aliases: accumulation, sell-stop accumulation, "time-based delivery")
- **Kind:** definition / explanation
- **What ICT says:**
  - When a large unfilled range (here the ORG) sits above, price often first drops below relative equal lows. Smart money needs a counterparty. It "accumulates" the sell stops/sell orders resting there, which become their buy orders in size.
  - It does not matter whether those sell orders are protective stops on longs or breakout entries. Relative equal lows are the visual shorthand he uses so he does not have to reveal how he distinguishes them.
  - Accumulation means price spending time at a pre-determined liquidity level. Inefficiencies (FVGs) are different: you do not want price meandering inside them (no "bull flags"). Price should trade to them, through them, and use them as a "springboard / rocket fuel".
  - Smart money never buys on an uptick or breakout. It buys on sell-side (momentum-down) delivery at discounted prices. The first down-close candle after a run of up-closes is the first "discounted" delivery. They add as price trades up, but the volume comes from the loading below.
  - The longer price spends below a pool, the larger and more protracted the later run ("it's spending time now because it's going to be a longer protracted run").
  - Delivery speed is in proportion to how close the inefficiency or liquidity is. Once the buying is done, candles expand and the run accelerates toward the full closure.
  - He contrasts this with a prior-day observation where price stabbed down inside a wick's CE and immediately came out: that is not accumulation.
- **How to use:**
  - If price keeps ignoring a PD array in the narrative direction and spends time at a liquidity pool, expect the repricing leg to be fast.
  - If price goes "off script" after a run to a level, do not over-leverage or force a trade; sit still.
- **Example in this video:** NQ: price opens, runs a little above, then drops below relative equal lows (inside the lower ORG half) and sits there, forming the pre-move FVG and ORG remainder above. After the sweep it reprices up to the first FVG and on to the ORG gap high.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:46:43](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=2803) — what benefit for smart money to engage sell stops.
  - [0:57:17](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=3437) — unfinished business above, accumulation below.
  - [0:58:50](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=3530) — liquidity vs inefficiency: spend time below liquidity, never inside FVGs.
  - [1:02:50](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=3770) — smart money never buys breakouts; off-script = sit still.
- **Nuance / caveats:** He says the "delivery to terminus" has already started (caption unclear on exact meaning; "terminus" appears here as the destination of the delivery, likely not the named model).

=== [2024 #18 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #18  August 27, 2024 (ID yOZHawAE8QI)] — Smart-money counterparty / why "buying and selling pressure" is rejected  (aliases: algorithm, Composite Man, market maker, draw on liquidity)
- **Kind:** rule / philosophy
- **What ICT says:**
  - Price is delivered by an algorithm. Stops "to the tick" at pre-announced levels cannot be explained by random buying and selling pressure. Depth of market is a facade; orders can be pulled or spoofed, so a big book at a price is not a magnet.
  - PD arrays are "arrays" in the programming sense: reference data points the algorithm refers back to, like ingredients in a recipe. The outcome is sometimes over- or under-cooked but still profitable for the market maker.
  - Draw on liquidity analogy: you go to the shoe store because you want shoes; the store is the draw.
  - Smart money does not need much volume at the end of the move because it loaded earlier. Retail can push orders into a single candle that smart money cannot.
  - Market structure is a controlled environment that can be "manipulated and disturbed".
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:33:48](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=2028) — start of the "no algorithm" rebuttal.
  - [0:46:43](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=2803) — draw on liquidity / counterparty.
  - [1:07:40](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=4060) — depth of market spoofing; not a magnet.
  - [1:14:10](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=4450) — what an "array" is.
- **Nuance / caveats:** Mostly argument rather than rules. Treat as motivation; the usable rules are in the neighboring concepts.

=== [2024 #18 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #18  August 27, 2024 (ID yOZHawAE8QI)] — Previous Day High/Low and the 3-day high/low reference list  (aliases: PDH/PDL, "honey pot", old highs/lows, buy-side/sell-side)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Keep a written list of the highest high and lowest low of the last three trading days, plus each of those days' own high and low (e.g., on Tuesday: Monday, Friday, Thursday). Sundays do not count; the only thing taken from Sunday is the 6:00 PM ET opening, for the NWOG.
  - Markets will refer back to these. Liquidity sits above old highs (buy stops) and below old lows (sell stops). They act as draws on liquidity "just like a new week opening gap or a new day opening gap".
  - Mark them by hand with a horizontal ray rather than relying on an indicator ("high touch over high tech"). He criticizes dependence on indicators such as LuxAlgo and says some of their smart money plotting is not what he would use.
  - Simple rule: when you sit down to trade and the market is in the lower half of the previous day's range, always look for a return to the previous day's low. The PDH/PDL is "like a honey pot", available every single day.
  - Tendency while approaching the target: speed and distance should increase as price nears the previous day's low. Anyone with a protective stop there can quickly pull it, so price runs to the pool fast, jumping and skipping (visible on 1-second or 5-second charts) before they can. One contract is enough to book the print.
- **How to identify / use:**
  - Opening in the lower half of the prior day's range = bias toward a sweep of PDL (and vice versa for the upper half).
  - After the sweep, look for the reaction: price rallies, takes relative equal highs.
- **Example in this video:**
  - ES 1m, Aug 27: price drops toward Monday Aug 26's low. Short entries at the order block / propulsion block, then at the volume imbalance as price opened and tagged it. The exit filled one tick from the candle low just above Monday's low area, then price spiked below it.
  - After taking Monday's low, price rallied easily above the relative equal highs, which shows the run on liquidity done.
  - He calls these two or three a week "easy ones", fractal to smaller ranges.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [3:08:27](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=11307) — highest high/lowest low of last 3 days; each day's high/low.
  - [3:10:03](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=11403) — Sunday: only the 6 PM opening matters.
  - [3:19:35](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=11975) — previous day's low as a "honey pot"; lower half → look to PDL.
  - [3:24:22](https://youtu.be/yOZHawAE8QI?t=12262) — why the approach to the low is fast.
- **Nuance / caveats:** ES price levels for this trade are not stated in the transcript. He says he does not trade or teach crypto and has no view on it.

=== [2024 #19 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #19  August 28, 2024 (ID i3olkndPmEE)] — Previous days' highs and lows as liquidity / algorithmic reference points  (aliases: old highs/lows, last three days' highs and lows, PD array, draw on liquidity)
- **Kind:** rule / definition
- **What ICT says:** Note the high and low of each of the last three days on your chart. Even if a later day has already traded through the stops above/below one, the algorithm still refers back to that same price. The algorithm does not know how many orders are resting there and does not need to; the reference point is defined by time (the daily interval's highest high / lowest low). Large, deep-pocket institutional participants keep buying/selling interest around those levels, and coded logic assumes resting stops (trailing stops of trend followers sit just under higher lows / over lower highs). So price keeps being drawn back to old levels and market participants can trade the fluctuation right after the level is touched. "One and done" is retail logic.
- **How to identify:** Mark the daily high/low of each of the last ~3 sessions as horizontal lines (he marks them on both charts so the same price shows on the 1m and 15s).
- **How to use:** Use them as draws on liquidity (targets) and as catalysts for turtle soup: e.g. a break above a previous high while bearish is a place to look to short, even on a daily chart for a position trader. Same logic works on daily/weekly/monthly charts; you just wait much longer for it to play out. "If you note the previous days highs and lows you're never going to run out of setups."
- **Example in this video:** NQ on Wed Aug 28: the area around Tuesday's (Aug 27) daily low is the focus. Morning: price sells down into and below Tuesday's low, makes an intraday short-term low, retraces, then dives lower again. Afternoon: after the sell-off to a lower low of the day, price is drawn back up toward Tuesday's low again (the "fishing" target at the end).
- **Timestamps:** [0:08:21](https://youtu.be/i3olkndPmEE?t=501) — why old highs/lows still matter; [0:12:19](https://youtu.be/i3olkndPmEE?t=739) — algorithm only needs the time-based reference; [0:13:56](https://youtu.be/i3olkndPmEE?t=836) — use as DOL, turtle soup catalyst, higher-timeframe use.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The area just above and just below a previous day low is "rich in order flow" and can be "mined" repeatedly, but only if you know what you are looking for ([0:39:55](https://youtu.be/i3olkndPmEE?t=2395)).

=== [2024 #19 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #19  August 28, 2024 (ID i3olkndPmEE)] — Taking partials when targeting a previous day's low  (aliases: partial profits, graduated approach to holding runners)
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:** When you are inside the range and reaching down for an old daily low (previous day's low), do not take your partial the first time price pierces it. Let it make a short-term low below the old low, retrace, and dive again; take the partial beneath that low. If you take profit just under the old low you may sell yourself short of a large run, because you cannot know how far the first drop goes. It teaches patience and holding.
- **How to identify:** Distinguish the type of low you are holding for: (a) a previous day's low, vs (b) an intraday low (a low formed inside the range between the day's highest high and lowest low). He says he does not like applying the wait-for-the-second-leg method to intraday lows (caption unclear on exact wording).
- **How to use:** A graduated plan for those who cannot yet hold runners: Stage 1 — if price breaks the same-day low, measure the day's high down to that low and project one quarter (one quadrant) extension below the low; getting out around there is a reasonable first partial ([0:26:09](https://youtu.be/i3olkndPmEE?t=1569), [0:31:03](https://youtu.be/i3olkndPmEE?t=1863)). Stage 2 — wait for price to make the short-term low below the target, then use the dynamic trailed partial (next concept). He says it is fine to simply exit below the low while you are learning, then watch whether the run materialises and grow into the trail.
- **Example in this video:** Price runs down through Tuesday's low, pops back above it with a wick, then dives again; the second leg is where the partial belongs. He also notes the "low of the day" keeps being reset: first low, then a lower one, then the current low of day, and attention shifts to each new one.
- **Timestamps:** [0:17:59](https://youtu.be/i3olkndPmEE?t=1079) — don't partial on first pierce; [0:26:09](https://youtu.be/i3olkndPmEE?t=1569) — range + 1 quadrant extension for same-day low; [0:31:03](https://youtu.be/i3olkndPmEE?t=1863) — stage 1 then stage 2.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He frames this as "a graduate perspective" so students do not force themselves into holding like he does and feel "like a deer in headlights". The one-quadrant extension references earlier teaching (range from high to low, quadrant projections).

=== [2024 #19 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Lecture #19  August 28, 2024 (ID i3olkndPmEE)] — Fake bull/bear flag at relative equal highs  (aliases: stop hunt then consolidation; fake bull flag)
- **Kind:** definition / identification
- **What ICT says:** Relative equal highs followed by a retracement/consolidation that holds above them looks like a bull flag/breakout to the unlearned (trend lines, waiting for a breakout "that never comes"). When the market is predisposed lower, that is a fake bull flag: the stop hunt above the highs followed by time spent there, then displacement down. Taught in the 2016/2017 mentorship (fake bull flags and bear flags).
- **How to identify:** A stop run above equal highs, then price lingers (spends time) inside inefficiencies or above the highs instead of leaving fast. Companion rule: you do not want price to stay in an inefficiency; you want it to spend little time and leave; for a bearish premium array you want the upper half not filled, and ideally not even traded to.
- **Example in this video:** NQ morning: highs relatively equal, price bumps them and spends time, then displaces down, entering several models at once.
- **Timestamps:** [0:35:02](https://youtu.be/i3olkndPmEE?t=2102) — fake bull flag logic; [0:36:40](https://youtu.be/i3olkndPmEE?t=2200) — what the unlearned see.

=== [2024 #22 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ September 04, 2024 (ID Ipjk9S8HTbA)] — Buy-side / sell-side liquidity, relative equal highs and the "news smokescreen"  (aliases ICT uses: opposing liquidity, SSL below Tuesday's low, REH)
- **Kind:** example / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - Tuesday (9/3) daily low and the lows formed in the new opening range are the sell-side liquidity below; relative equal highs plus the top of the NDOG are the buy-side liquidity above ("that's the liquidity" — take a screenshot).
  - Stops for traders who shorted the open sit above; the market runs them first, then forms the first FVG.
  - He says that **high or medium-impact news** on the economic calendar is used as a "smoke screen" so a drop looks like selling pressure. The 10:00 reaction after NDOG was an example: longs who stayed in past the NDOG were hit quickly and price went down to Tuesday's daily low, where sell-side liquidity rested.
  - After a large down day, "street money" (retail) wants to repeat yesterday's profitable direction (short). Smart money buys into the lows and rallies to the NDOG in the upper quadrant of the ORG. (His narration of who is being drawn in is loose; paraphrase with care.)
- **How to identify:** Mark prior day low/high, REH/REL, and the NDOG edges; note scheduled news times.
- **How to use:** Expect price to run the nearest obvious liquidity first and reverse into the PD array; avoid holding longs through a stop-run at the target level without a partial.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 9/4/2024: price drops/runs lows first, forms first FVG, rallies to ORG midpoint and NDOG, reverses sharply around 10:00 ET into the Tuesday daily low, later returns and pokes through the REH/NDOG top.
- **Timestamps:** [0:35:12](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=2112) — "invisible barrier" at the NDOG · [0:36:00](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=2160) — news as smokescreen · [0:43:58](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=2638) — REH + top of NDOG as the liquidity · [0:47:07](https://youtu.be/Ipjk9S8HTbA?t=2827) — street money vs smart money positioning
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The event is not named (he does not say which 10:00 release); do not assume one.

=== [2024 #23 — ICT NQ Live Execution September 6, 2024 100k [OSOK] (ID N9RW9v2kOoY)] — Turtle Soup short ("the real turtle soup") / One Shot One Kill  (aliases ICT uses: OSOK, "one shot one kill", fading a retail bull flag)
- **Kind:** entry model / example
- **What ICT says:**
  - This is "a turtle soup short, one shot one kill": he sells short by fading a retail bull flag into old highs, willing to let price run up and sweep those highs first.
  - "The real turtle soup" = knowing how to operate inside the daily candlestick: understanding what the range will do, where it will go and how it forms, so that the stop is in the right place.
  - He worked a **bearish order block** and annotated the **sell-side liquidity** below as the objective.
  - The whole position was one trade (single entry sequence at/near the high of the day), not a series of separate trades; he jokes about people claiming $100,000 in four days — "what took you so long?"
- **How to identify:** Price sweeps old/relative equal highs (retail sees a bull flag / breakout), reaches a bearish order block, and the higher-timeframe draw is down into a daily FVG / sell-side liquidity.
- **How to use:** Short into the sweep of the highs from the bearish order block; hold for the daily-range delivery rather than scalping; target the sell side below and the daily FVG midpoint.
- **Example in this video:** NQ, Thursday 9/5 to Friday 9/6/2024. He enters near the high of the day (his fills are shown at 8:05: an initial fill near the high, then adds of 4 and 4 more contracts — initial size not stated clearly in the captions), holds through the day, and covers on a limit order at the $100,000 profit level after price reaches the CE of the Aug 13, 2024 daily FVG in the London session.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:01](https://youtu.be/N9RW9v2kOoY?t=1) — turtle soup short, OSOK, bearish OB, SSL annotated · [0:03:12](https://youtu.be/N9RW9v2kOoY?t=192) — "the real turtle soup … how you operate inside of the daily candlesticks" · [0:07:18](https://youtu.be/N9RW9v2kOoY?t=438) — one single trade at the highs attacking the $100,000 level · [0:08:05](https://youtu.be/N9RW9v2kOoY?t=485) — fills shown: adds of 4 and 4, then the exit
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He does not give the stop distance or the initial size. "Turtle soup" here is defined by the context (sweep of highs, daily range logic), not by a lookback count.

=== [2024 #24 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ NonFarm Payroll September 06, 2024 (ID MxaJD1WIzos)] — 15-minute "bellwether" liquidity framing  (aliases: primary vs minor buy-side/sell-side, "dealing range" for the day)
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - The 15-minute chart is the bellwether time frame for where the obvious, clear pools of liquidity the algorithm refers to for that day's range are resting. Frame 15-minute relative equal highs (buy side) and relative equal lows / key lows (sell side) as the primary buy-side and sell-side pools. These give the first parameters for a narrative for the session or daily range.
  - Drop to the 5-minute: levels there are "minor" buy-side/sell-side pools inside the 15-min range. The 5m breaks the primary dealing range into smaller pools.
  - Do this every day, and put the 15-minute pools annotated at the start of 9:30 in every journal entry. No order-book/heatmap tool needed ("I've never been ambiguous about where it is").
  - He told his son Caleb to expect to mark the wrong pools at first and that is fine; do it on your own chart before watching the review.
- **How to identify:** 15m relative equal highs above / relative equal lows below; the pool of the 5m shows as a smaller equal-high/low inside that range.
- **How to use:** After framing, ask: stay inside the range, run just outside the low, just outside the high, or run both sides (and which first)? That frames the morning narrative.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 15m on Sept 6: relative equal highs above (buy side) and lows below; the sell-side low had been taken slightly lower than a second adjacent low, which he said "tipped its hand" (see next concept). On 5m he annotated a minor buy side at slightly-unequal highs (copying the line with Ctrl-drag, labeling "minor buy side liquidity pool").
- **Timestamps:** [0:17:53](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=1073) 15m is the bellwether; [0:21:47](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=1307) journal every day with 15m pools at 9:30; [0:36:58](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=2218) 5m = minor pools; [0:51:24](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=3084) primary vs minor
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** This is a beginner-level starting point he is giving Caleb; "primary" = defined on the 15-minute chart.

=== [2024 #24 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ NonFarm Payroll September 06, 2024 (ID MxaJD1WIzos)] — Choosing which liquidity pool is more likely to be run  (aliases: "high probability" pool, jagged vs blocky, tipped its hand)
- **Kind:** refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - Pre-NFP read: the sell-side relative equal lows had been undercut (a lower low was taken slightly below the adjacent low), and the move down also spent time below a wick (accumulation). He took this as sell side already "acted on / hand tipped", and the blocky, relatively-equal buy-side highs above as the more likely draw for the news.
  - Signature of a real stop hunt: price traded through a wick (piercing, jagged look, spent time below it = accumulating). A blocky, smooth-edged structure is less "disturbed". The disturbed side is treated as already satisfied; the untouched blocky side is the next candidate. (He also says the lower-high / undercut structure matters: a high that is lower than the adjacent one is already "disturbed", per his 2024 earlier teaching.)
  - Add context: day of week (Friday), what the week has done (down all week, big Tuesday drop), and whether the weekly/daily objective has already been achieved. Here the Aug 13 daily FVG low objective was already hit so a Friday NFP should not be a "prolonged move lower".
  - If it is a medium-impact driver instead of NFP/FOMC/CPI, he expects the same draw but gradually rather than in a flash.
- **How to identify:** (1) which pool has been taken or "softened" by a wick-through; (2) which side is blocky/untouched; (3) weekly direction and day of week; (4) HTF objective reached.
- **Example in this video:** 15m NQ Sept 6 pre-NFP: sell-side lows undercut, buy-side equal highs intact; price ran up to the buy side at the release. He notes the alternative (could have run the other low then up) and that he was not trading it.
- **Timestamps:** [0:15:23](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=923) why one pool is more likely; [0:40:11](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=2411) lower-than-adjacent highs/lows and wick vs blocky; [0:47:27](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=2847) real stop hunt vs blockiness + Friday + week direction
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The narration on this is partly garbled (he points at "this one/that one" on the chart). Treat the exact wording as (caption unclear); the principle (prefer the pool whose structure is already disturbed/undercut or the untouched blocky counterpart, plus day-of-week and weekly-direction context) is what is stated. He explicitly warns it is a lean, not a certainty ("it could just as easily gone down, wiped this one, then went up").

=== [2024 #24 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ NonFarm Payroll September 06, 2024 (ID MxaJD1WIzos)] — What moves price: only two mechanisms  (aliases: draw on liquidity)
- **Kind:** rule / definition
- **What ICT says:** Price only goes up to (a) go into inefficiencies above market price or (b) take buy-side liquidity (buy stops: either breakout longs' entries or stops protecting shorts). Price only goes down to (a) go into inefficiencies below (FVG, opening range gap) or (b) take sell stops below old lows (breakout shorts or stops protecting longs). "Nothing else"; manual interventions use the same levels, just farther and quicker. It does not need many contracts (he claims one contract can move price a tick when the next price is offered), so "buying and selling pressure" is not the driver; price skips creating small gaps when it is delivered to a level.
- **How to use:** Always ask which inefficiency or which pool price is reaching for.
- **Timestamps:** [1:29:59](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=5399) the two mechanisms; [1:31:33](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=5493) volume/contracts study; [3:12:17](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=11537) house-price analogy
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Stated as an absolute; he is demystifying the "algorithm" language.

=== [2024 #24 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ NonFarm Payroll September 06, 2024 (ID MxaJD1WIzos)] — Turtle Soup (ICT version)  (aliases: stop hunt reversal and continuation)
- **Kind:** entry model
- **What ICT says:**
  - Turtle soup is both a reversal and a continuation pattern: a short-term high is run (pierced) at the right time of day, then price reverses. You can use an FVG to enter the turtle soup. His use: price wicks above a short-term high / above the lower edge of a HTF bearish FVG into the premium of that FVG, you short, targeting the opposite extreme.
  - The idea comes from Linda Raschke's and Larry Connors' book (the turtle traders used a 20-day-high breakout); he took the name and the stop-hunt-then-return-into-range concept only; nothing of the rules. He claims his application is "the highest tier" of the idea. "Candle range theory" (the Twitter version of turtle soup) he calls worthless.
  - Rule for this trade: entry to be above the Wednesday daily high (the lower edge of the daily bearish FVG) and below the FVG's upper edge, and only after a short-term high forms at the intended time of day and is pierced; target the low of the Aug 13 daily FVG ("black line").
- **How to use:** Define the HTF FVG zone (entry window) and the target; wait for a short-term high to form at the right time; enter on the piercing and retrace; sit still.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Thursday Sept 5 (video recorded and executed live, with market-order hot buttons shown on chart): above the Wednesday daily high (about 18,552.5 as captioned, caption unclear), inside the daily bearish FVG; after the 9:30–10:00 opening range, price traded up into the 1h/15m FVG, wicked above the high and he sold, adding again on the wick (pyramid) and held to the Aug 13 daily FVG low.
- **Timestamps:** [2:33:05](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=9185) turtle soup reversal/continuation, CRT dismissed; [2:35:27](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=9327) run above Wednesday high = turtle soup; [2:44:19](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=9859) real turtle soup vs Raschke/Connors; [3:05:06](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=11106) executions shown
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Consistent with his earlier Turtle Soup teaching; new is the explicit pairing with an FVG entry and HTF target.

=== [2024 #24 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ NonFarm Payroll September 06, 2024 (ID MxaJD1WIzos)] — Liquidity engineering / late-day run to unseat trailed stops  (aliases: building liquidity, fake bull flags, time distortion)
- **Kind:** example / new coinage
- **What ICT says:** In the last hour of Thursday, price wicked up into the daily FVG high / balanced range area to unseat trailed stop losses of shorts; then it "marked time" and dripped down, leaving old NDOGs and fake bull flags in the upper quadrant of the daily FVG so buyers would place long entries with sell stops beneath ("engineering liquidity"). As price neared the low it was delivered quickly so longs would not pull orders; the sell stops needed to be there to be taken. He calls the holding pattern in the middle "time distortion": the market held in place while entities accumulate or distribute. He says this is scripted, a macro or "short list of orders and directives for the price engine" and denies it is buying/selling pressure.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 1m/5m, Thursday last hour into Friday early session: wick up, slow grind, fake flags, then quick drive into the Aug 13 FVG low at ~18,765.
- **Timestamps:** [3:18:44](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=11924) wick up in last hour, previous NDOGs; [3:20:22](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=12022) engineering sell stops; [3:13:53](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=11633) denying harmonic/S&R traders; [3:00:17](https://youtu.be/MxaJD1WIzos?t=10817) time distortion defined
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He had planned to close at the consequent encroachment of the daily FVG on Thursday (it only reached CE); he held for the low.

=== [2024 #26 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ September 11, 2024 (ID A1mLNIoEsq0)] — Micro liquidity and "bread-and-butter" scalps (high-frequency scalps, "Scout" (caption unclear))  (aliases: minor buy side / sell side, small structure)
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - There is always liquidity, not only above old highs and below old lows: small micro structure (candle lows/highs, tiny BPRs, wick midpoints) also holds stops. If you have the higher-timeframe inefficiencies on the chart and watch price gravitate to short-term sell side / buy side, you don't need the full daily range to deliver; you get small repeating scalps.
  - On a 15-second or 30-second chart there are many examples inside one daily range; adding all the up/down fluctuations NQ could "move" 1,500 points in a day even if the daily range is smaller.
  - Quants/HFT algorithms don't need a daily directional bias; they frame specific reference points and need a confirming quality (bodies' opens/closes matching the reference level), then trade both directions all day.
  - Claim: finding 2–3 of these per day and making 1.5–3% a day is feasible even with a very small account and micro lots; he contrasts this with swinging for 10–15%.
  - Warning: don't instantly start trading every OTE/FVG/iFVG/order block on a 15-second chart without a model; complexity comes from not knowing what you're looking for.
  - The factor to look for: a discount array that stops price falling (but not sideways) to afford buy-side delivery, or a premium array that stops price rising (not sideways) to afford sell-side delivery.
- **How to identify:** Bodies at a higher-timeframe inefficiency high/low plus a nearby small liquidity pool (candle low, equal lows).
- **How to use:** Scalp from the micro premium/discount arrays; it works because it needs only a small run, not the full range.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:26:59](https://youtu.be/A1mLNIoEsq0?t=1619) — wick CE hit and delivery is "enough for a scalp"
  - [0:28:35](https://youtu.be/A1mLNIoEsq0?t=1715) — "always liquidity", micro structure
  - [0:32:36](https://youtu.be/A1mLNIoEsq0?t=1956) — 2–3 setups/day, 1.5–3% a day claim; micro lots
  - [0:34:13](https://youtu.be/A1mLNIoEsq0?t=2053) — the premise: premium array -> not higher, not sideways, so lower; discount array the reverse
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Extends "buy-side / sell-side liquidity" teaching down to micro scale; the % claim is his statement, not a backtested figure.

=== [2024 #26 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ September 11, 2024 (ID A1mLNIoEsq0)] — Standard-deviation / measured-move projection from a dealing range to external range liquidity  (aliases: measure move, 1 standard deviation, fib extension)
- **Kind:** refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - Take any inefficiency (any timeframe, not only daily) and project its range (high to low) lower: one "standard deviation" is where price is reasonable to reach.
  - To anticipate going beyond that, the simple Fibonacci change: a viewer said "1.25"; ICT said go to the 1.5 level and add a 2 (likely meaning a -2.5 extension; caption unclear). That is "a beautiful area to anticipate external range beyond a measured move"; being content there means "you're done".
  - In this session price went down to the wick low, which would have been a superstar move; but a limit at the wick CE would never have been filled, hence the need for flexibility.
- **Timestamps:** [1:12:47](https://youtu.be/A1mLNIoEsq0?t=4367), [1:13:34](https://youtu.be/A1mLNIoEsq0?t=4414), [1:15:11](https://youtu.be/A1mLNIoEsq0?t=4511)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Exact extension numbers unclear in captions; verify against other videos on fib/standard deviations.

=== [2024 #27 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ September 12, 2024 (ID DRuEMX4RhMw)] — Two-layer buy-side / sell-side routine for the PM session (starting 1:30 PM ET)  (aliases: PM session, afternoon session protocol)
- **Kind:** rule / entry framework
- **What ICT says:**
  - Don't need to trade the morning session at all; the PM session is "always" the easiest session for index futures because the day's range is already printed. You won't get the lion's share, but it is ripe for consistent setups. After a large-range day expect the better trades from 1:30 PM ET into the last hour.
  - Routine: come back around 1:15–1:30 PM ET. On the 15-minute chart note the highest high and lowest low of the day; drop to 5m (or 1m) and mark the next lower high and next higher low inside that range. That gives two layers of buy side (day high and the lower high) and two layers of sell side (day low and the higher low).
  - Before 1:30, write down in the journal (with the time) which side you think price will gravitate to; then see what happens as the PM session unfolds into about 2:00 PM. Chances are it takes one of them, not both.
  - Same method for London: use the previous session; previous day's high/low and the high/low inside it formed up to 2:00 AM ET.
  - Last hour: ICT says price prices in the extreme high/low of the day with big expansion candles (an open near the low and a close at the high); then in the last ~45 minutes it comes off the high/low into settlement; four macros in the last hour (five if you count the 2:00 bleeding into 3:00). He pushes back on someone who said no new high/low gets made in the last hour (the caption's "that is absolute" is unclear).
  - If the AM kept the low of day intact and consolidated, it is "all positive for the afternoon". He also expects an eventual reach for the 19,695 relative equal highs.
- **How to identify:** 15m day high/low; 5m/1m nearest lower high / higher low inside.
- **How to use:** Predict side, journal it, trade once price shows structure toward it; he says the same process is what he does every afternoon ("never changes").
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:34:37](https://youtu.be/DRuEMX4RhMw?t=2077) — better trading from 1:30 PM after an anemic open
  - [1:21:22](https://youtu.be/DRuEMX4RhMw?t=4882) — mark highest high / lowest low plus inner high and low; journal the prediction
  - [1:22:12](https://youtu.be/DRuEMX4RhMw?t=4932) — same for London; previous session and 2:00 AM reference
  - [1:27:06](https://youtu.be/DRuEMX4RhMw?t=5226) — 15m then 5m then 1m layers; 1:30 and 2:00 PM
  - [1:30:15](https://youtu.be/DRuEMX4RhMw?t=5415) — last hour expansion, last 45 minutes into settlement; four macros
  - [1:31:02](https://youtu.be/DRuEMX4RhMw?t=5462) — PM session is the easiest for index futures
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** New explicit operational routine; he says it's "the same stuff in the book", so a reader can cross-check.

=== [2024 #32 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ CFDs Vs. Futures \ September 20, 2024 (ID Vo8WKYKtzIU)] — Time distortion and the 9:50–10:10 macro  (aliases ICT uses: time distortion, macro, "spool", algorithmic window)
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Back-and-forth consolidation before a macro is NOT lack of buyers or sellers and not a balance between them; it is "time distortion" — price waiting for a specific time. Here 9:50–10:10 AM is the algorithmic window when the market "spools" (starts to run).
  - A macro is a short list of directives (a script) that the delivery algorithm runs: it looks back through the previous session (overnight, yesterday afternoon, London, Asia), finds liquidity pools not yet traded to, and then delivers price there. It does not know how many contracts rest there, only that the low/high has not been traded.
  - Macro times repeat: the last 10 minutes before the top of each hour for 20 minutes total (here 9:50 for 20 minutes).
  - He says he does not require macro times when volatility is high; but when factors stack against you (small gap, Friday, end of week), stick to time.
  - "First the time, then what price around that time."
- **How to identify:** clock 9:50–10:10 ET (and other hourly :50–:10 windows); preceding choppy consolidation.
- **How to use:** expect the move toward untraded opposing liquidity once the window opens; wait for it rather than breaking out early.
- **Example in this video:** Friday Sept 20: at 9:50 price began to move lower toward the relative equal lows on the micro NQ.
- **Timestamps:** [0:13:41](https://youtu.be/Vo8WKYKtzIU?t=821) time distortion waiting for 9:50 · [0:33:02](https://youtu.be/Vo8WKYKtzIU?t=1982) it's scripted; repeats 10 minutes before each hour, 20-minute duration · [0:37:52](https://youtu.be/Vo8WKYKtzIU?t=2272) what a macro is · [0:45:07](https://youtu.be/Vo8WKYKtzIU?t=2707) macro = short program script heading to the pools
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** this is a more mechanical description of macros (looking back through the prior session) than in earlier lectures.

=== [2024 #35 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Tape Reading \ September 25, 2024 (ID NDwNIqUkASc)] — Higher-timeframe draw and leverage side  (aliases ICT uses: draw on liquidity, higher-time-frame draw, "by program", not picking the top)
- **Kind:** rule / bias
- **What ICT says:**
  - His interest remains the August 22, 2024 daily high (old daily high with BSL above) and smooth highs / relative equal highs left in place the night before (the "24.75/14.75 level", about 20,214.75, caption unclear). If price goes above the relative equal highs and rejects, that sets the stage for a run into the Aug 22 high; he is "not trying to pick the top in NASDAQ".
  - He cites an election year as a reason the higher-time-frame stays bullish (opinion; he says there is an incentive to keep the stock market looking up).
  - If the higher-time-frame is bullish, sticking to intraday longs is most appropriate; if you use the largest leverage, trade the side of the higher-time-frame draw — it theoretically lowers the chance of an over-leveraged trade on the wrong side (not 100%).
  - The initial run at the open was "not a Judas swing; it's just trying to get somewhere" (the smooth highs).
  - He will be wrong eventually; it is acceptable.
- **How to identify / use:** pick the intraday level as terminus, the higher-timeframe level as the next draw.
- **Example in this video:** Wed Sept 25 small lower gap (<40 handles) that filled; price ran to equal highs, fell back into the first FVG, then rallied again.
- **Timestamps:** [0:03:06](https://youtu.be/NDwNIqUkASc?t=186) small gap <40 handles, election year, HTF interest · [0:09:38](https://youtu.be/NDwNIqUkASc?t=578) relative equal highs, Aug 22 high above · [0:20:15](https://youtu.be/NDwNIqUkASc?t=1215) largest leverage on the HTF side · [0:43:37](https://youtu.be/NDwNIqUkASc?t=2617) not a Judas swing

=== [2024 #36 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ How To Identify High Resistance Liquidity Conditions \ September 27, 2024 (ID nr6CJqNWChw)] — Narrative (not bias) and stacking factors — time, PD arrays, liquidity  (aliases: narrative, "three factors", macro 9:50–10:10)
- **Kind:** rule / definition
- **What ICT says:**
  - "Narrative is not bias." Bias is bullish or bearish. Narrative also says where price is likely to go and why that is realistic.
  - He wants at least three factors behind a trade or an expectation, and one of the main ones must be time. Setups without time lack institutional sponsorship.
  - **Time:** the first 10 minutes before the top of the hour (9:50–10:00) and 10:00–10:10 form a roughly 20-minute macro window. Algorithms start firing and "spooling" price to run to an inefficiency or to liquidity above an old high / relative equal highs or below an old low / relative equal lows. Spooling is not directional.
  - Questions he asks before the open: Where did we open relative to yesterday's 4:14 PM RTH close? Where is the liquidity? Are there relative equal highs or lows? Is there a large inefficiency above or below? Why should price be interested in going higher or lower?
  - **Direction filter:** if you can justify the trade on both the buy side and the sell side, it is not high probability. Prefer days and times when the evidence leans heavily one way.
  - Other factors stacked: Friday/TGIF, an up week, gap-up open, relative equal lows below, an FVG to sell into.
  - Immediate feedback test: if the idea is right, you should see price quickly doing what you expected. If the things that should keep it from going higher are not showing up, you already have feedback that you are not on side.
  - Monday vs Friday: a retracement idea like this is less likely on a Monday because a new week may have an entirely different narrative.
- **How to identify:** A checklist of time + PD array + liquidity + day/week context.
- **How to use:** Only act when several of these line up; if they do not, sit still.
- **Example in this video:** Narrative for the day: Friday, up week, gap-up (premium) open, relative equal lows below as the draw, inside the macro at 9:57, so look for sells and ignore longs ("I don't care if it went up 100 handles").
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:13:44](https://youtu.be/nr6CJqNWChw?t=824) gives the "at least three factors, one is time" line.
  - [0:14:32](https://youtu.be/nr6CJqNWChw?t=872) gives the 9:50–10:10 macro description.
  - [0:16:07](https://youtu.be/nr6CJqNWChw?t=967) gives the one-sided versus two-sided test.
  - [0:31:19](https://youtu.be/nr6CJqNWChw?t=1879) gives the narrative-versus-bias line.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He frames the 9:50–10:10 window as a macro but does not name or number it here beyond those times.

=== [2024 #36 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ How To Identify High Resistance Liquidity Conditions \ September 27, 2024 (ID nr6CJqNWChw)] — Smooth vs jagged — obvious liquidity and retail perception  (aliases: relative equal lows as "support", "smooth lows", "jagged highs")
- **Kind:** definition / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - Retail sees relative equal lows as support and expects buying there; stops sit just below (sell stops, or "short buy stops"). That makes them a draw.
  - Price action is "smooth" where liquidity remains untaken (here the relative equal lows below) and "jagged" where it has already been run (the upside highs). The market tends to deliver to the smooth side.
  - Retail chart patterns (bull flags, pennants, wedges, descending triangles) are not the cause of price movement. They only "work" if price is already heading to a liquidity pool. If it is smooth above a bull flag and the downside is already made jagged, the flag may deliver, but only because price is running for liquidity.
  - You do not need a bookmap, DOM or ladder. You can see everything on the chart if you know the time, the day, the inefficiencies and the equal highs/lows.
- **How to identify:** Look for untouched equal highs/lows; compare which side of the market is "smooth".
- **How to use:** Frame the draw toward the smooth side; use obvious retail patterns as a clue to where stops will be.
- **Example in this video:** Relative equal lows below were smooth, the top had been made jagged. Later, bull-flag-looking consolidations were the setups for stop runs.
- **Timestamps:** [0:09:00](https://youtu.be/nr6CJqNWChw?t=540), [0:17:41](https://youtu.be/nr6CJqNWChw?t=1061), [0:50:30](https://youtu.be/nr6CJqNWChw?t=3030).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The term "smooth/jagged" is used loosely in this video; he does not give a formal definition.

=== [2024 #37 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Premarket Concepts \ September 30, 2024 (ID l7eu9yvUUtg)] — Relative equal lows / sell-side liquidity stacking on multiple timeframes  (aliases: "relative equal lows", SSL, resting stops)
- **Kind:** definition / example
- **What ICT says:** Sell-side liquidity rests under relative equal lows; the Sept 23 weekly low is also the 15-minute relative equal lows, so you can annotate them together as "weekly low + 15m relative equal lows sell-side". On the 15m chart he first notes there are NOT clean equal lows because each low is followed by a lower low ("probing lower") until a later zoom-out shows the daily relative equal lows and the weekly BISI high clustered together.
- **How to use:** these are the objective for the short; they are also where smart money would cover shorts and accumulate longs, hence a plausible narrative turning point.
- **Example in this video:** NQ sell-side at the 15m/daily relative equal lows near 19,953 below the hourly discount wick CE and the weekly BISI high; later the first-hour trade targets the intraday relative equal lows below the open.
- **Timestamps:** [0:26:19](https://youtu.be/l7eu9yvUUtg?t=1579) — 15m lows keep being undercut (not equal lows); [0:48:32](https://youtu.be/l7eu9yvUUtg?t=2912) — daily relative equal lows + weekly BISI; [0:49:23](https://youtu.be/l7eu9yvUUtg?t=2963) — stating the sell-side below them
- **Nuance / caveats:** None stated beyond "if it isn't obvious you are straining".

=== [2024 #37 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Premarket Concepts \ September 30, 2024 (ID l7eu9yvUUtg)] — Turtle Soup (origin of his stop-hunt thinking)
- **Kind:** definition / history
- **What ICT says:** Linda Raschke and Larry Connors' Turtle Soup (from "Street Smarts") fades the 20-day breakout used by Richard Dennis's turtles — buy the break below the 20-day low, sell the break above the 20-day high, a false-breakout pattern capitalizing on the turtles' low strike rate. He took from it the idea of looking at stop hunts and being on the other side of stopped-out traders, applied to intraday charts; he does not count 20 days, he counts candles/structure. He credits Larry Williams for other ideas (e.g. the currency-futures comparison that preceded SMT).
- **Timestamps:** [0:32:35](https://youtu.be/l7eu9yvUUtg?t=1955) — Turtle Soup explained; [0:34:11](https://youtu.be/l7eu9yvUUtg?t=2051) — how it became his intraday stop-hunt foundation; [0:30:13](https://youtu.be/l7eu9yvUUtg?t=1813) — 90s currency comparison
- **Nuance / caveats:** The 20-day-high/low rule is the book's, not his.

=== [2024 #37 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Premarket Concepts \ September 30, 2024 (ID l7eu9yvUUtg)] — Floor pivots and the "no panacea" principle  (aliases: pivot numbers, draw on liquidity vs terminus)
- **Kind:** refinement
- **What ICT says:** Early on he used floor pivot numbers as support/resistance; he later saw price run to the liquidity beyond them. A pivot is only a suggestion of where price might reach, like a draw on liquidity, never a terminus; liquidity below old lows/relative equal lows can be the low of the day, above old highs the high of the day. Nothing in trading is a panacea; the game is time, price, liquidity, inefficiencies.
- **Timestamps:** [1:46:52](https://youtu.be/l7eu9yvUUtg?t=6412) — pivots and liquidity; [1:47:40](https://youtu.be/l7eu9yvUUtg?t=6460) — nothing is a panacea
- **Nuance / caveats:** None.

=== [2024 #38 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Limit Orders & Volatility Pinball Drills \ October 01, 2024 (ID DnuCB5Stk-0)] — Retail breakout logic and why stops are the draw  (Buy-side/Sell-side liquidity, relative equal lows)
- **Kind:** definition / example
- **What ICT says:**
  - Retail buys the breakout above a short-term high (buy stop) and places stops below "support" (relative equal lows, a smooth gap, a wick low) and trails stops under wicks.
  - When price creates relative equal highs and then drives down hard, it is going after those sell-side stops. Read price by looking at where retail already got long and where their stops are.
  - The macro window starts the move; the target is the inefficiency or liquidity pool above or below.
  - He wants to be "diametrically opposed" to John Murphy's technical analysis book, which he recommends only to learn what the retail mindset sees; he has "zero affinity" for its content.
- **How to identify:** Relative equal highs followed by a quick drop; breakout-buy high; smooth swing lows and a clean gap below as "support".
- **How to use:** Wait for the stop run, then use PD arrays at the HTF level.
- **Example in this video:** Same NQ 1m: relative equal highs, aggressive drop, buy stops above the little high are the retail longs, their stops below the lows/wick are the target.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:41:28](https://youtu.be/DnuCB5Stk-0?t=2488) — "too perfect" classic example.
  - [0:43:02](https://youtu.be/DnuCB5Stk-0?t=2582) — buy stop on breakout.
  - [0:44:37](https://youtu.be/DnuCB5Stk-0?t=2677) — stops below the wick.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The "new day opening gap" low inside the area was "too insignificant" to buy at.

=== [2024 #38 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Limit Orders & Volatility Pinball Drills \ October 01, 2024 (ID DnuCB5Stk-0)] — Macro — "spooling" (7:50–8:10 window)
- **Kind:** refinement
- **What ICT says:** At 8:10 the drop occurred "in the last portion of that 750 to 810 macro" (caption; evening ET since this is the Asian session, inferred). A macro window does not mean the trade goes to target there: it means price "starts to spool", i.e. begins an algorithmic run to an inefficiency or liquidity pool above or below.
- **How to identify / use:** Combine the time window with the liquidity left behind (here the relative equal lows) and the HTF quadrant.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 1m Asian session, the stop run below the relative equal lows ended inside the macro; the rally followed.
- **Timestamps:** [1:07:19](https://youtu.be/DnuCB5Stk-0?t=4039)
