=== [2024 #40 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Tape Reading NQ \ October 02, 2024 (ID YxpHUwZhu80)] — Near-term liquidity first, then flip (range-bound week approach)  (aliases: "volatility pinball", "broken wing trick")
- **Kind:** entry model / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - In news weeks, expect range-bound, listless trading. Do not mindset a "trending day". Price bumps stops on one side, fakes a trend, and returns to the range.
  - Identify the nearest buy-side and sell-side pools (minor liquidity). Wait for one to be taken. Usually the first side taken can simply be flipped and traded to the other.
  - Significant price runs (30, 50+ handles, not 10-20) usually follow liquidity being taken first. Taking the sell-side first, especially with a bullish higher-timeframe bias, makes any discount array more trustworthy.
  - Why: the sell-side raid stops out profitable longs, traps breakout shorts, and gives smart money cheap fills (the sell stops become market sell orders that smart money buys into).
  - If neither side has been taken, he is "indifferent" and sits still.
- **How to identify:** Mark the closest clean highs/lows (minor BSL/SSL). Annotate whichever has been raided.
- **How to use:** After one side is taken, look for PD arrays (FVG, OB) in the direction away from the raid to ride to the other side. Without the raid, do nothing.
- **Example in this video:** Pre-market NQ 15m/5m: relative equal highs were disrupted early, price traded down into the weekly BISI. Minor sell-side sat below a down-close 5m candle and relative equal lows sat beneath. ICT preferred a sweep of the lows before a rally into the highs. He said an FVG forming after a drop below both lows, with price rallying, would have been a long. Price never did that, so no trade.
- **Timestamps:** [0:17:52](https://youtu.be/YxpHUwZhu80?t=1072) identify pools, wait for one to be taken, flip · [0:19:29](https://youtu.be/YxpHUwZhu80?t=1169) significant runs follow liquidity taken · [0:21:56](https://youtu.be/YxpHUwZhu80?t=1316) volatility pinball · [0:29:20](https://youtu.be/YxpHUwZhu80?t=1760) smart money buys the sell-stop liquidity
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Long bias was based on the election-year argument (markets propped up) and the bullish long-term trend. He says that is only his lean, not a requirement.

=== [2024 #40 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Tape Reading NQ \ October 02, 2024 (ID YxpHUwZhu80)] — Minor vs primary BSL/SSL (annotation at 9:00 ET)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:**
  - Before 9:00 ET, annotate the closest buy-side/sell-side pools as "minor". At 9:00 ET, re-annotate for the opening bell. The clean highs, clean lows or a singular high/low closest to price at that time are the "primary" buy-side and sell-side liquidity.
  - Do the same on 1m, 5m and 15m. Primary pools are not looked for until 9:00, because you wait for the opening bell and then expect a run to one of them.
  - The best trade circumstance is when the higher-timeframe (daily/weekly) draw is already clear.
- **How to identify:** At 9:00 ET find the nearest clean high and low relative to current price. Anything closer or smaller is minor.
- **How to use:** Treat the primary pools as the targets for the 9:30 opening bell and morning session.
- **Example in this video:** At 9:00, primary buy-side was a recent clean high, higher buy-side was farther away, and the lows below were primary sell-side. A minor sell-side sat at about 19924 (caption unclear).
- **Timestamps:** [0:22:46](https://youtu.be/YxpHUwZhu80?t=1366) minor vs primary defined · [0:53:46](https://youtu.be/YxpHUwZhu80?t=3226) annotating primary at 9:00
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** None.

=== [2024 #42 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ October NFP NQ \ October 04, 2024 (ID qcYYm1iNCIM)] — Minor vs primary liquidity drill (8:15 ET pre-news annotation)  (aliases: "minor buy side / sell side", "primary buy side / sell side")
- **Kind:** rule / study drill
- **What ICT says:**
  - At about 8:15 ET, on a 15-minute chart, see where price is. Mark the closest buy-side and sell-side pool at that moment. Those are the "minor" pools. Then look a little broader, above and below, for the next logical level of liquidity. Those are the "primary" pools.
  - Minor buy side: here a recent high (after the previous high was already cleared). Minor sell side: the nearest low. He ignores small consolidation lows. Using lows that are lower than earlier lows is what makes the pool the "first minor sell side".
  - Primary: cut through the consolidation to the relative equal lows (primary sell side) or relative equal highs (primary buy side). "Primary is just the next logical level of liquidity below your near close proximity minor."
  - Study drill for every NFP: watch which pool is taken first, then look for the opposite side to be traded to.
  - He promises to explain why this will not work as a trading method. It is a study mechanism and not a signal. (The explanation is not clearly given. Caption unclear.)
  - Primary buy side: if price goes up there it will run through to clear it, because shorts above think they are safe behind "retail resistance".
- **How to identify:** Nearest high/low at 8:15 equals minor. Next clean relative-equal highs/lows equals primary.
- **How to use:** Watch the 8:30 reaction on the 15m or 5m. Record which pool engages first. Wait 15 minutes. Then look for the other side.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 15m: minor buy side was tagged within seconds at 8:30, and the first pool taken was the minor buy side. ICT then looked to see whether price would go down to the minor sell side. He viewed the run as a possible accumulation ahead of running the primary buy stops.
- **Timestamps:** [0:13:16](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=796) drill rules · [0:18:12](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=1092) minor identified · [0:20:54](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=1254) primary = next logical level · [0:27:19](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=1639) first pool taken is the minor buy side
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Same definition as M24-040 (minor before 9:00, primary at 9:00). Here it is anchored at 8:15 for the 8:30 event. It answers a viewer question on what constitutes minor vs primary.

=== [2024 #42 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ October NFP NQ \ October 04, 2024 (ID qcYYm1iNCIM)] — Trading Asia after a last-hour sell-side raid at the close  (aliases: "market on close", "Asia protocol", "6:50-7:10 macro", "7:50-8:10 macro")
- **Kind:** entry model / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Preconditions: no medium- or high-impact news and no speeches in the economic calendar between 8:30 and 10:00 of the next New York session. Bullish context is assumed.
  - During the last hour of the cash session, price trades down to take a pool of sell-side (below relative equal lows or a multi-day low) and is held there into the close. That is the setup. (The move below the low and then rallying in the last hour is not the same high-probability case, since then smart money is only using the last hour to accumulate sell stops and hold price.)
  - At 6:00 PM ET (not Friday) the new ETH session starts. Wait until about 6:50-7:10 ET and look for reasons to be long.
  - The 6:50-7:10 macro should start sending price higher if smart money is long. After roughly the first 50 minutes of Asia the algorithm "starts spooling" price. Each short-term discount array is used to accumulate more longs.
  - If the 6:50-7:10 macro is silent, the 7:50-8:10 macro can still deliver, in his opinion a better one. Typically 20-40 handles (he says "anywhere between 25 and 40" and "20 to 40"), not necessarily 100. This holds even if news is due the next morning.
  - Asia is often the low of the day when bullish, or the high of the day when bearish.
  - Working window: Asia 7:00 to 9:00, which can extend to 10:00 (PM ET, inferred from the 6:00 PM ETH open). At 10:00, square up, or take some profit and leave a runner if you expect a daily continuation and there is no news at 8:30-10:00 next day.
  - Best case: Asia runs to midnight, London consolidates (or Asia is the real run and London consolidates), New York gives a second stage. The day can total 500-700 handles from the 6:00 PM ETH open to the NY high of day. He says this is not an everyday event and not every Asia session is good.
  - Validation: study by contrast the last hour of cash trading before the better Asia days. Do not rely on the last two weeks only.
  - Daily-chart filter he mentions: the previous close and next open are often close, bodies near each other. (Brief. Not fully explained.)
- **How to identify:** Check the economic calendar for 8:30-10:00 next day. See whether the last hour of RTH raided a clear sell-side and stayed there into the close.
- **How to use:** Look for longs after 6:50 PM ET, with the macro as the timing window. Manage at 10:00 with partial plus a runner.
- **Example in this video:** A "couple weeks back" he bought into a market-on-close idea in an instrument whose name is unclear in the caption ("radi", caption unclear). Asia kept rising for hours. Viewers emailed asking what happened in Asia.
- **Timestamps:** [1:16:50](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=4610) last-hour raid setup · [1:17:38](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=4658) wait until 6:50-7:10 · [1:18:26](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=4706) why the order matters · [1:20:01](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=4801) no news precondition · [1:21:36](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=4896) 10:00 profit and runner · [1:24:01](https://youtu.be/qcYYm1iNCIM?t=5041) 7:50-8:10 macro fallback
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** AM/PM is not stated explicitly in the captions. The 6:00 PM ET ETH open and a "6:50" macro make the Asia/evening reading near-certain. Instruments are not confirmed here (NQ context). Timing of the macro windows (6:50-7:10, 7:50-8:10) is stated as the first macro of Asia.

=== [2024 #44 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ NQ Tape Reading \ October 15, 2024 (ID HMn46vLjKvY)] — Old Highs/Lows of Previous Days and Week -- "endless supply of setups" (not support/resistance)  (aliases: "daily highs and lows", "old highs and lows")
- **Kind:** rule / example
- **What ICT says:**
  - Know each daily high and low of the last five days, at least last Friday's high/low, and the previous week's high and low. These are where order flow will come in; if bullish, wait for displacement.
  - He is not a support/resistance trader: price trading above an old high and returning to it does not mean it bounces from the line. He learned as a 20-year-old that textbook S/R doesn't reliably work that way.
  - Process (using last Thursday Oct 10 daily high): price rallies above the old high, dives back into the range, goes above again, drops again but does NOT take the relative equal lows (leaves the "bait"). That tells you it is unwilling to go lower; since you are already bullish ("in a buy program"), expect protraction higher. Do not buy just because it touched the level. Look for an inefficiency on the way down that acts as an inversion FVG; if it is good, price won't even touch it before leaving.
  - Assignment mindset: put the Oct 10 high on your own chart and watch how it gates above and returns; he leaves parts out deliberately for an interactive study.
- **Example in this video:** 30-second NQ chart, Mon Oct 14: price above the Thu Oct 10 daily high, back below, back above, below once more, then rallied; relative equal highs left behind (minor BSL). He went long on the inversion FVG formed on the drop, add-ons at the discount FVG at the 8:30 launch (see Volume Imbalances).
- **Timestamps:** [1:07:30](https://youtu.be/HMn46vLjKvY?t=4050) — yesterday's session; [1:09:59](https://youtu.be/HMn46vLjKvY?t=4199) — five days + last week's high/low = endless supply; [1:11:35](https://youtu.be/HMn46vLjKvY?t=4295) — watching how price gates above the old high; [1:13:08](https://youtu.be/HMn46vLjKvY?t=4388) — iFVG on the way down.

=== [2024 #46 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ News Release Tape Reading \ October 17, 2024 (ID z2t62hgjLXo)] — Draw on liquidity: nested relative equal highs and prior-day high as targets
- **Kind:** example | rule
- **What ICT says:** On the daily chart there is sell-side liquidity "still building underneath" and buy side above, including Tuesday 15 Oct's daily high. On the 60-minute chart the area is "too clean" (begging to be run). He anticipates a run up to the "640s at the minimum," then Tuesday's high (caption: 20,659), then a higher high (caption: ~680) — digits are caption-derived. On the 15-minute chart he points to "two nested relative equal highs" (a high, then a higher-but-nearly-equal high, then a high above both): when the targets are that obvious, "it's easy to just side with that as the initial draw until price proves it's not interested," which he reads at 8:30.
- **How to use:** Treat obvious relative equal highs as the initial draw. Ignore nearer minor buy-side (the "first pass up here I would completely ignore that") and focus on the two relative equal highs. If price runs through them it can visit the next high; alternatively it can only reach the consequent encroachment of the range between the relative equal highs and the high above (about halfway) and then roll over to take relative equal lows.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 15m, 17 Oct: price consolidates after 8:30; ICT wants an up move to the CE of the relative-equal-highs-to-next-high range; wicks do reach that CE but candle bodies stop below it ("bodies respecting the level while the wicks are tossed above it"), and then price snaps down. He wanted it to come back down, disrupt the lows, and work down into a volume imbalance, and said he would NOT be interested in being long yet.
- **Timestamps:** [0:06:34](https://youtu.be/z2t62hgjLXo?t=394) — 60-min "too clean"; [0:09:49](https://youtu.be/z2t62hgjLXo?t=589) — nested relative equal highs; [0:24:28](https://youtu.be/z2t62hgjLXo?t=1468) — ignore minor buy side, focus on two equal highs; [0:31:59](https://youtu.be/z2t62hgjLXo?t=1919) — CE of range between REH and higher high works; [0:32:48](https://youtu.be/z2t62hgjLXo?t=1968) — wicks vs bodies "horseshoes" analogy.
- **Nuance:** Wicks tag a level while bodies decline to close beyond it — he calls this the horseshoe-tossing idea (orders tossed around a general area; bodies show real acceptance). He jokes that the correct read of the CE reaction looked like "time travel."

=== [2024 #46 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ News Release Tape Reading \ October 17, 2024 (ID z2t62hgjLXo)] — London session false breakout / run on liquidity then discount-array entry (ICT's own London trade, recap)
- **Kind:** example only (with entry-model content)
- **What ICT says:** He woke during London and traded. Sequence: market breaks a short-term high; then a run on liquidity below a low; then trades down into a delivery of a volume imbalance, and rallies. The breakout above the high is "going to be long above this high" — and he loves seeing false breaks in London where breakout traders would get in long or short: because it is an election year the market is predisposed higher, so breakout buyers buy the high, price runs down and stops them out, then takes the market higher again ("ripping their guts out") — "beautiful setup." What he waits for: does the market give him the chance to get in while price is dropping into an obvious discount array? The consolidation before looks like first-stage accumulation. He enters in the fair value gap (bid-side/BISI efficiency, volume imbalance included) as price approaches the 4:50–5:10 ET macro (caption "450 to 510 macro"), accumulating an early position.
- **How to use:** Entry range anywhere within the FVG/volume imbalance (his fill was in the volume imbalance); a breakaway gap above shown in yellow (he wanted it to stay open); a retracement into an order block "that is a propulsion block" gives repeated entries: price trades back into the propulsion block, he entered as it traded into it, hit it twice and exited, then price rolled back to his initial entry. Target: Fib extension on the price run — he says the setting he uses to place the target is shown on screen and he aims for "1.25" (caption; likely the 1.25 extension, i.e., 125% of the run). He refuses to reveal his personal fib settings until his book is out ("it's too good for public consumption right now"), noting "quarters" retracements will be in the book.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 5m, London morning 17 Oct 2024: short-term high broken, sell-side run below a low, drop into volume imbalance/FVG (fill), rally; breakaway gap marked in yellow; propulsion block entry (traded into twice); exit at 1.25 fib projection of the run; price later rolled back to his initial entry level.
- **Timestamps:** [0:12:16](https://youtu.be/z2t62hgjLXo?t=736) — London sequence; [0:13:04](https://youtu.be/z2t62hgjLXo?t=784) — false breaks, trapped breakout traders; [0:14:41](https://youtu.be/z2t62hgjLXo?t=881) — FVG + volume imbalance entry before the 4:50–5:10 macro; [0:15:31](https://youtu.be/z2t62hgjLXo?t=931) — breakaway gap, propulsion block; [0:16:21](https://youtu.be/z2t62hgjLXo?t=981) — exit via fib 1.25.
- **Nuance:** Points to the 2016 mentorship Month 4 content for the propulsion block lesson (on his YouTube playlist). He admits his on-screen boxes sometimes miss the exact volume imbalance because he juggles annotating and talking (he is 52 and keyed on the price axis), but says you should always annotate the volume imbalance with the FVG.

=== [2024 #47 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ Premarket Tape Reading NQ Futures \ October 21, 2024 (ID CQk-5lAirn0)] — Session highs and lows as the default draw on liquidity (no-news protocol)  (aliases: "previous session highs and lows", "four opportunities per day")
- **Kind:** rule | entry-framing model
- **What ICT says:**
  - When there is no significant data (empty economic calendar, no volatility injection at 8:30), aim for session highs and lows: the high/low of London, Asia, the previous day's PM session and AM session (not just the daily high/low). Four sessions per day = four chances to frame a setup: Asian, London, NY AM, NY PM. He does NOT want people trading every session, but if your schedule allows, tape-reading them all makes you a well-rounded trader.
  - It also applies on news days; it is just his "usual reach."
  - Use these finite reference points ("not something that's always morphing") to anticipate where the next premium/discount array forms, including pre-market without being present for the bell.
  - London's range: use the "overnight dealing range" (taught last week). Midnight NY time is the true day open; the London killzone is 2:00–5:00 a.m. ET; the highest high and lowest low in that window are the London session high/low (the algorithm looks for liquidity and PD arrays in that window). If price is in a clean protraction in one direction, don't grade the range; if there is a consolidation, grade it.
  - Which buy side matters? He asks which is more likely the draw for the morning: the London session high (a cluster of relative equal "smooth" highs) or a single nearby candle high. His answer: the London session high is the more salient; the single-candle high is a minor objective on the way, and he wants to see price accelerate through it to reach the London session high ("before they take their stops out").
  - The NY AM window referenced: 9:30 to 11:00 (London close) going into noon.
  - Applies equally to Forex, bonds, currency futures: "time is universal" across asset classes except crypto (he has not investigated crypto) and he has no experience with the Nifty 50.
  - On any day, these references mean "you'll never run out of setups," including on high resistance liquidity run days; on low resistance days they are "absolute bangers" for easy low-hanging objectives.
- **How to identify:** Mark Asian, London, previous NY AM/PM highs/lows; annotate relative equal highs/lows; Thursday's/Wednesday's Asian lows can be older sell-side targets (he shows Thursday 17 Oct's Asian session sell-side low, at about 8:30 p.m. ET, and Wednesday's Asian session SSL with relative equal lows).
- **How to use:** Pick the nearest significant session level on the side of your bias, framed with the opening range gap and the 9:30–10:00 window. Annotate minor liquidity thinly (ghosted) and the major reference levels bold, so your eye goes to the impactful level; minor buy side "top left" is noted as a stop-trail level of shorts.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 5m, Mon 21 Oct pre-market: Thursday's opening range gap low and Thursday's Asian session sell-side lie below; the London session high (relative equal highs) is above; the current range between a minor high and minor low is "either it stays in the range or runs for the buy side or the sell side."
- **Timestamps:** [0:13:22](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=802) — session highs/lows when no data; [0:15:00](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=900) — four sessions per day; [0:19:49](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=1189) — midnight true day, London killzone 2–5 a.m., London high/low; [0:21:32](https://youtu.be/CQk-5lAirn0?t=1292) — which buy side is the real draw.
- **Nuance / caveats:** Forms the bridge between the "overnight dealing range" (last week) and the opening range gap rule below.

=== [2024 #48 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ How ICT Picks Winning FVG's & Orderblocks \ October 25, 2024 (ID svYZKOrWPRo)] — Direction from smooth highs/lows left behind (draw on liquidity after a sell-off)  (aliases: "smooth highs", "unfinished business", "relative equal highs", "single highs")
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - After a large drop (Wed), retail expects continuation lower, so shorts defend "smooth" relative-equal highs left above the market. Those stops are the target.
  - Therefore the inside day's direction is solved by looking at where price left smooth highs or lows that have not been engaged, and not at the drop itself. His Thursday tweets said "look up, don't go short". He calls the untouched orders above "unfinished business".
  - A single high also counts; it is not always relative equal highs. Equal highs and lows are just what new students spot easily.
  - Prime candidate on the 15-minute chart: "high, lower high, lower high" smoothness above price.
  - He also leans on the higher-timeframe narrative: primary bull market, election year, "we're not trying to pick the top in a primary bull market".
- **How to use:** if bias is bullish, only look for buy-side draws and bullish PD arrays. He is "not interested in shorts".
- **Example in this video:** 15-minute relative equal highs above price, with unfinished business above NQ roughly 20,659.25 (spoken "659 and a quarter", caption unclear) and 20,680. Both buy-side levels were run Friday.
- **Timestamps:** [0:02:36](https://youtu.be/svYZKOrWPRo?t=156) relative equal highs and the "10 o'clock may use data to go through" idea; [0:15:29](https://youtu.be/svYZKOrWPRo?t=929) "unfinished business"; [1:10:14](https://youtu.be/svYZKOrWPRo?t=4214) bias bullish means only bullish PD arrays; [1:40:54](https://youtu.be/svYZKOrWPRo?t=6054) smooth highs on 15m are the prime candidate.

=== [2024 #48 — ICT 2024 Mentorship \ How ICT Picks Winning FVG's & Orderblocks \ October 25, 2024 (ID svYZKOrWPRo)] — Draw on liquidity inventory (sessions)  (aliases: "DOL", "session highs and lows")
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** you never run short on liquidity references: Asian session high/low, London, the NY pre-market range between 7:00 and 9:00, NY AM, the lunch range between 11:30 and 1:30, and the previous day's PM session. Judge each one against whether the economic calendar lets price reach it. You need a DOL and a one-rule-set model is not realistic; there are too many facets.

=== [2024 #51 — NQ December 05, 2024 Seek & Destroy Review (ID 403Dzt_Vl5Y)] — One-quarter-of-the-broken-range extension as an upside objective  (aliases: rule of thumb, "easiest draw on liquidity", frame of reference for turtle soup)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Once a range's high is taken out, look for an upside objective: simple, cut-and-dry draw on liquidity, or a frame of reference for a potential turtle soup.
  - His rule of thumb: one quarter of the previous range, projected beyond the broken high, is where he thinks markets can trade to. He says it is not based on anything in technical books, just something he has used over the years.
  - When the market is at all-time highs, you stay with going long until it proves otherwise.
  - The extension target does not need to be hit. Here price made a higher high but failed to reach it (the hourly high stopped just below 21580, at what the captions give as about 21567.25, caption unclear). He says it does not matter, and that it could rip through it later.
  - He told Sunday viewers "this is about as far as I would like to see it trade to" (21580.25).
- **How to identify:** a daily retracement leg (the high down to the retracement low) is the range; draw the extension of one quarter of that range above the old high with the Fib tool.
- **How to use:** draw/frame for targets or turtle-soup reversals at that level.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Dec 2024: Sunday video stated 21580.25 after the November/prior-week high was taken; Thursday traded above the high but stopped short of it.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:52](https://youtu.be/403Dzt_Vl5Y?t=52) the level and the Fib reference · [0:01:40](https://youtu.be/403Dzt_Vl5Y?t=100) one-quarter rule · [0:02:27](https://youtu.be/403Dzt_Vl5Y?t=147) "not important for me to be accurate about that"
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** pairs with M24-050 where the same 21580.25 was introduced. Whether the "range" is measured from the retracement low or other swing is inferred from the Fib on his chart; the transcript only says "previous range".

=== [2026 #2 — NonFarm Payroll October 2026 Lecture (ID 59Elm8myGEc)] — Keep the toolbox small: draw on liquidity first, a few PD arrays  (aliases ICT uses: note the draw on liquidity, "you only need a couple things")
- **Kind:** rule (process)
- **What ICT says:**
  - The first lecture topic is knowing the draw on liquidity (where price is going and why). With that, you only need a couple of PD arrays: inversion FVG, bullish FVG, bearish FVG, a breaker, and perhaps an immediate rebalance (it signals speed afterward).
  - "You got 80% of this game licked" by spending time on why price should go to a specific level.
  - You do not need SMT, Commitment of Traders, volume profile or indicators on every trade. He did not use SMT today or yesterday, nor COT.
  - Choose the one PD array that clicks for you first; stay with what you identify easily in hindsight; do not chase new tools.
  - Futures markets share one high, low, open and close for everyone, so the candlestick's OHLC is the only data needed. He argues volume-profile traders already single out time but over a broad range of what has already happened; he predicts what is not there yet.
  - Do not look at the crowd: "I don't want to be where the majority is"; smart money hunts singly or in small packs and will be in the herd without doing what the herd does.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:27:28](https://youtu.be/59Elm8myGEc?t=1648) — only a couple PD arrays; 80% rule; no SMT/COT needed
  - [0:23:26](https://youtu.be/59Elm8myGEc?t=1406) — candlestick book vs volume totals
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Directly rebuts adding every concept from his channel to each trade.

=== [2026 #5 — NQ December Commentary 09/29/2026 (ID I7fIf1eEz0w)] — Liquidity Pools from Highs and Lows
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Each new high and each prior low holds resting liquidity. When price reaches a new high, that high becomes an anchor point for future buy-side liquidity; old lows hold sell-side liquidity.
- **How to identify:** Mark every swing high and every swing low on the chart.
- **How to use:** Use these as target zones; when price runs upward, look for highs ahead as resistance; when price runs downward, look for old lows as support.
- **Example in this video:** London session lows hold liquidity; Tuesday morning highs hold liquidity; these are identified as next salient levels.
- **Timestamps:** [0:03:28](https://youtu.be/I7fIf1eEz0w?t=208) "buy side there" at opening range high, [0:07:30](https://youtu.be/I7fIf1eEz0w?t=450) "both of those are pools of liquidity that I think are going to be salient."
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Not every high/low holds liquidity; only those that represent swing turning points.

=== [2026 #6 — NQ AM Session Low Of Day Long Review (ID UVVmS0de0g0)] — Macro Times (Spooling Windows)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Algorithmic order clustering at specific times: last 10 minutes of every hour + first 10 minutes of the next hour (e.g., 10:50–11:10 AM ET). Institutions schedule major order flow into these windows to avoid moving the market aggressively. During macro time, price "spools" (elongates, protract, creates volatility) because order flow arrives scheduled, not reactive. Volume doesn't need to be high; one contract can create movement if order flow is timed.
- **How to identify:** Mark 10:50, 11:50, 12:50, 1:50 PM ET, etc., on 1-minute chart.
- **How to use:** Anticipate major price moves into and out of macro times. If first 10 minutes (10:50–11:00) don't show spooling, expect it in the last 10 minutes (11:00–11:10). Use macro time perspective to know when to expect momentum and when to wait.
- **Example in this video:** Market dropped into macro window 10:50–11:10 AM ET, hitting the two-SD target of the RTH opening range gap and Thursday's daily low.
- **Timestamps:** [0:35:00](https://youtu.be/UVVmS0de0g0?t=2100) "The market trades down into 1050," [0:35:48](https://youtu.be/UVVmS0de0g0?t=2148) detailed macro window logic.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Criticism that macros don't exist (e.g., from fake students) is ignorance; ICT emphasizes macros are not about volume, they're about order-timing spooling.

=== [2026 #9 — NQ Dec Futures September FOMC Trade Review (ID 50VbES10ULs)] — Relative Equal Highs / Lows (REH/REL)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Swing highs or lows that are at approximately equal prices across multiple sessions. When price reaches REH or REL, it often acts as liquidity resistance/support. Breaking above REH or below REL confirms directional bias.
- **How to identify:** Mark all swing highs across multiple sessions; identify those at similar price levels = REH. Same for lows = REL.
- **How to use:** Use as breakout trigger; wait for close above REH or below REL to confirm direction; buy-side liquidity rests at REH, sell-side at REL.
- **Example in this video:** Tuesday's high and Monday's high are at similar levels (REH); price pierced this twice on Wednesday pre-FOMC, then broke through on the second attempt post-FOMC.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:24](https://youtu.be/50VbES10ULs?t=264) "relative equal highs," [0:05:12](https://youtu.be/50VbES10ULs?t=312) "Tuesday's daily high and the relative equal highs... we pierced that already at 2:00."
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Visual smoothness of REH/REL (when charted) indicates strength of the liquidity level.

=== [2026 #19 — How New Students Should Start Part 1 (ID BWEPtHZ1Y2Y)] — Draw on liquidity — step-by-step mapping  (aliases: DOL)
- **Kind:** process
- **What ICT says:**
  - "Flesh out the chart" with no entries: know what you are looking for, why it should be there, which levels are important, and what would change it. This is "the first part of what you are supposed to learn".
  - Layers used: monthly high/low, daily range equilibrium, daily BISI/SIBI zone graded in eighths, relative equal highs on the hourly, minor sell-side lows, RTH OG settlement.
  - Draw hierarchy: price moves from the nearest key level to the next (e.g., from the daily gray box's upper octant down toward the 0.25 line, then a minor sell side). He wants to see the candles "book" with interest to keep going lower, not just reach the level.
  - The more PD arrays and levels you can anchor to the daily chart, the more probable the setup; "probable" does not mean guaranteed or no losing trades; it means never feeling confused. If your read is wrong, that is "a transactional operating error" and the operator owns it.
- **Example in this video:** at 8:39 price sits inside the daily gray zone after a 8:09 buy-side raid (relative equal highs taken on Friday at 10:00, then a drop). He labels the lowest low as sell-side liquidity (deep red, potential draw). He tracks whether price will reach the 0.25 level (29,371) and then the minor sell side.
- **Timestamps:** [0:53:15](https://youtu.be/BWEPtHZ1Y2Y?t=3195) · [0:54:05](https://youtu.be/BWEPtHZ1Y2Y?t=3245) "how to arrive at draw on liquidity step by step" · [0:56:43](https://youtu.be/BWEPtHZ1Y2Y?t=3403) why setups are reliable only if you know why price should go there

Everyone who sees resistance there puts their stop just above.

=== [2026 #23 — NQ High Of Day Short Review (ID P8Pn8DHFSW8)] — Relative Equal Highs (aliases: REH, relative equal lows)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** When price makes two (or more) highs at approximately the same level. This level represents buy-side liquidity above, which traders will target. If price pokes above and rejects, that's a short entry (turtle soup, false breakout).
- **How to identify:** Look for two highs within same session separated by at least one candlestick. Mark them on chart.
- **How to use:** Likely to trade up into REH; watch for wick or body rejection above. Short-entry zone if bodies fail to close above REH.
- **Example in this video:** Two highs in early morning (before 8:18 ET) at approximately same level. Buy side liquidity sits above. When price trades above late morning at ~10:20 ET, it pokes the REH level but doesn't close above it—short signal.
- **Timestamps:** [0:23:23](https://youtu.be/P8Pn8DHFSW8?t=1403) — identifying REH
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Don't expect it to be blown out without first showing rejection.

=== [2026 #28 — NQ Consolidation Day Algorithmic Rules In Action (ID 0olLwAiLa3o)] — Waiting for traps on a consolidation day; unfinished business at a shallow low  (aliases: soft bias, traps)
- **Kind:** rule / example
- **What ICT says:**
  - The run down to the Aug 6 low (29,241.25) was so shallow that it is a reasonable draw to be revisited: it may have "unfinished business, wanting to go a little bit deeper". But "even though the market's likely to consolidate, we wait for traps."
  - What a trap looks like here: an inefficiency above (a SIBI), the market rallies into premium to take highs (relative equal highs) and run buy stops/shorts' stops, drawing breakout buyers in (their stops sit right below the low), then breaks lower.
  - He holds a "soft bias" for lower prices because of the unfinished business below, while expecting consolidation; he would prefer the Aug 6 low be taken "more convincingly" but says "today's just not a day that you want to be pushing it".
  - Mechanics of the day: after the 9:30 open, price rallied to premium, gave indication that order flow was not continuing higher, broke lower, and consolidated around the 9:30 opening price.
- **Timestamps:** [0:08:00](https://youtu.be/0olLwAiLa3o?t=480) wait for traps · [0:08:48](https://youtu.be/0olLwAiLa3o?t=528) unfinished business at 29,241.25 · [0:13:37](https://youtu.be/0olLwAiLa3o?t=817) not a day to push it

=== [2026 #30 — Trade Management & Removing The Need To Be Right (ID F8s_vfK9HaM)] — Posted levels create liquidity (influence)  (aliases ICT uses: "pool of liquidity to be sacrificed")
- **Kind:** new coinage / commentary
- **What ICT says:** ICT claims that because so many people watch the levels he posts, those levels become a large pool of liquidity that others can run. People who act on his levels and lose faith in him are the ones being targeted. He compares it to seeing a wallet across the street on the way to a destination: price may take the easy liquidity first and then continue. He says this is "not ego, just market dynamics". He sees "not organic" price action around his levels.
- **How to use:** for the student, the point is to expect that a widely shared level may be run first. That is why stops and partial management matter, and why you should have your own analysis.
- **Timestamps:** [0:31:53](https://youtu.be/F8s_vfK9HaM?t=1913), [0:33:30](https://youtu.be/F8s_vfK9HaM?t=2010), [0:34:20](https://youtu.be/F8s_vfK9HaM?t=2060)
- **Nuance / caveats:** this is opinion; treat it as context, not a testable rule.

=== [2026 #32 — The Week In The Life Cycle Of Price (ID dmHSwlmS9iY)] — Weekly bias: proximity to previous week/month high vs. low  (aliases: "path of least resistance", "draw on liquidity")
- **Kind:** rule / entry model (bias method)
- **What ICT says:**
  - After the weekly close, ask where price opens relative to the previous week's high/previous month's high and previous week's low/previous month's low. Price is more likely to go to the nearer, easier side. He opens the week "right here", far closer to the previous week and previous month highs than the lows, so those highs are the likely draw. This is "the path of least resistance".
  - He adds the primary trend: he sees a primary bullish market ("propped up"), so he takes the bullish order flow at face value until it is proven otherwise.
  - Even if the market later crashes, he says he does not know what it will do. He waits to see where it opens on Sunday, lets Sunday trade, and watches the opening range on Monday.
  - A modest range (previous week high/low) is a big deal when you do it every week, he says in answer to people calling it small.
  - His weekend routine: right after the Friday close he does the analysis on a notepad (sometimes waits until the family is asleep, or does it early Saturday morning) and shares what is on that pad.
- **How to identify:** open (Sunday/Monday) is much closer to one of the previous-week or previous-month extremes, with the primary trend pointing the same way.
- **How to use:** set the weekly draw on the nearer liquidity pool, look for PD arrays that support it (below), and expect him to be indifferent to the far side.
- **Example in this video:** the week of 10–14 Aug 2026 in NQ: open was near the previous week's high and previous month's (July) high; the draw on liquidity was higher.
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:41](https://youtu.be/dmHSwlmS9iY?t=461) — "where is it likelier to go if we open here"; [0:08:27](https://youtu.be/dmHSwlmS9iY?t=507) — path of least resistance; [0:12:27](https://youtu.be/dmHSwlmS9iY?t=747)
- **Nuance / caveats:** he insists he does not predict the exact number; he names the level he is interested in.

=== [2026 #32 — The Week In The Life Cycle Of Price (ID dmHSwlmS9iY)] — Liquidity hierarchy: which highs/lows matter  (aliases: static vs dynamic ranges, session highs/lows, equilibrium)
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Pools to track: Asian session high/low, London session high/low, NY AM session high/low, the 2-hour NY lunch window high/low (he cites a 2-hour window), previous day's high/low, the last three days' high/low (this one is a dynamic range that continuously changes), previous week's high/low, previous month's high/low.
  - Static levels are session-oriented highs/lows plus previous day/week/month. The last-three-day range is dynamic.
  - Always note the midpoint (equilibrium) of each range.
  - The higher the time frame of the level, the more likely price will draw to it, because a daily-level old high carries a lot of institutional sponsorship between price and that level ("rocket fuel").
  - He checks his notes, e.g. "previous Monday's Asian session buy side liquidity pool", to make sure he has the right level.
  - Using a 15-minute chart for the week: he calls the 15-minute a "bellwether" time frame; it shows the whole week and every session level.
- **Example in this video:** the 29,984 level (relative equal highs) was Monday's London session buy-side liquidity; Monday's NY AM session buy-side liquidity and Tuesday's AM session buy-side liquidity were other pools he named for the Wednesday–Thursday run.
- **Timestamps:** [0:26:57](https://youtu.be/dmHSwlmS9iY?t=1617), [0:28:35](https://youtu.be/dmHSwlmS9iY?t=1715), [0:31:50](https://youtu.be/dmHSwlmS9iY?t=1910), [0:32:38](https://youtu.be/dmHSwlmS9iY?t=1958)
- **Nuance / caveats:** he says there is a private-mentorship set of lectures (months 6–12) that map algorithmic moves. He asks viewers to make their own notes. In the transcript he mixes up one level and corrects it (see Process section).

=== [2026 #32 — The Week In The Life Cycle Of Price (ID dmHSwlmS9iY)] — Relative equal highs/lows: when to expect they will be taken or ignored  (aliases: "guarded buy side", "chaff")
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Relative equal highs/lows are not automatically targets and not automatically shorts. He says many students short into relative equal highs and get run.
  - Early in the week, he named the relative equal highs at 29,984 (Monday London buy-side) as his interest and the relative equal lows below as a possible sweep, with nothing lower named. Price swept the lows while remaining bullish and without reaching the daily BISI. He treated that as strong bullish order flow.
  - On the Friday: with relative equal highs just above, he wants evidence they will be guarded. He uses the macro 9:50–10:10 and the first-hour dealing range / close of the opening range (30 minutes long) to decide. If buy-side is being "guarded" during the macro, you will not get your best fill; price might still take those highs, sweep, then go lower.
  - He prefers not to take on losses and wait for a clean read rather than "live through the losses".
- **How to use:** don't short into relative equal highs until bodies show failure (see Rejection Block and iFVG below), and don't "get precise" early. If you are not sure the pool is guarded, wait one more candle.
- **Timestamps:** [0:29:23](https://youtu.be/dmHSwlmS9iY?t=1763), [1:04:37](https://youtu.be/dmHSwlmS9iY?t=3877), [1:05:24](https://youtu.be/dmHSwlmS9iY?t=3924), [1:13:26](https://youtu.be/dmHSwlmS9iY?t=4406)
- **Nuance / caveats:** the later section (1:19:01) says the relative equal highs "are not going to get taken out if the things I explained here are true", i.e. inversion FVG plus rejection block plus bodies below.

=== [2026 #33 — PPI Market Review August 13, 2026 (ID RVHX56v-gUo)] — Relative Equal Highs as Resistance in Daily Context
- **Kind:** definition + example
- **What ICT says:** When the daily chart shows two or more highs at approximately the same level, that's a relative equal high. It's a zone where momentum historically failed, so price must "bust through" it with significant follow-through to make a new high. If price just pokes above and closes back down, it's a false breakout. Clearing it cleanly (with wicks/bodies above and no rejection) suggests continuation higher.
- **How to identify:** Zoom out to daily chart; mark the last two swing highs. Are they within ~50–100 points? If yes, that's a REH zone.
- **How to use:** Resistance. On a break above, add longs or short-cover confirmation. On a rejection, short into the level. If body closes above and away, it's bullish breakout signal.
- **Example in this video:** NQ daily shows relative equal highs at 30,094. Price needs to break above this to reach Wednesday's high (30,600 area). Once it clears 30,094 cleanly, the path to Wednesday's high opens up. ICT calls 30,600 and 30,975 as additional targets after breaking REH.
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:14](https://youtu.be/RVHX56v-gUo?t=434) — wick measurement; [0:09:42](https://youtu.be/RVHX56v-gUo?t=582) — identifying REH on daily; [0:10:29](https://youtu.be/RVHX56v-gUo?t=629) — projecting targets past REH
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** REH is most powerful on daily; less so on lower timeframes. Once cleared, don't expect price to come back into it easily.

=== [2026 #35 — ES Day Trade Review With 1min & 15Sec Timeframe (ID KEqzv2yOAf0)] — Relative Equal Lows and Entry Patience
- **Kind:** definition + rule + example
- **What ICT says:** When a market makes two or more lows at the same (or very close) price level, that's a relative equal low. It represents sell-side liquidity below the market, which will be targeted on any decline. More importantly, the sell-side liquidity pool *resting just below the RELs* is the true target; you don't need to hit the exact low—just get close enough to run the liquidity.
- **How to identify:** Look at prior swings (same-day or previous days); mark any two lows at approximately same level.
- **How to use:** Short target. Place limit orders just above the REL (to avoid hitting the exact low and getting stopped out). If you hit the REL midpoint, that's success.
- **Example in this video:** ES shows "perfect" relative equal lows from Friday's London session. ICT places limit orders just *above* that level to capture the sell-side liquidity just below it, exiting before the market reaches the absolute low.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:47](https://youtu.be/KEqzv2yOAf0?t=47) — identifying RELs; [0:01:34](https://youtu.be/KEqzv2yOAf0?t=94) — entry/exit planning; [0:03:57](https://youtu.be/KEqzv2yOAf0?t=237) — actual fill at planned level
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Don't be greedy and wait for the exact low; capture the liquidity pool just above it.

=== [2026 #36 — AM Session Review & Course Correction (ID 0Bu4g-2aMA4)] — Relative Equal Highs and Lows as Liquidity Pools
- **Kind:** definition + rule + example
- **What ICT says:** (Refresher) When two or more highs (or lows) form at the same level, that's a relative equal high/low. Each represents a pool of liquidity (buy-side above a high, sell-side below a low). The market will target these liquidity pools. If it initially fails to reach them, that failure is itself a signal—it means that direction isn't being favored; look for a reversal instead.
- **How to identify:** Mark all swing highs/lows from the current session and prior days; identify any at approximately the same price level.
- **How to use:** Bias and target planning. If trying to go long, expect it to reach the relative equal high. If it fails before getting there, flip the bias to short and target the relative equal lows. Conversely for shorts.
- **Example in this video:** NQ opens up; ICT expects it to run to relative equal highs. Price immediately sends it lower instead (failure of his bias). He pivots: now expects a run to relative equal lows, which it hits by midday. Multiple REL zones are marked: confirms the eventual target.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:02](https://youtu.be/0Bu4g-2aMA4?t=2) — initial setup; [0:00:54](https://youtu.be/0Bu4g-2aMA4?t=54) — RELs identified; [0:06:36](https://youtu.ye/0Bu4g-2aMA4?t=396) — final RELs marked and expected next move
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** If initial move fails before hitting a level, don't force it—wait for the market to deliver the alternate setup.

- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** the lows he trusts are chosen from session highs and lows, daily highs and lows, the London lunch range high/low (caption: "London lunch"), the previous week's high/low, and the highest high / lowest low of the last three days. Friday's final-hour RTH low was one of those key liquidity pools. He says he has taught this "ad nauseam" and will not answer every repeated question.

- **Nuance / caveats:** this repeats the list from the "Week In The Life Cycle Of Price" lecture (L26-032).

=== [2026 #38 — Navigating High Resistance Liquidity Run Conditions (ID O217xQsEUgI)] — Targets: relative equal highs of the London session, and not "emptying the magazine"
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** the best case is a run to the overnight swing highs (Monday London session relative equal highs, buy-side liquidity). He did not want to empty the magazine on everything far below those highs, so he kept some contracts for them. === [2026 #53 — Trading In High Resistance Liquidity Run Conditions - July 21, 2026 (ID tp0qkiWCctw)] — Relative Equal Highs & Lows (Old Highs/Lows)
- **Kind:** rule · liquidity target
- **What ICT says:** A previous swing high or low from an earlier session that is retested at roughly the same price level. When price approaches these levels in a new session, they act as draw-on liquidity points (magnets) because market makers know traders want to break/clear those levels. Price often reverses near them after a retest, or uses them as "trap" zones where stops are run.
- **How to identify:** Chart previous 1-3 sessions' highs and lows; when price returns to those zones, note them as zones of interest.
- **How to use:** Use as bias confirmers: if price reaches and respects (reverses off) relative equal low, it's bearish; if price reaches and reverses off relative equal high, it's bullish. Use as stop-placement guides (put stops slightly above relative equal highs if shorting). Use as entry or exit targets.
- **Example in this video:** Relative equal lows marked around 0:02:02 and repeatedly referenced. Price reaches and respects them around 0:22:06–0:33:18, then revisits them around 0:49:02. ICT places orders to exit at these levels.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:02:02](https://youtu.be/tp0qkiWCctw?t=122) — Marking the target lows.
  - [0:15:03](https://youtu.be/tp0qkiWCctw?t=903) — "I'll take one off underneath those relative equal lows we were aiming at earlier."

=== [2026 #53 — Trading In High Resistance Liquidity Run Conditions - July 21, 2026 (ID tp0qkiWCctw)] — Sell-Side Liquidity (SSL)
- **Kind:** definition · liquidity concept
- **What ICT says:** Resting sell orders (limit orders waiting to be filled at higher prices) left behind when price falls sharply. Traders who longed near lows and want to exit at breakeven or profit place sell orders; when price returns up into that zone, those orders get hit. For a short trader, this is a drag on momentum upward—the zone resists upward movement.
- **How to identify:** Look at the 4H or 1H chart: find a recent sharp fall. The zone where the rise stopped (relative low before that fall) often contains SSL. Visually: bodies above a level but no bodies below (suggesting sellers waiting above, not below).
- **How to use:** As a short trader, use SSL zones as profit-taking zones or as "drag" zones where upward momentum stalls. Use as a pullback magnet if price is falling through SSL—price will pull back into it before resuming down.
- **Example in this video:** Around 0:04:40, ICT notes "sell side there" and uses it as a target for the short.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:04:40](https://youtu.be/tp0qkiWCctw?t=280) — "Next point of interest is this sell side here."

=== [2026 #53 — Trading In High Resistance Liquidity Run Conditions - July 21, 2026 (ID tp0qkiWCctw)] — PHIL (Market Maker / "Person Handling Institutional Liquidity")
- **Kind:** new coinage · narrative / teaching device
- **What ICT says:** Personification of the market maker—the entity controlling price and running stops. "PHIL" is shorthand for the person/algorithm that sets prices, runs liquidity, and manages the order flow. Used pedagogically: "If I were PHIL, I would..." ICT uses this to predict likely price actions because PHIL's job is to run stops and extract liquidity, not to move price smoothly.
- **How to use:** When analyzing a trade, ask "What would PHIL do here?" to predict stops that will be run or liquidity that will be drawn before the main move.
- **Example in this video:** Around 0:29:17, 0:34:54, 0:35:45, 0:37:45, 0:41:18—ICT repeatedly predicts what "PHIL" will do next, then watches it happen.
- **Timestamps:**

  - [0:34:54](https://youtu.be/tp0qkiWCctw?t=2094) — "They went right to my stop. I mean, look at this. Ran those relative equal highs out. Like I told you, if I was Phil, well, they're going to say you're Phil."

=== [2026 #54 — NQ Futures Trade Review - July 20, 2026 (ID 2L88yH3LKcs)] — Engineered Liquidity & Buy-Side / Sell-Side Distribution
- **Kind:** definition · market structure
- **What ICT says:** Market makers create the appearance of strength (or weakness) by distributing (or accumulating) positions at key levels. On a Judas swing (false breakout up), market makers will allow price to rip higher to reach old resistance levels, sell heavily into the move to retail traders who see "confirmation," then dump it lower. This is called "engineering buy-side liquidity"—creating the appearance of buying pressure to trap buyers. "Smart money can go long here, sell, and sell more to the engineered liquidity that it forms." Bodies stay out of key levels; wicks may touch, but bodies won't, signifying that the move is engineered redistribution, not organic buying.
- **How to identify:** (1) Price reaches an old high or resistance level (relative equal high, previous swing high, etc.). (2) Bodies cannot touch or cannot stay above a key level (like consequent encroachment), but wicks do. (3) Price fails to go higher despite reaching an old level. (4) The failure is followed by a sharp reversal.
- **How to use:** When you see engineered liquidity signatures (bodies avoiding a level, wicks touching but bodies above), expect a reversal. Sell into the engineered move (short at the engineered buy-side; long at engineered sell-side). This is a higher-probability trade because you're front-running the reversal.
- **Example in this video:** 9:30 opening: price runs up in Judas swing, wicks reach up to touch upper levels (consequent encroachment between the hourly low and high, octant levels), but bodies cannot touch them. This signals engineered buy-side distribution. ICT shorted this.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:12:17](https://youtu.be/2L88yH3LKcs?t=737) — "Bodies can't touch consequent encouragement breaks lower then it runs through these highs. This is engineered buy liquidity."
  - [0:17:57](https://youtu.be/2L88yH3LKcs?t=1077) — "Bodies are not touching consequent encouragement. The wick can, but the wicks are allowed to do damage. The narrative and volume is really shown in the bodies."

=== [2026 #57 — Midnight ET Principles In Relationship To PreMArket Session (ID KHkUPAsdyfk)] — After London relative equal lows and a 7:00 retake: don't expect a return to the midnight open; target the relative equal highs  (aliases ICT uses: short-covering rally, "clean up the relative equal highs")
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Price took the daily relative equal low after 7:00; then "massive short covering" (he jokes the whole internet had the same target) took price back above and cleaned up the relative equal highs.
  - The rally came "real short" of the midnight open and fell short.
  - Rule: whenever we go into London with relative equal lows, and right before 7:00 get that low taken once more, do not hold with the expectation of going back all the way to the opening price; it can, but many times it won't. Target the relative equal highs instead and take some profit there.
- **How to identify:** London relative equal lows -> sweep right before/at 7:00 -> reversal.
- **How to use:** after the sweep and reversal, target the relative equal highs (not the midnight open); bank partial profits; consider being done for the day.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 5m/1m Friday: sweep of the daily relative equal lows at the 7:00 acceleration; rally to the relative equal highs; stops just below the midnight open; sell-off again.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:05](https://youtu.be/KHkUPAsdyfk?t=245) — what I am teaching: don't hold for the open; [0:04:55](https://youtu.be/KHkUPAsdyfk?t=295) — take profit at the relative equal highs
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** A refinement of using the midnight open as a retracement target: it is a possibility, not a requirement.

=== [2026 #62 — Trading Complex Opening Ranges With Fed Impact 07/14/2026 (ID fFi2QEApVGM)] — Single-candle run below the 8:30 low  (aliases ICT uses: one single delivery, "40 seconds to do this", liquidity below the 8:30 lows)
