=== [2026 #66 — One Shot - One Kill Trade Setup Review (ID zq4hz_XiTRE)] — Buy-Side Liquidity and Sell-Side Liquidity (aliases: BSL, SSL, old high, old low, relative equal liquidity pools)
- **Kind:** definition + rule
- **What ICT says:** Buy-side liquidity is resting above a recent high (where buy orders sit unfilled during rallies; smart money looks to take them out with a spike, then reverse lower). Sell-side liquidity is below a recent low (where sell orders sit; smart money spikes lower to take them, then reverses higher). These are the pools of resting orders that institutional order flow "takes out" or sweeps. When you see a spike to a level and then a reversal, that level was likely liquidity being targeted.
- **How to identify:** Mark recent swing highs and lows on your 15m or daily; label them as BSL and SSL respectively.
- **How to use:** Use them as targets. When you're short, your target is often an SSL (take out the sell-side, then reverse). When you're long, target BSL. Smart money adds to positions when these are being swept. When all BSL is taken and price reverses down, expect a continuation of the downtrend.
- **Example in this video:** Monday's relative equal highs contained buy-side liquidity; price spiked to take it, and Michael announced beforehand he expected this. After BSL was taken, price rolled over and delivered down into discount wick.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:03](https://youtu.be/zq4hz_XiTRE?t=243) (BSL resting above old high) · [0:05:40](https://youtu.be/zq4hz_XiTRE?t=340) (BSL taken, then expected to work toward SSL) · [0:10:26](https://youtu.be/zq4hz_XiTRE?t=626) (smart money accumulates shorts when BSL taken, SSL defined).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Once BSL/SSL is taken, the direction flips; don't expect another reversal back to it in that move.

=== [2026 #66 — One Shot - One Kill Trade Setup Review (ID zq4hz_XiTRE)] — Relative Equal Highs / Relative Equal Lows (aliases: REH, REL, equal levels, relative equal liquidity)
- **Kind:** definition + rule
- **What ICT says:** Relative equal highs are two or more swing highs at approximately the same price level. Same for lows. These represent a "pool of liquidity"—likely where many stop losses or limit orders cluster. When price approaches a REH/REL, it often pauses, reverses, or sweeps through. These are NOT support/resistance; they're institutional reference points.
- **How to identify:** Scan backward for the last 5–20 candles on your 15m or 1m; find two or more local highs or lows within 5–10 ticks of each other.
- **How to use:** Use as targets on reversals. If you're short and price is near a REL, that's a likely target (where the sweep finishes before a reversal up). If long, REH is your target.
- **Example in this video:** Monday lunch macro setup formed relative equal highs; smart money hammered them (took BSL), then reversed down. Later, new RELs formed below the old swing low, and price targeted those as well.
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:15](https://youtu.be/zq4hz_XiTRE?t=435) (REL as target: relative equal lows) · [0:12:01](https://youtu.be/zq4hz_XiTRE?t=721) (REH during lunch macro) · [0:13:37](https://youtu.be/zq4hz_XiTRE?t=817) (REL based on old pool of liquidity, algorithm likely to draw down into it).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** RELs can be formed on new data (e.g., a current low at same level as an older low), creating a fresh institutional reference point.

=== [2026 #67 — Analysis Delivering On NQ (ID N3qz13Hl-gg)] — Draw on That Low / Draw to Liquidity (aliases: draw, target, confluence draw)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** When price is moving toward a lower timeframe structure (like a 1m inefficiency) that is nested within a higher timeframe (15m) draw, smart money is "drawing" price toward that liquidity or inefficiency. The institutional algorithm pulls price toward these nested draws. If price "wilts" (weakens) into a draw, it's confirming continuation; if it bounces before reaching the draw, the move is questioned.
- **How to identify:** Identify inefficiencies on both 15m and 1m; note when a lower-timeframe inefficiency sits within a higher-timeframe target. That's a draw.
- **How to use:** Use draws as confirmation. If price is moving toward a draw and still showing strength (wicking around efficiently, bodies committed), it's likely to reach it. If weakening before reaching, question the move.
- **Example in this video:** Michael identified a 15m draw (sell-side inefficiency) and a nested 1m inefficiency within it. Price was drawing toward both; the move was so clean it "wilted" exactly into the target inefficiency.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:49](https://youtu.be/N3qz13Hl-gg?t=49) (draw = target below) · [0:07:07](https://youtu.be/N3qz13Hl-gg?t=427) (any movement lower should find momentum build up to get down into that inefficiency) · [0:12:07](https://youtu.be/N3qz13Hl-gg?t=727) (draw on that low).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Draws aren't random; they're institutional algos targeting nested inefficiencies. Precision comes from nesting timeframes.

=== [2026 #68 — Post US Holiday Monday Followup (ID 2bke4YH6ZuE)] — Draw on Liquidity (aliases: draw, liquidity draw, institutional target)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** When price action points toward a specific inefficiency or liquidity pool, smart money is "drawing" price toward it. This is the institutional algorithm doing its work. If price is moving toward a draw and showing no weakness, expect it to complete the draw. If it's weakening before reaching, the draw is questioned. A draw is characterized by sustained directional movement with clean structure (no choppy wicks or reversals).
- **How to identify:** Identify an inefficiency that price is moving toward; watch for commitment (bodies aligned, wicks controlled). If commitment is strong, you're watching a draw.
- **How to use:** Don't fight a draw; if price is drawing to a level, position trades in that direction. Use stops above (for shorts) or below (for longs) the structure showing the draw. Draws often complete cleanly.
- **Example in this video:** Michael was watching for a potential draw on liquidity lower in the evening/early Asian session after the Monday close. If price could "gravitate down towards that" and set up structure implying a continued draw, he'd engage.
- **Timestamps:** [0:08:08](https://youtu.be/2bke4YH6ZuE?t=488) (potential draw on liquidity lower) · [0:08:55](https://youtu.be/2bke4YH6ZuE?t=535) (using event horizon and draw logic).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Draws are directional; they're institutional moves, not retail chop. Recognize the difference between chop (rangebound wicks) and draws (committed bodies).

=== [2026 #68 — Post US Holiday Monday Followup (ID 2bke4YH6ZuE)] — Minor Buy-Side and Minor Sell-Side Liquidity (aliases: minor buy-side, minor sell-side, internal liquidity pools)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Within a dealing range, there are inefficiencies at the edges (extreme/major) and inefficiencies inside the range (minor). Minor buy-side liquidity is an imbalance where buyers are resting in the middle of a range (not at the top). Minor sell-side is the same but for sellers. These are "secondary" liquidity pools that may be swept en route to the major (extreme) pools.
- **How to identify:** Identify the dealing range high and low; scan inside for smaller gaps or imbalances; those are minor zones.
- **How to use:** Use minor liquidity pools as intermediate targets. If you're short and moving lower, expect price to touch the minor sell-side on the way down to the extreme sell-side. These are bounce points or partial-profit zones.
- **Example in this video:** Michael identified minor buy-side and minor sell-side within the 15m dealing range; price took out the minor buy-side in the morning, then failed to push higher and eventually worked toward the minor sell-side and extreme sell-side lower.
- **Timestamps:** [0:16:13](https://youtu.be/2bke4YH6ZuE?t=973) (minor buy side and minor sell side inside the dealing range).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Minor zones are secondary targets; don't mistake them for major support/resistance.

=== [2026 #73 — Futures Commentary & Motivational Lecture \ June 24, 2026 (ID hxdAOfcp6gE)] — Previous day's range retracement rule  (aliases: "how much of the previous day's range", "lower half of the previous day's range")
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - Look at previous day's high/low/range and grade the sensitivity around it: how much of the previous day's range is price retracing? When the market is bearish, it should retrace only into the lower half of the previous day's range. If it does retrace up to the half, expect an afternoon or late-session continuation or the next day; you may have to cut a losing day-trade and look for the downside to resume.
  - Example (EURUSD): price retraced into the lower half and sold off; on another day it did not even reach the half, which tells him "algorithmically" the market is bearish; on a day that opened where it closed he used the wick instead.
  - He taught this in his paid mentorships (2016–2021) and says it underlies many of his executions.
- **How to identify:** plot a fib on the previous day's high to low; mark 50%.
- **How to use:** retrace into the lower half = bearish continuation expected; a trade into the half = caution/resumption later.
- **Timestamps:** [1:05:27](https://youtu.be/hxdAOfcp6gE?t=3927); [1:06:16](https://youtu.be/hxdAOfcp6gE?t=3976); [1:07:50](https://youtu.be/hxdAOfcp6gE?t=4070).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** he states only the bearish case; the bullish mirror (retrace only into the upper half) is implied, not stated.

=== [2026 #73 — Futures Commentary & Motivational Lecture \ June 24, 2026 (ID hxdAOfcp6gE)] — Draw on liquidity as expectation, not a requirement  (aliases: "drawing liquidity", "reasonable expectation")
- **Kind:** rule / psychology
- **What ICT says:** Draw on liquidity is the premise that builds the idea before the trade; price does not have to reach it. "If you're going to be long, you have to know where it's going to reach to." Without that you are trading blind, which is gambling. If you are new and lack conviction, taking a profit before the target is fine; if wrong, a hypothetical trailing stop below the low gets hit and you "accept it, number one" because it is one transaction. He compares these small, partial wins to earning belts in martial arts: they keep a beginner motivated and teachable.
- **Example in this video:** DXY run into 101.977 target; price near the target by this date.
- **Timestamps:** [0:52:10](https://youtu.be/hxdAOfcp6gE?t=3130); [0:58:26](https://youtu.be/hxdAOfcp6gE?t=3506); [0:59:12](https://youtu.be/hxdAOfcp6gE?t=3552).

=== [2026 #75 — Futures Market Commentary \ June 18, 2026 (ID bUN_f8U_1Eg)] — Draw on Liquidity (aliases: draw, short-term draw, best-case target, near-term draw)
- **Kind:** rule + targeting
- **What ICT says:** A draw is when price moves toward an identified inefficiency or liquidity pool with institutional conviction (sustained move, clean structure). Michael identifies two draws per setup: near-term draw (lower-probability, closer target) and best-case scenario (higher-probability if momentum continues, further away). Use these to define targets and partial profit zones.
- **How to identify:** Identify inefficiencies at multiple levels; the nearest one is near-term draw; the furthest is best case.
- **How to use:** Set targets at draws. If price is moving toward a draw and showing strength, expect it to reach the draw. Use near-term as first target; best case as runner target.
- **Example in this video:** Euro Dollar: near-term draw was a minor sell-side liquidity pool; best-case scenario was a deeper inefficiency. Michael would take partial at near-term, hold runner to best case.
- **Timestamps:** [0:03:13](https://youtu.be/bUN_f8U_1Eg?t=193) (best-case scenario short-term, near-term draw; minor sell-side liquidity pool) · [0:06:24](https://youtu.be/bUN_f8U_1Eg?t=384) (draw down here; idea to share, not hard and fast).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Draws don't always complete in the timeframe you expect; they may take days or require additional structure to materialize.

=== [2026 #75 — Futures Market Commentary \ June 18, 2026 (ID bUN_f8U_1Eg)] — Minor Sell-Side Liquidity Pool (aliases: minor SSL, secondary target)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Within a daily range or between major inefficiencies, there may be a smaller pool of sell-side liquidity (resting sell orders at an intermediate level). This is a minor target—a bounce point or partial profit zone, not the ultimate target. Use it as a first-take profit zone; then run the rest toward best-case (the major inefficiency).
- **How to identify:** Scan the daily chart between the opening range and a lower major inefficiency; if there's a swing high or clustering of small inefficiencies, that's a minor SSL.
- **How to use:** Treat minor SSL as your first target for shorts. Take bulk of position here; hold runners for best case. If minor SSL is taken out, expect a continuation to major inefficiency.
- **Example in this video:** Euro Dollar had a minor sell-side liquidity pool at an intermediate level; Michael noted "That would be enough for me if I was a bearish forex trader" to take considerable profit there.
- **Timestamps:** [0:04:02](https://youtu.be/bUN_f8U_1Eg?t=242) (minor sell-side liquidity pool; take considerable profit there).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Minor zones are stepping stones; they're not final targets, just profitable exit points.

=== [2026 #75 — Futures Market Commentary \ June 18, 2026 (ID bUN_f8U_1Eg)] — Relative Equal Lows (aliases: REL, equal lows, old low reference)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Two or more swing lows at approximately the same price level. These are institutional reference points—clusters of stop losses and limit orders. When price breaks below RELs, it signals weakness; the sweep is a "liquidity grab" before continuation.
- **How to identify:** Scan recent price action (5–20 candles back on daily); find two or more lows within a few ticks of each other.
- **How to use:** RELs are targets for shorts. When you're short and price approaches a REL, expect a test or sweep. Use as a partial-profit zone or a confirmation of directional continuation.
- **Example in this video:** Crude Oil had relative equal lows; price broke below them with a displacement, signaling institutional participation and lower targets ahead.
- **Timestamps:** [0:06:24](https://youtu.be/bUN_f8U_1Eg?t=384) (strong displacement below relative equal lows).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** RELs are liquidity clusters; sweeps through them often accelerate the move.

=== [2026 #78 — Futures Review & Commentary \ June 07, 2026 (ID UTrm6uCOUZo)] — Stop Hunt / Stop Loss Sweep (aliases: stop hunt, liquidity grab, spike and reverse)
- **Kind:** institutional pattern
- **What ICT says:** A stop hunt is when institutional traders spike price to sweep stops (take out stop losses) and then reverse. Example: Crude oil spike higher to take out sell-side stops, then reverse down. This is predatory order flow targeting retail stop losses. Recognize it and don't get caught.
- **How to identify:** Watch for a sharp spike or trend reversal that seems unmotivated by news, followed by a quick reversal (often at the market open or during thin liquidity).
- **How to use:** When you spot a stop hunt, don't chase the spike. Wait for the reversal and trade in the opposite direction.
- **Example in this video:** Crude oil: "to me, I you know, I I I suspect that crude oil's going to go higher. Nothing's really changed over in the Middle East... But this market's going to be affected greatly because of that." Michael suspects crude will be manipulated (stop hunted) and warns to stay away.
- **Timestamps:** [0:05:32](https://youtu.be/UTrm6uCOUZo?t=332) (this was a stop hunt).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Stop hunts are often news-driven (Judas swings); use order flow, not headlines.

=== [2026 #78 — Futures Review & Commentary \ June 07, 2026 (ID UTrm6uCOUZo)] — Relative Equal Lows (aliases: REL, equal lows, liquidity pool)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Two or more lows at the same price level within a range or across multiple days. These are pools of sell-side liquidity (resting sell orders). When price breaks below RELs and shows commitment (bodies stay down), expect lower prices.
- **How to identify:** Scan the daily chart backward; find two or more local lows within a few ticks of each other.
- **How to use:** RELs are targets and stop-out points. Price often tests and breaks through them cleanly. Use as partial-profit zones for shorts.
- **Example in this video:** Euro Dollar, Pound Dollar, ES, NQ: all had relative equal lows marked as targets for lower prices.
- **Timestamps:** [0:02:23](https://youtu.be/UTrm6uCOUZo?t=143) (relative equal lows where sell-side resides) · [0:12:38](https://youtu.be/UTrm6uCOUZo?t=758) (relatively equal lows on ES).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** RELs are clusters of institutional sell orders; they're sweeps that accelerate moves down.

=== [2026 #78 — Futures Review & Commentary \ June 07, 2026 (ID UTrm6uCOUZo)] — Sell-Side Liquidity Pool and Minor Sell-Side (aliases: SSL, minor SSL, secondary pool)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** A sell-side liquidity pool is a zone where sell orders cluster (above/below a level). Minor sell-side is an intermediate pool; major sell-side is the deepest or furthest away. Use minor sell-side for first targets; major for best-case targets.
- **How to identify:** Identify inefficiencies at different price levels; mark each as minor (closer) or major (further away).
- **How to use:** Use minor SSL for partial profit; major SSL for runner targets. If price sweeps minor SSL, expect continuation to major SSL.
- **Example in this video:** Multiple markets had identified minor and major sell-side pools: Euro, Pound, Gold, Silver, etc.
- **Timestamps:** [0:01:36](https://youtu.be/UTrm6uCOUZo?t=96) (relative equal lows where sell-side resides) · [0:03:57](https://youtu.be/UTrm6uCOUZo?t=237) (minor sell-side and more prominent sell-side liquidity).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Minor/major classifications depend on proximity; closer inefficiency = minor.

=== [2026 #79 — NQ Futures Review & Commentary \ June 03, 2026 (ID 7M-AO01cAZ8)] — Relative Equal Lows and Relative Equal Highs (aliases: REL, REH, equal highs/lows)
- **Kind:** definition + targeting
- **What ICT says:** REL = two+ lows at same price; acts as a sell-side liquidity cluster (support that's also a target for shorts). REH = two+ highs at same price; acts as a buy-side liquidity cluster (resistance). These are institutional reference points.
- **How to identify:** Scan the daily chart; find two or more equal highs or lows within a few ticks.
- **How to use:** Use REL as partial target for shorts; REH as partial target for longs. These are stepping stones, not final targets.
- **Example in this video:** NQ: "target here was the relative equal lows." Michael exited into REL.
- **Timestamps:** [0:02:25](https://youtu.be/7M-AO01cAZ8?t=145) (takes out relative equal highs) · [0:05:36](https://youtu.be/7M-AO01cAZ8?t=336) (target: relative equal lows).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** REL/REH are liquidity clusters; they're profitable exits, not final targets.

=== [2026 #80 — NQ Futures Review & Commentary \ June 02, 2026 (ID bqmqylAuLcI)] — Dead Time (5:00 AM – 6:00 AM ET) and 6:00 AM Start Time (aliases: dead zone, low-liquidity time)
- **Kind:** timing rule
- **What ICT says:** Between 5:00 AM and 6:00 AM ET (after London close, before pre-session officially starts), price action is low-probability. Price runs can occur, but they're not to be trusted. 6:00 AM is the earliest that higher-probability price runs can materialize. Michael prefers to wait for 6:00 AM before looking for scalps.
- **How to identify:** Mark 5:00 AM and 6:00 AM on your chart; avoid trading 5:00–6:00; watch for setups forming at 6:00+.
- **How to use:** Use 5:00–6:00 AM for observation and setup identification, not execution. At 6:00 AM, if a setup is forming, you can enter with higher conviction.
- **Example in this video:** Michael waited until 6:00 AM to start watching for the price run. He notes: "6:00 a.m. is the earliest that the higher probability price runs can be trusted."
- **Timestamps:** [0:01:36](https://youtu.be/bqmqylAuLcI?t=96) (dead time 5:00-6:00 AM; 6:00 AM start; 6:00-7:00 AM window; 7:00-9:00 pre-session).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Pre-market before 6:00 AM is thin; avoid it unless you have a specific edge.

=== [2026 #80 — NQ Futures Review & Commentary \ June 02, 2026 (ID bqmqylAuLcI)] — Relative Equal Highs (aliases: REH, equal highs, liquidity cluster)
- **Kind:** definition + reference
- **What ICT says:** Two or more highs at the same price level. These form pools of buy-side liquidity (limit orders resting above). When price approaches REH from below, expect a test or break above. Once taken, expect pullback or reversal.
- **How to identify:** Scan recent price action (especially overnight/London session); find two+ highs within a few ticks of each other.
- **How to use:** Use REH as targets for long trades or as stop-out points for shorts. Once REH is taken (break above), prepare for volatility and potential reversal.
- **Example in this video:** "Relative equal highs formed during the London session." Later: "relative equal highs, be mindful that they'll probably utilize that as a next draw on liquidity." Michael uses them as directional markers.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:48](https://youtu.be/bqmqylAuLcI?t=48) (REH formed London session) · [0:07:55](https://youtu.be/bqmqylAuLcI?t=475) (REH as next draw on liquidity).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** REH is a two-way level; it's both a target and a reversal point depending on direction.

=== [2026 #80 — NQ Futures Review & Commentary \ June 02, 2026 (ID bqmqylAuLcI)] — Buy-Side and Sell-Side Liquidity Sweeps (aliases: sweep sequence, double-sweep, buy then sell)
- **Kind:** pattern + execution framework
- **What ICT says:** When price sweeps buy-side liquidity first, then sell-side second, it's a "buy side taken, sell side taken" sequence. This double-sweep creates inversion fair value gaps and validates the directional move. Smart money uses this sequence to take out both retail longs (at buy-side highs) and retail shorts (at sell-side lows) before executing the true directional move.
- **How to identify:** Watch for price spike above a level (buy-side swept), then pullback and spike below (sell-side swept). Each sweep creates an inefficiency/gap.
- **How to use:** After buy-side is taken and pull back occurs, watch for the sell-side to be taken next. That second sweep validates the direction.
- **Example in this video:** "Convenient how it ran up above taking that buy side. We broke down traded down into that low there clearing out that area. So buy side taken first then sell side."
- **Timestamps:** [0:02:23](https://youtu.be/bqmqylAuLcI?t=143) (buy side taken, then sell side swept; sequence).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The sequence is key: buy-side first validates the algorithm is "running" that direction.

=== [2026 #82 — NQ Futures Review & Commentary \ May 28, 2026 (ID NS5EcfChL3o)] — Two Pools of Liquidity and Range Grading (aliases: dual-level liquidity, high and low pools)
- **Kind:** framework / measurement
- **What ICT says:** To define a trade zone, identify two pools of liquidity: a higher pool (upper level) and a lower pool (lower level). Grade these two levels; the range between them is your operational zone. Use this range to identify equilibrium (midpoint), premium side (above midpoint), and discount side (below midpoint). Shorts are taken on the premium side; longs on discount. This framework predicts where price will consolidate and where it will break.
- **How to identify:** Scan the daily/15m chart; find two significant support/resistance zones (old lows, inversion FVGs, swing points); mark them as high and low pools.
- **How to use:** Use the range to frame your trading zone. Target breaks below the lower pool or above the higher pool for true directional moves.
- **Example in this video:** Michael identified two pools: 30,055.75 (high) and 29,886 (low). The range between them (169.75 points) was the zone. Equilibrium = 30,055.75 - 29,886 = ~29,970. Above equilibrium = premium (short zone); below = discount (long zone).
- **Timestamps:** [0:05:32](https://youtu.be/NS5EcfChL3o?t=332) (two pools of liquidity, grading) · [0:10:33](https://youtu.be/NS5EcfChL3o?t=633) (equilibrium between two pools; 30,055.75 and 29,886).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** This framework is institutional; price oscillates between pools, and breaks predict the next move.

=== [2026 #82 — NQ Futures Review & Commentary \ May 28, 2026 (ID NS5EcfChL3o)] — Stop Hunt (aliases: stop loss sweep, false reversal)
- **Kind:** institutional pattern
- **What ICT says:** A stop hunt is when price spikes in one direction to sweep stop losses (rout out traders), then reverses. Example: price runs up, triggers sell-side stops, then drops. Michael notes: "It was a stop hunt. It runs low." Recognize these and don't get shaken out; they're opportunities to add or re-enter.
- **How to identify:** Watch for a spike that wicks above/below a level, then immediately reverses without closing in that direction.
- **How to use:** When you spot a stop hunt, prepare to trade in the direction AFTER the hunt (when price resumes). Don't panic-exit; the hunt is often followed by the true directional move.
- **Example in this video:** Price ran up, triggered stops above a high, then dropped sharply. The drop was the true move.
- **Timestamps:** [0:08:56](https://youtu.be/NS5EcfChL3o?t=536) (stop hunt: runs low, took out short-term high, it was a stop hunt).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Stop hunts are predatory institutional tactics; recognize them and turn them to your advantage.

=== [2026 #83 — NQ Futures Review & Commentary \ May 27, 2026 (ID qf5qokimwO4)] — Relative Equal Lows
- **Kind:** definition + use
- **What ICT says:** When the market creates a low, then retraces, and later creates another low near the same price, that level becomes a magnet on price. Market makers target these for liquidity runs and stop sweeps. On daily charts, relative equal lows are key resistance to the downside in bear moves.
- **How to identify:** Look at prior swing lows; if a new low is within ~5–10 points (on NQ), it qualifies as a relative equal low.
- **How to use:** In bull markets, target relative equal highs (upside). In bear markets, target relative equal lows (downside). These are "universal" targets; accumulate shorts when aimed at them; use them as targets on short entries.
- **Example in this video:** NQ daily, May 27, 2026. "Relatively equal lows" from prior month to current level were discussed as magnet on price for potential downside draw.
- **Timestamps:** [0:03:27](https://youtu.be/qf5qokimwO4?t=207) — relative equal lows taken out in May · [0:40:29](https://youtu.be/qf5qokimwO4?t=2429) — planning entry near relative equal lows
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** ICT uses this as a universal rule for all markets; applies to equities, forex, futures.

=== [2026 #84 — Oil Review & General Commentary (ID dwMgP2zkH0w)] — Relative Equal Lows (in crude oil)
- **Kind:** example only
- **What ICT says:** Crude oil had previous swing lows; when price creates another low near the same level, that becomes a magnet and target. ICT had shared this observation on X the prior day and was aiming for price to drop below the lower of those equal lows.
- **How to identify:** Mark prior swing lows on the chart; when a new low is near them within a small range, it qualifies.
- **How to use:** Target the lower of the two equal lows for a down move; use price respecting or breaking below this level as a sell signal.

=== [2026 #85 — Tapereading \ Practice Session Final Hour ES (ID 7zlblhLGraA)] — Relative Equal Highs as Targets
- **Kind:** example only
- **What ICT says:** Throughout the day, price creates multiple highs at similar levels (or with small wicks near the same level). These become targets on up moves. Use relative equal highs as accumulation and scaling points.
- **How to identify:** Mark intraday swing highs; if multiple candles top out near the same price, that level is a relative equal high.
- **How to use:** On up moves, target relative equal highs. Scale out at each equal high. Use them as reference for where price "wants" to reach.
- **Example in this video:** ES final hour. Multiple prior highs near 3400, 3450, 3475, 3500 area. Used each as a scale-out point. Price attacked these "clean highs" repeatedly.

- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** "Clean highs" do not need to be exact twins; even relative equal highs within a few ticks are valid targets.

=== [2026 #85 — Tapereading \ Practice Session Final Hour ES (ID 7zlblhLGraA)] — Twin Candle Highs / Lows as Liquidity Targets
- **Kind:** refinement
- **What ICT says:** When two candlesticks in a row have the same high (or within 1–2 ticks), that is a "twin candle high" and represents a very small, specific liquidity level. These are targets for market makers to run stops and sweep liquidity. Do not mistake these for larger relative equal highs; they are micro-level targets.
- **How to identify:** Look at consecutive candles; if two in a row share the same high/low, mark it as a twin high/low.
- **How to use:** Twin highs are often hit briefly then reversed; use them as quick scale points. On short-term moves (final hour), these are valid targets.
- **Example in this video:** ES. Multiple twin candle highs were hit during the final hour; each was a small liquidity target.
- **Timestamps:** [0:18:55](https://youtu.be/7zlblhLGraA?t=1135) — twin candle highs on 15s chart · [0:19:46](https://youtu.be/7zlblhLGraA?t=1186) — not requiring exact match; relative equal highs suffice
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Do not "demand" exact matching; even relative equal highs within small range are valid.

=== [2026 #86 — Market Alchemy - Trading ATH \ May 14, 2026 (ID wprXVW1pW9Y)] — 8:30 micro vs. mini divergence redelivery (news-event stop hunt)  (aliases: "re-deliver", "rerun")
- **Kind:** rule / example only
- **What ICT says:**
  - He posted on X that when a market makes a spike at the 8:30 news event in one contract (micro NQ went down precipitously) but not in the other (mini did not), the market "generally wants to redeliver that": it will go back to repeat that same price run. If the move had been higher, it would repeat that run higher.
  - It does not just overlap the level; it goes through it and operates as a stop hunt.
  - On the day: the micro made a spike low at 8:30; the mini did not. Price later traded lower to take out that low, taking out all the short-term sell-side liquidity, "primed everyone to anticipate it going lower", and then rallied. You can capitalise with either the micro or the mini.
  - He mentions a "bread and butter" setup on a 30-second chart using micros.
- **How to identify:** compare micro and mini NQ around 8:30; a one-sided spike low (or high) in one product that the other lacks marks the level to be revisited.
- **How to use:** expect a revisit and a stop run beyond that spike extreme before the real move; trade the reversal after the sweep.
- **Example in this video:** NQ micro prints a spike low at 8:30; mini does not; at the open price runs lower again, takes the 8:30 low and the short-term sell-side, then extends to the upside through the day.
- **Timestamps:** [0:02:44](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=164) — micro vs mini at 8:30 and redelivery; [0:03:30](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=210) — "goes through it and operates as a stop hunt"; [0:04:17](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=257) — sell-side liquidity taken, then extrapolates to the upside.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** an intermarket-style (instrument-divergence) SMT-like observation inside one index; not a standard named concept, so treat as a coinage-free observation.

=== [2026 #86 — Market Alchemy - Trading ATH \ May 14, 2026 (ID wprXVW1pW9Y)] — Minor vs. primary sell-side liquidity, and exiting early against the higher-timeframe trend
- **Kind:** rule / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - "Minor" sell-side is not the real sell-side he expects price to go for; the primary sell-side is the relative equal lows (the 10:29 low). Price reached the primary sell-side during the lunch macro just before 1:30 and then the market could go higher.
  - He marks minor sell-side lows as profit targets first and places limit exits slightly above the primary low, because price only took the 10:29 low by one tick at 1:26 PM (29,595.25 vs 29,595.50). An order sitting at or just beneath that low would never have filled ("too shallow"); experience shows exits must be "a tick or two" earlier.
  - Reason: trading against a bull market, if he is wrong "it can violently shift against me", so he takes "easy objectives" ("the cookie", the moral victory) rather than the best-case low.
  - He explains that exits normally have "that little overrun": the market goes a bit further without him; that is not a failure of the analysis.
  - Also keep time in mind: do not spend too much time in the trade as the 1:30 end of the lunch macro approaches.
- **How to use:** short against HTF bias: aim for minor sell-side first, take profit, accept being early.
- **Timestamps:** [0:20:53](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=1253) — the 1-tick overrun; [0:21:40](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=1300) — exit placement; [0:24:49](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=1489) — minor vs primary sell-side; [0:26:22](https://youtu.be/wprXVW1pW9Y?t=1582) — "little overrun", time risk.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** captions are slightly garbled around which direction the limit sits; the stable lesson is "primary low was tagged by one tick only; exit before it".

=== [2026 #87 — Turning Loss Into Gain - Market Alchemy (ID d8du4fMvOQU)] — 8:30 news run as the mark: fade the news-driven move toward the opposite-side 8:30 liquidity  (aliases ICT uses: "the mark", "8:30 buy side", "economic calendar", "news embargo")
- **Kind:** rule / narrative
- **What ICT says:**
  - The 8:30 news candle was used "to send price lower to build in a bearish narrative"; he is fading that. The high of the 8:30 candle (the buy-side sitting right before the news release) is the target.
  - "You have to identify the economic calendar and the liquidity off of a price run that originates at the 8:30. That's the mark... where the sniper has his crosshairs trained." Everything between is distraction and "wind gusts".
  - Ask "who made money this morning?" (shorts) and "who are they going after?": longs were stopped out, breakout sellers were baited short; he expects both sides to have been delivered before the move up.
  - "I don't have to be leading the pack. I just got to be part of the meal."
  - Why price may be held below: if they take the buy-side above 8:30 they can go short, ride it down and buy back at a low price from sellers, keeping the low intact first.
- **How to identify:** 8:30 (news) candle; a price run that originates from it; the 8:30 high is the resting buy-side pool when the news drove price down.
- **How to use:** go long once the sell-side below has been taken and a bullish PD array holds; target the 8:30 candle high (Terminus), with minor buy-side levels along the way as first partials.
- **Example in this video:** NQ-like chart (levels about 29,2xx–29,3xx). News at 8:30 drove price lower; ICT buys inversion FVGs in the morning chop, targets the minor buy-side highs (the "29,315s" area), then the 8:30 high (around the 29,360 level, caption).
- **Timestamps:** [0:01:43](https://youtu.be/d8du4fMvOQU?t=103) — minor buy-side vs the 8:30 high; [0:02:31](https://youtu.be/d8du4fMvOQU?t=151) — the 8:30 move was implemented to build a bearish narrative; [0:13:08](https://youtu.be/d8du4fMvOQU?t=788) — "the mark"; [1:18:53](https://youtu.be/d8du4fMvOQU?t=4733) — why they may keep lows intact first.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** minor buy-side is called minor "because it's below the low that will be targeted" (i.e. lower than the real draw). The 8:30 buy-side is the final target ("Terminus").

=== [2026 #87 — Turning Loss Into Gain - Market Alchemy (ID d8du4fMvOQU)] — Terminus (the final draw on liquidity) with minor buy-side first partials  (aliases ICT uses: "Terminus", "first partial", "stepping stones")
- **Kind:** definition (as used here) / rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - "The draw overall is here. So, this is Terminus": the overall target (the 8:30 high). Before it there is a logical first objective (a minor buy-side, an old relative-equal-high pool and SIBI) used for the first partial.
  - "I don't care if it goes up to 30,000 today" — the 8:30 high is the terminus; as a developing trader "look for the market to go to specific locations and be content with enough".
  - Knowing the draw on liquidity makes the stepping stones easy to find; it is not support/resistance.
  - After the stop-out he still thinks the 8:30 high will be reached ("daily range"), but he can't stay with the trade.
- **How to identify:** the 8:30 candle's high on a day where news sent price down; minor buy-side = nearer highs ("smooth highs") below it.
- **How to use:** first partial at the minor buy-side (29,315s area), second at event horizon, final at Terminus.
- **Example in this video:** minor buy-side ~29,312–29,315 first partial (filled ~59:32), next objective ~29,360, final = 8:30 high (not reached).
- **Timestamps:** [0:42:08](https://youtu.be/d8du4fMvOQU?t=2528) — "this is Terminus", first partial vs overall draw; [1:22:09](https://youtu.be/d8du4fMvOQU?t=4929) — "I don't care if it goes up to 30,000"; [1:20:32](https://youtu.be/d8du4fMvOQU?t=4832) — draw on liquidity and handholds.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** "Terminus" here is a target/destination concept (the title of an ICT model in other videos; not defined separately here).

=== [2026 #89 — ICT 2026 Futures Review & RTH ORG Commentary \ April 29, 2026 (ID tbEzAhdv_Ak)] — Relative Equal Highs / Lows as Liquidity Targets
- **Kind:** example only
- **What ICT says:** After a range-bound or choppy day, relative equal highs (prior swing highs repeated at similar price) are magnets on price. Market makers target them to run stops and accumulate / distribute. On FOMC mornings, these are used as reference levels for potential break-outs once the Fed announcement clears.
- **How to identify:** Mark intraday swing highs/lows; if multiple are within a few ticks, that level is a relative equal high/low.
- **How to use:** Target relative equal highs on up moves; target relative equal lows on down moves. These are confirmed liquidity removal points.
- **Example in this video:** NQ April 29. Relative equal highs from the open were at the target level; if price were to close above the upper quadrant and accelerate, it would head for those highs.
- **Timestamps:** [0:20:35](https://youtu.be/tbEzAhdv_Ak?t=1235) — relative equal highs as targets · [0:30:20](https://youtu.be/tbEzAhdv_Ak?t=1820) — confluence of highs from two timeframes
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** On choppy days, be flexible; price may test relative equal highs multiple times before breaking through.

- **Kind:** example only

These are the ultimate liquidity pools where market makers will run shorts' stops and absorb selling pressure.
- **How to identify:** Mark prior swing lows on the daily and intraday charts; if multiple are clustered within a few ticks, that is a relative equal low.
- **How to use:** Target relative equal lows as the final profit-taking zone. Do not expect price to go beyond relative equal lows easily; use them as hard stops for taking profits or managing the full position.

=== [2026 #90 — ICT 2026 Trading MNQ Futures & CFD US100 \ April 28, 2026 (ID jz0RbL1oVK8)] — Sell-Side Liquidity Pool (SSLP) / Minor Sell Side
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** A sell-side liquidity pool is a zone where sellers' stop-losses are clustered (above a market low). When price approaches this area, market makers will often run those stops briefly before allowing a larger continuation move. A minor sell side is a smaller cluster of liquidity; a major sell side is a larger one. Always identify the CE of the sell-side wick before breaking through.
- **How to identify:** Identify a wick that left behind inefficiency on the way down. The consequent encroachment of that wick marks the minor sell-side level.
- **How to use:** Before breaking below the sell-side level on a close, confirm the order flow by tracking whether bodies are respecting the midpoint. Once bodies close below the minor sell side with heaviness, the move lower is confirmed.
- **Example in this video:** MNQ April 28. A minor sell-side level was at ~27,082 (approximate, from wick CE); price had to close below this with heaviness to confirm the continuation to the relative equal lows.
- **Timestamps:** [0:11:58](https://youtu.be/jz0RbL1oVK8?t=718) — sell-side liquidity pool identified · [0:16:03](https://youtu.be/jz0RbL1oVK8?t=963) — minor sell side respecting and breakage
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Do not close below the minor sell side with just a wick; bodies must close below it for confirmation.

=== [2026 #91 — ICT 2026 Futures Review \ April 24, 2026 (ID 2OVAvl4H3IY)] — Shorting Previous Day's High (Daily High as Resistance)
- **Kind:** rule + refinement
- **What ICT says:** When price trades above the prior day's daily high (especially on consecutive days), it is a prime area to short. The reason: it attracts breakout traders and bull-flag traders expecting continuation; they place buy stops above the daily high. Smart money runs these stops, then drops. This is a structural setup that works across market conditions (bull or bear market); it is not specific to all-time highs. Targeting sell-side liquidity pools below the daily high is the natural target.
- **How to identify:** Pull the prior day's daily high from the chart; watch when price trades above it. If price is above it and showing weakness (bodies not advancing, pullbacks forming), it is ripe for shorting.
- **How to use:** Short entries at or just below the prior day's daily high. Use sell-side liquidity pools below as targets. Confirm entry with PD array structure (fair value gaps, order blocks, relative equal lows that align below the daily high).
- **Example in this video:** NQ April 24. Wednesday's daily high was at a specific level. Price traded above it on Thursday morning, triggering retail breakout buys. ICT shorted at that level and targeted the sell-side pool(s) below (ultimately hitting the relative equal lows near 27,069 / 26,805 range across the week).
- **Timestamps:** [0:06:06](https://youtu.be/2OVAvl4H3IY?t=366) — endless supply of setups from prior day's highs/lows · [0:09:35](https://youtu.be/2OVAvl4H3IY?t=575) — shorting at daily high despite all-time high · [0:14:23](https://youtu.be/2OVAvl4H3IY?t=863) — context as confirmation
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Works in bull, bear, and sideways markets. The key is recognizing retail breakout logic and the structural sell-side pool below.

=== [2026 #91 — ICT 2026 Futures Review \ April 24, 2026 (ID 2OVAvl4H3IY)] — Sell-Side Liquidity Pool (SSLP) / Engineering Liquidity
- **Kind:** definition + psychology
- **What ICT says:** When price leaves large wicks (especially down and back up), retail traders using candlestick analysis see those wicks as "bullish accumulation" and protection for their shorts' stop-losses. Smart money capitalizes on this: they use those wicks as areas where they know retail will have stops placed. They then "engineer liquidity" at these levels, raiding them briefly before continuing in the original direction. The wicks are not bullish; they are bait.
- **How to identify:** Look for a down wick with a body that closes well above it (creating a large wick to the downside). Mark that wick; expect price to revisit it and run the stops clustered above the wick.
- **How to use:** The wick area is a sell-side liquidity pool. Use it as a reference: price will likely be attracted to it on retracements. Do not mistake it for support; it is a raid zone. Build your short target accounts for price to attack these wicks and then continue lower.
- **Example in this video:** NQ April 24–Thursday. A sell-side liquidity pool (from wicks) was identified; price returned to it during the short move, briefly rallied into it (causing rallies against the short), then broke lower. ICT uses this knowledge to trail stops and manage partials.
- **Timestamps:** [0:15:59](https://youtu.be/2OVAvl4H3IY?t=959) — engineering liquidity explained · [0:16:46](https://youtu.be/2OVAvl4H3IY?t=1006) — coordinated strike analogy (orcas/killer whales)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** This requires understanding smart money intent; candlestick theory alone misses it.

=== [2026 #93 — ICT 2026 Fading Run To ATH Using MMSM \ Judas Swing \ April 22, 2026 (ID 6VaEdP4TYe8)] — Sell-side Liquidity (SSL) & Buy-side Liquidity (BSL)
- **Kind:** definition + identification
- **What ICT says:** Sell-side liquidity is resting above price where late sellers are holding stops or sell orders. Buy-side liquidity is resting below price where buyers are hiding. Smart money "takes" (sweeps) one side to trigger stops and create panic, then reverses and "leaves" liquidity resting in that zone for the next move.
- **How to identify:** SSL is above the recent high; BSL is below the recent low. Mark them on the chart at round levels or key resistance/support zones. In this trade, SSL sits at 26,830 and 26,805 (marked as "sell side imbalance" after a bearish FVG forms).
- **How to use:** Target the SSL with your profit-taking limit orders. Expect smart money to take one side (BSL first, leaving SSL intact) before reversing. Place partials just at the top of buy-side inefficiency (the zone that absorbed the buy orders when price was higher).
- **Example in this video:** SSL at 26,830 and 26,805 (the high area from Monday RTH open and prior trading). ICT places limit orders to buy partials at these levels, expecting smart money to sweep BSL and leave SSL for the retracement back down.
- **Timestamps:** [0:00:48](https://youtu.be/6VaEdP4TYe8?t=48), [0:07:19](https://youtu.be/6VaEdP4TYe8?t=439), [0:19:04](https://youtu.be/6VaEdP4TYe8?t=1144)

=== [2026 #93 — ICT 2026 Fading Run To ATH Using MMSM \ Judas Swing \ April 22, 2026 (ID 6VaEdP4TYe8)] — Equal Highs / Relative Equal Lows
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** When price prints two consecutive lows at or very close to the same level, that's a "relative equal low." This level often becomes a support or exit target (limit order level). It's a sign price is trying to hold support or is vulnerable to a drop through it.
- **How to identify:** Chart tool: mark the lows of the last two candles (or recent lower lows). If they're within a few ticks, you have a relative equal low.
- **How to use:** Place limit orders to exit partials at these levels, as they often get swept. Also use them as a reference for stops—if price trades below the relative equal low convincingly, the setup has failed.
- **Example in this video:** ICT refers to "relatively equal lows" (around 26,804–26,805) as his partial exit zone and as a point where, if price won't go through quickly, he expects reversal (loss of momentum).
- **Timestamps:** [0:15:51](https://youtu.be/6VaEdP4TYe8?t=951), [0:39:31](https://youtu.be/6VaEdP4TYe8?t=2371)

=== [2026 #94 — ICT 2026 Futures Market Review \ April 21, 2026 (ID PCMv3J6_9Lg)] — Engineering Liquidity
- **Kind:** concept + advanced technique
- **What ICT says:** Smart money deliberately creates patterns that appear to be targeting buy-side or sell-side liquidity, luring retail traders into false breakouts and reversals. "Engineering buy-side liquidity" means leaving relative equal highs (false highs that look like resistance) so that when price rises again, retail traders place buy stops just above them. Smart money then runs price up, takes those stops, and reverses. This is not random; it's deliberate market structure manipulation.
- **How to identify:** Watch for patterns: price makes a high, retreats, makes another high at roughly the same level (relative equal high). This looks like failed breakout resistance. Next, watch if price rallies back up and above those equal highs with bodies in the upper portions of FVGs (usually a bullish sign), but then reverses sharply downward. That's engineered buy-side liquidity (the equal highs were the bait).
- **How to use:** When you see equal highs being formed, don't assume they're traditional resistance. Ask: am I being set up to buy here and get stopped out? Watch the bodies—if they're in the upper half of inversion FVGs on the way up, it's a trap. Respect it and don't go long. Expect a reversal once the equal highs are cleared. Use this as a sign to place short entries instead.
- **Example in this video:** ES, April 21, 2026. Equal highs are engineered at 26,800+, then price rallies back up, takes those highs, but bodies print in the upper half of the inversion FVG with no continuation. This signals engineered buy-side liquidity—the rally is a trap. Price then reverses and sells off aggressively. This is exactly what ICT predicted on the livestream.
- **Timestamps:** [0:29:19](https://youtu.be/PCMv3J6_9Lg?t=1759), [0:30:08](https://youtu.be/PCMv3J6_9Lg?t=1808), [0:31:00](https://youtu.be/PCMv3J6_9Lg?t=1860)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Engineering liquidity is not the same as simple support/resistance. It's active market making, not passive level holding. Recognize it by the deliberate pattern and the body placement in FVGs—not just the wick behavior. Bodies in upper halves of inversion FVGs + equal highs = trap in progress.

=== [2026 #95 — ICT 2026 Low Probability Tape-reading \ April 21, 2026 (ID 5PpnT-l-zSk)] — Draw on Liquidity (DOL) & Entry Models
- **Kind:** entry signal + process
- **What ICT says:** A "draw on liquidity" occurs when price approaches a known liquidity level (a gap, an old high/low, a pool of buy-side or sell-side orders). Not every draw is an entry. An entry requires **order flow confirmation**—additional price action (a specific candle formation, body placement, wick behavior) that signals smart money is about to move. Just because price reached your target doesn't mean you enter; you wait for the order flow signal. "It's not 'he said that level, so let's go short.' That's not what this is at all."
- **How to identify:** Identify where price is going (RTH gaps, old highs/lows, key wicks). Watch for price to approach that level. When it does, pause and ask: "Do I see order flow confirmation here?" Look for bodies in specific halves of FVGs, bodies respecting key wicks, or small reversal candles. If you don't see that confirmation, don't enter.
- **How to use:** Use draws on liquidity as an **observation** tool. When price hits your target level, annotate it and study what happened. Did bodies respect the level (order flow confirmed)? Or did it blow through? This builds your intuition. Over time, you'll recognize order flow signals instinctively. In real-time trading, use it as a filter: draw on liquidity + order flow confirmation = entry.
- **Example in this video:** ICT drew on liquidity at Monday's RTH opening range gap low (26,695) on April 21. Price approached it. But because the Fed speaker was at 10:00, he didn't force an entry—he waited for order flow confirmation. The market didn't provide clean confirmation due to the news event, so he reduced conviction.
- **Timestamps:** [0:25:44](https://youtu.be/5PpnT-l-zSk?t=1544), [0:26:32](https://youtu.be/5PpnT-l-zSk?t=1592), [0:37:19](https://youtu.be/5PpnT-l-zSk?t=2239)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Many retail traders watch ICT's livestreams, see a level, and assume it's a "signal" to trade that level. It's not. It's an **observation point**. The entry signal is the order flow. If you're a new trader watching, focus on learning order flow patterns, not just level identification.

=== [2026 #97 — ICT 2026 Futures Market Review \ April 18, 2026 (ID 9nmjg331xGQ)] — Stop Hunting & Manual Market Intervention
- **Kind:** market structure + psychology
- **What ICT says:** When you announce your stop level publicly (e.g., on Twitter/X), market makers know where retail traders have clustered stops. They will deliberately run price down to hit those stops, creating a sudden wick and panic liquidation. This is **not algorithmic**; it's manual intervention by a person (Phil) who sees the opportunity and takes it in seconds. ICT demonstrated this on Friday live: he announced his stop, then price wicked down to it in a fast, sudden move, hit all the stops, then reversed higher. This proves stops are deliberately targeted, not random.
- **How to identify:** Watch for sudden, fast wicks that hit a known level (e.g., a level ICT tweeted about), followed immediately by a reversal. The wick is almost instantaneous—not a steady decline, but a snap. This is the signature of manual intervention.
- **How to use:** Accept that stop hunting happens. Place your stops tactically, not predictably. Don't announce them publicly (or if you do, as ICT does, expect them to be run). Consider using stops that are far from obvious levels, or use position sizing + mental discipline instead of hard stops. Alternatively, understand that a stop run might be a final capitulation that precedes your move higher—it's a feature, not a bug, of the market structure.
- **Example in this video:** ICT places a stop and tweets about it. Price wicks down, hits it, stops him out. He immediately re-enters higher (after the run is done) and continues the long position. This is ICT in "Shinobi mode"—understanding the stop hunt is coming and planning to re-enter after it happens.
- **Timestamps:** [0:25:15](https://youtu.be/9nmjg331xGQ?t=1515), [0:26:02](https://youtu.be/9nmjg331xGQ?t=1562), [0:26:49](https://youtu.be/9nmjg331xGQ?t=1609)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** ICT is emphatic: "This is not algorithmic. This is manual intervention. This is a person." He later goes into "Shinobi mode" (invisible, no posts, no resets) to avoid giving away his re-entry plan.

=== [2026 #100 — ICT 2026 Asian Session Short Review \ April 14, 2026 (ID 3J8drYX2zHM)] — Turtle Soup Entry Model
- **Kind:** entry setup + liquidity trap
- **What ICT says:** A turtle soup is when price runs above a **two-stage buy-side liquidity pool** (buyers holding stops above two different levels), then reverses sharply into the zone between the two pools, where sell-side liquidity rests. The "soup" is the trap: retail buyers place buy stops above the first pool, smart money runs price up and through those stops, then reverses and trades through the second pool, creating panic selling. Entry is in the zone between the two pools (or just above the upper pool, expecting reversal). Target is the sell-side liquidity below the lower pool.
- **How to identify:** 
  1. Identify two buy-side liquidity pools (two previous highs or resistance levels).
  2. Price runs above the first, then above the second (taking both buy-side pools).
  3. Expect sharp reversal and downside run.
  4. Entry: above the second pool (where reversals form) or in the "soup" zone (between the two pools).
- **How to use:** Turtle soup setups in macro time windows have the highest probability. Short aggressively once both pools are taken and bodies show reversal (up close candles shift to down close). Target the sell-side liquidity below.
- **Example in this video:** Two buy-side pools were identified on the Asian session chart. Price ran above both during the macro window. ICT then entered short, targeting the sell-side liquidity below. The trade worked as expected.
- **Timestamps:** [0:03:26](https://youtu.be/3J8drYX2zHM?t=206)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Turtle soup is one of the most reliable setups when macro time aligns. However, in very strong bull markets, it may fail—price may run through and keep going. Identify bull market bias first before using turtle soup.

=== [2026 #100 — ICT 2026 Asian Session Short Review \ April 14, 2026 (ID 3J8drYX2zHM)] — Two-Stage Liquidity Extraction
- **Kind:** market structure + execution model
- **What ICT says:** Smart money often extracts liquidity in two stages: first, they take out the obvious buy-side pool (the first high that everyone sees), then they take out the second, less-obvious buy-side pool. Only then do they run price lower to the sell-side liquidity. This two-stage extraction is a signature pattern. When you see both stages hit, conviction is very high that the move will continue in the opposite direction.
- **How to identify:** Watch price run higher. First high = first buy-side pool. Price pulls back slightly, then runs higher again. Second high = second buy-side pool. Both are hit. Now expect reversal lower.
- **How to use:** Don't short after the first pool is taken; wait for the second. Once both pools are taken, short aggressively. This reduces false signals and aligns your trade with smart money's order flow.
- **Example in this video:** Two buy-side pools were taken during the macro window. ICT waited for both, then entered short with conviction.
- **Timestamps:** [0:05:06](https://youtu.be/3J8drYX2zHM?t=306)

=== [2026 #101 — ICT 2026 Entries & Drills \ April 15, 2026 (ID KASpfAd1MnI)] — Relative Equal Highs/Lows — "to it and through it"  (aliases: REH/REL, stop hunt, false breakout, turtle soup)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - When price sits just below relative equal highs and has already run (taken) sell-side/short-term lows, the stops of longs have already been trailed under those lows and "enough damage" is done. A move through the equal highs should then accelerate ("to it and through it").
  - A shallow peek above the equal highs, then back into the range, is a false breakout and more indicative of an intraday reversal or a deeper retracement (bulls chased, "dragged across the coals"). If it then rolls over and takes the low, he is "not interested at all for the rest of the day".
  - After going above, you want to see it expand aggressively, not stall.
  - If a high had buy-side above and gets run, and a lower high etc. all keep running without give-back, longs should be developing without stutter.
- **How to identify:** two or more highs within a tick or two (his words: "look how relatively equal they are"), with sell-side already taken below.
- **How to use:** if long, expect continuation immediately after the high is taken; if it fails (small run then back inside), exit/partial and consider the opposite as a stop-hunt reversal; practice false-breakout (turtle soup) shorts here in demo only.
- **Example in this video:** MNQ, about 10:45 ET: price took a stop hunt of equal lows, rallied via an FVG, sat under REH; he outlines both outcomes; price went through the highs and hit the drill target, then continued ([1:24:51](https://youtu.be/KASpfAd1MnI?t=5091) to [1:28:02](https://youtu.be/KASpfAd1MnI?t=5282)). Earlier ([1:01:14](https://youtu.be/KASpfAd1MnI?t=3674)) price takes a higher high but falls back into the range, which he calls wrong behaviour if bullish and possibly a failure swing.
- **Timestamps:** [1:25:37](https://youtu.be/KASpfAd1MnI?t=5137) — "to it and through it"; [1:26:24](https://youtu.be/KASpfAd1MnI?t=5184) — false breakout for the day; [0:43:41](https://youtu.be/KASpfAd1MnI?t=2621) — how to practice becoming a turtle-soup type trader.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** he says repeatedly "I could have it wrong"; it is a conditional read, not a prediction.
