=== [2026 #102 — ICT 2026 Futures Review \ April 14, 2026 (ID DJ67ft6XENo)] — Dealing Range vs. External Range Liquidity
- **Kind:** liquidity structure + targeting framework
- **What ICT says:** The **dealing range** is the range of price from the high of the current move down to a significant low (e.g., the 10:00 low during the morning session). **External range liquidity** is the sell-side liquidity that sits OUTSIDE and BELOW the dealing range—further lows that were formed earlier (yesterday, earlier this week). When price retraces into the dealing range (say, a lunch macro retracement), targeting the low of the dealing range is one target. Targeting external range liquidity is the more aggressive target, going below the dealing range to unexploited sellers.
- **How to identify:** Draw the dealing range (high to low of current session/move). Mark the external range liquidity (previous session's low, earlier week's low, or other old support).
- **How to use:** In lunch macro shorts, first target the low of the dealing range (smaller profit). If price breaks below that, the next target is external range liquidity (larger move). Use the dealing range low as a first partial, then hold for external range.
- **Example in this video:** The dealing range was from the morning high (26,600+) to the 10:00 low. External range liquidity was older support levels below. ICT targeted both, taking a partial at the dealing range low, then holding for the external range liquidity.
- **Timestamps:** [0:20:11](https://youtu.be/DJ67ft6XENo?t=1211), [0:29:57](https://youtu.be/DJ67ft6XENo?t=1797)

=== [2026 #102 — ICT 2026 Futures Review \ April 14, 2026 (ID DJ67ft6XENo)] — Street Smarts Reference & Turtle Soup History
- **Kind:** personal trading history / concept attribution
- **What ICT says:** ICT credits Linda Raschke and Larry Connors' 1995 book "Street Smarts" for introducing him to false-breakout concepts, which became the foundation for his trading philosophy. However, his "turtle soup" is NOT the same as the book's definition. He uses the term facetiously because the concept of running above a level and reversing (false breakout) inspired his own rule-based model. His turtle soup involves two-stage liquidity extraction and specific macro time windows, which go beyond the book's straightforward definition.
- **How to identify:** ICT often references this in live streams to clarify that he's not just copying book concepts; he's adapted and built on them with his own rules.
- **How to use:** This is more context than a trading rule. It's a reminder that good traders build on existing frameworks and adapt them to their own experience and market conditions. Don't assume ICT's turtle soup is the book definition; it's his evolved version.
- **Example in this video:** ICT mentions Linda Raschke (who has since blocked him on X) and acknowledges her influence early in his trading career. He respects the book but clarifies that his current methodology diverges significantly.
- **Timestamps:** [0:16:15](https://youtu.be/DJ67ft6XENo?t=975), [0:17:01](https://youtu.be/DJ67ft6XENo?t=1021), [0:17:48](https://youtu.be/DJ67ft6XENo?t=1068)

=== [2026 #104 — ICT 2026 Futures Opening Range Tape-Reading \ April 13, 2026 (ID Il_DM4T7nzg)] — Stop Hunt  (aliases: stop run, liquidation run)
- **Kind:** rule + market maker behavior
- **What ICT says:** When price rapidly dips below a key support level (where retail traders' stops are placed), takes them out, then immediately reverses back up (often higher than before), that's a stop hunt. Market makers/institutions are "hunting" for retail stop orders. The subsequent reversal (after stops are cleared) shows their real intent is to go the opposite direction. This happens in sync with algorithmic models firing once the algorithmic condition is met.
- **How to identify:** Price drops sharply, wicks below a key low (often below a support zone or FVG), then body closes far above that low on the next candle. The wick is much longer than the body.
- **How to use:** When you see a stop hunt (bodies didn't go lower, just the wick), don't assume the move is over. Expect the opposite direction. Follow the same model, watch for the next FVG on the lower timeframe, and anticipate a run in the opposite direction. Don't be fooled into shorting after the hunt.
- **Example in this video:** NQ 1m: bodies reluctant to go lower, then a sharp wick down (stop hunt likely), followed by a powerful rally. ICT warns: don't assume this is an inversion; watch the bodies stay above subsequent levels (which they did) and re-affirm bullishness. Another run down (potential stop hunt) then reversal and run to higher minor buy side. This is "the same model, just in a different location."
- **Timestamps:** [0:12:13](https://youtu.be/Il_DM4T7nzg?t=733) wick here but body avoiding lower half = stop hunt coming. [0:20:06](https://youtu.be/Il_DM4T7nzg?t=1206) reluctance = warning; price about to reverse (stop hunt). [0:25:44](https://youtu.be/Il_DM4T7nzg?t=1544) engineering sell side liquidity via stops, then running it. [0:26:31](https://youtu.be/Il_DM4T7nzg?t=1591) orders aren't just "stops"—they're also algorithmic triggers for shorts expecting weakness. [0:35:26](https://youtu.be/Il_DM4T7nzg?t=2126) using the prior high after stop hunt dips.
- **Nuance / caveats:** Stop hunts are not "rigged" against you in a moral sense; they're algorithmic efficiency: once the algorithmic condition is met (stop cleared), the dormant model fires up and hunts longs.

=== [2026 #106 — ICT 2026 Tape-Reading Opening Range \ April 10, 2026 (ID Hiq47zliJ_Y)] — Sell-Side Liquidity (aliases: short stops, sell orders waiting, liquidity pool)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Sell-side liquidity is a cluster of sell orders or old resistance levels where traders have stop losses (shorts). When price reaches sell-side, it runs through it quickly (often called a liquidity sweep or raid). Identify sell-side using old highs, prior swing highs, and FVG boundaries.
- **How to identify:** Look at prior session's highs, swing highs, tops of consolidations. These are sell-side pools. Also, the upper boundary of an FVG is sell-side.
- **How to use:** When price approaches sell-side, expect a potential run through it (liquidity sweep) or a stall/reversal. If you're short and price is approaching sell-side below you, tighten stops.
- **Example in this video:** MNQ: multiple sell-side pools identified at prior highs and FVG boundaries; when price reaches them, it either sweeps through or reverses.
- **Timestamps:** [0:08:07](https://youtu.be/Hiq47zliJ_Y?t=487) identifies sell-side; [0:13:45](https://youtu.be/Hiq47zliJ_Y?t=825) references liquidity sweeps as myth vs. reality.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** "No such thing as liquidity sweeps" is a myth; ICT just proved one live on tape.

=== [2026 #106 — ICT 2026 Tape-Reading Opening Range \ April 10, 2026 (ID Hiq47zliJ_Y)] — Buy-Side Liquidity (aliases: buy stops, long stops, liquidity at lows)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Buy-side liquidity is a cluster of buy orders or old lows where long stops are. When price drives down and hits buy-side, it may be swept (longs stopped out) or bounce. Identify buy-side using old lows, prior swing lows, and FVG boundaries.
- **How to identify:** Look at prior session's lows, swing lows, bottoms of consolidations. These are buy-side pools. Also, the lower boundary of an FVG is buy-side.
- **How to use:** When price approaches buy-side, expect potential interaction (bounce or sweep). If you're long, watch buy-side below as support.
- **Example in this video:** MNQ: buy-side at opening range gap low; price drawn to this level repeatedly.
- **Timestamps:** [0:10:05](https://youtu.be/Hiq47zliJ_Y?t=605) watches for buy-side interaction.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Buy-side and sell-side are always contextual; use both to frame your tape reading.

=== [2026 #108 — ICT 2026 OR Tape-reading \ April 08, 2026 (ID yqBvOeOKcBM)] — Slow is Smooth, Smooth is Fast (aliases: patience, tempo, process over outcome)
- **Kind:** philosophy
- **What ICT says:** The fastest way to get good is to go slowly and methodically. Don't rush tape reading. Don't skip paper trading. Don't overtrade. When you slow down, you see details; when you see details, you trade better; when you trade better, you make more money faster than if you'd rushed. The irony: patience is the accelerant.
- **How to identify:** Are you trying to compress 6–8 weeks into 2–3 weeks? Are you jumping to live trading without paper trading? That's rushing.
- **How to use:** Stick to the timeline. 6–8 weeks tape reading. 6–8 weeks paper trading. Then live. Don't deviate. Trust the process.
- **Example in this video:** ICT says, "Slow is smooth, and smooth is fast. Doing it a different way, racing around or trying to skip over things, it's not it."
- **Timestamps:** [0:33:35](https://youtu.be/yqBvOeOKcBM?t=2015) states the phrase directly; [0:41:31](https://youtu.be/yqBvOeOKcBM?t=2491) applies it to the development timeline.
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** This cuts against the culture of instant results. ICT is explicit: there's no faster way.

=== [2026 #109 — ICT 2026 Market Review \ April 07, 2026 (ID zpLCZ1Y46Yk)] — Relative Equal Highs / Lows (aliases: equality levels, matched highs/lows)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** When two swing highs (or lows) are at nearly the same level, they form a relative equal high (or low). These are key breakout/breakdown levels. A break of relative equal highs is a strong bullish signal; a break of relative equal lows is bearish.
- **How to identify:** Plot daily swings. When two swing highs or lows align, mark them as relative equal.
- **How to use:** Relative equal highs act as resistance until broken; then they act as support. Relative equal lows act as support until broken; then they act as resistance.
- **Example in this video:** Pound/Dollar and EUR/USD analyzed using relative equal highs as targets.
- **Timestamps:** [0:31:10](https://youtu.be/zpLCZ1Y46Yk?t=1870) shows relative equal highs on cable (GBP/USD).
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** In event-driven markets, these levels may be blown through.

=== [2026 #110 — ICT 2026 Opening Range Tape Reading \ April 07, 2026 (ID gLt8kAG7noQ)] — Liquidity Sweeps (Buy-Side / Sell-Side)
- **Kind:** rule | entry model
- **What ICT says:** On a bearish sentiment day (engineered by news/geopolitical risk), market makers first run price up to take out shorts' stops (buy-side liquidity), then run it down to take out longs' stops (sell-side liquidity). Order of execution matters: if buy side is taken first on a bearish day, sell side remains and should be the next target.
- **How to identify:** Watch for a sharp move that "traps" a logical group of traders (e.g., shortsellers on a bearish day), then reverse. Mark the short-term highs and lows where stops would logically be placed.
- **How to use:** After buy-side (BSL) is swept on a bearish day, expect sell-side sweep next. Don't trade until both sides are cleared, as traders on both sides are "unseated."
- **Example in this video:** 9:30 price ran higher, sweeping shorts' stops. Then it failed to break above ORG high and reversed lower, setting up the sell-side sweep around the lower quadrant/octant levels. By 9:30am, both BSL and SSL were tagged.
- **Timestamps:** [0:13:53](https://youtu.be/gLt8kAG7noQ?t=833), [0:16:17](https://youtu.be/gLt8kAG7noQ?t=977), [0:20:29](https://youtu.be/gLt8kAG7noQ?t=1229), [0:35:11](https://youtu.be/gLt8kAG7noQ?t=2111)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** This is a special case of the market maker buy/sell model applied to opening ranges on sentiment-engineered days.

=== [2026 #110 — ICT 2026 Opening Range Tape Reading \ April 07, 2026 (ID gLt8kAG7noQ)] — Sentiment Engineering & Narrative Trading
- **Kind:** rule | definition
- **What ICT says:** Geopolitical news (e.g., threats of military action) creates fear-based sentiment. Retail traders position against that sentiment (bearish bias). Market makers exploit this by taking the opposite side first—running price up to stop out shorts, then down to trap breakout buyers. The narrative is the setup; price action confirms the execution.
- **How to identify:** Watch economic calendar and geopolitical headlines. Note when sentiment is "engineered" bearish or bullish. Then observe if price action goes the opposite direction initially.
- **How to use:** Don't trade with sentiment; trade against it once you confirm the setup. But on extremely high-uncertainty days, the risk may be too high.
- **Example in this video:** Trump posts and war threats made traders expect a downside move. Market ran up first (against sentiment), trapping shorts. By the time sellers entered, they were already trapped.
- **Timestamps:** [0:07:27](https://youtu.be/gLt8kAG7noQ?t=447), [0:08:15](https://youtu.be/gLt8kAG7noQ?t=495), [0:15:29](https://youtu.be/gLt8kAG7noQ?t=929), [0:18:02](https://youtu.be/gLt8kAG7noQ?t=1082)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** None stated; this is core to the framework.

=== [2026 #112 — ICT 2026 Market Commentary \ April 06, 2026 (ID m_ND0oO67cU)] — Turtle Soup (False Breakout)
- **Kind:** entry model
- **What ICT says:** Turtle soup is a false breakout; price breaks above a key level (relative equal high, resistance) but fails to sustain it, reversing back into the range. Entry: short the breakout at the high; cover at the range midpoint or prior lows. This is a reversal setup.
- **How to identify:** Watch for a candle that closes above a resistance level. If the next few candles close back inside the range, it's likely a turtle soup.
- **How to use:** Short the candle that breaks out above resistance. Set stop above the breakout high. Target: midpoint of the range or prior support.
- **Example in this video:** NQ: "Using this as turtle soup, it's a false breakout, okay? And then expect it to pull back down into the range."
- **Timestamps:** [0:26:26](https://youtu.be/m_ND0oO67cU?t=1586)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Works best in consolidating, non-trend markets; less reliable in strong trends.

=== [2026 #113 — ICT 2026 1st Hour Dealing Range \ March 28, 2026 (ID 6DuByzKLDsc)] — Turtle Soup logic (retail breakout flipped)  (aliases: turtle soup, stop run below range low)
- **Kind:** rule / explanation
- **What ICT says:**
  - After the first hour's range has been established and has been one-sided (down from the open), the market makes money for shorts and entices breakout sellers to chase support breaks. The algorithm uses that: a shallow run below the low, then back inside the range.
  - Quote worth keeping: "we're flipping retail logic upside down and attacking them with it."
  - "This is how you find wonderful turtle soup setups."
  - Expect the sweep below to be shallow in this climate ("not likely to break the range"), not an expansion.
- **How to identify:** One-sided first-hour range; a run that takes out the low by a small margin and does not hold; closes back inside.
- **How to use:** Do not chase the breakout; instead trade the reversal or, in a trending day, the continuation after the tested range (see next entry).
- **Example in this video:** Friday MNQ: low broken by a shallow run then back into range; he said live in advance it would just go below a little bit and return.
- **Timestamps:** [0:51:31](https://youtu.be/6DuByzKLDsc?t=3091) — "I am not a breakout trader"; [0:53:06](https://youtu.be/6DuByzKLDsc?t=3186) — flipping retail logic
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Ties turtle soup to the FHDR specifically.

=== [2026 #114 — ICT 2026 Market Commentary \ March 27, 2026 (ID z6AxPV2NQ9U)] — Buy-Side Liquidity (BSL) / Sell-Side Liquidity (SSL) and Liquidity Pools
- **Kind:** definition / concept
- **What ICT says:** Liquidity rests at certain levels. A high where many traders have stops above is "sell-side liquidity" (SSL); a low where stops are placed below is "buy-side liquidity" (BSL). Market makers hunt these levels. You must identify them on your structure and expect price to run to them. Sell-side imbalance = inefficiency below a high (e.g., gap down after a high). Buy-side imbalance = inefficiency above a low (e.g., gap up after a low).
- **How to identify:** 
  - Identify swing highs and lows.
  - Assume stops are placed just beyond them (e.g., 2–5 ticks above a high, 2–5 ticks below a low).
  - That aggregated stop placement = the liquidity pool.
  - When a gap forms adjacent to such a level, it is imbalance near liquidity.
- **How to use:** 
  - Bias towards price running to liquidity (e.g., if you're bullish, look for price to run to a prior swing high's SSL).
  - Use liquidity objectives as targets, not precise entry triggers.
  - Recognize that liquidity hunts are a market maker behavior; expect sudden runs and reversals at these levels.
- **Example in this video:** 0:07:12–0:08:46 — daily chart shows "an intermediate-term sell side liquidity pool" below the daily suspension block. ICT notes this as a target or resistance if price continues lower. On the 1m, multiple references to "buy side here" and "sell side there" throughout.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:07:12](https://youtu.be/z6AxPV2NQ9U?t=432) — sell-side intermediate-term liquidity pool on daily
  - [0:19:05](https://youtu.be/z6AxPV2NQ9U?t=1145) — buy-side order imbalance in opening range gap
  - [0:34:40](https://youtu.be/z6AxPV2NQ9U?t=2080) — daily buy-side imbalance / sell-side inefficiency trade example
- **Nuance:** Liquidity pools are not precisely defined; they are inferred from structure. Precision comes from the imbalance (gap) adjacent to them, not the exact high/low.

=== [2026 #115 — ICT 2026 Market Commentary \ March 25, 2026 (ID UQJW9obxt34)] — Relative Equal Lows (RELs) / Relative Equal Highs (REHs)
- **Kind:** definition / target
- **What ICT says:** A relative equal low is a prior low that price is likely to revisit or break through on a fresh downside move. A relative equal high is the analog for upside. Use them as targets after confirming direction via FVG/iFVG or intraday structures. They are NOT precise levels but "probable" objectives.
- **How to identify:** 
  1. Scan the chart for prior swing lows (RELs) or highs (REHs) that price has not recently touched.
  2. If trading lower off an iFVG, anticipate price will reach at least one prior REL.
  3. If breaking above, anticipate prior REH.
- **How to use:** Use as secondary targets. Primary target: consequent encroachment or imbalance rejection. Secondary: the nearest REL/REH. If price blows past REL/REH, expect continuation to the next level.
- **Example in this video:** 
  - Silver (0:18:20–0:19:07): "we would trade down to consequent encroachment. And if we got below that, we would clear up the smooth levels here. And eventually went down into that little volume imbalance there." Price cleared consequent encroachment, then hit sell-side.
  - MNQ (0:27:03): "You take this below, then come back, and then not go in the lower half… then I would expect it to start going higher" once REHs are available. Relative equal highs act as targets.
  - Intraday ES (0:28:36–0:29:24): "took the sell side into the imbalance here, left the sell side there. Rallied up, pushed it higher, left this high intact." Relative equal highs are tracked and expected to be hit.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:18:20](https://youtu.be/UQJW9obxt34?t=1100) — gold/silver consequent encroachment and smooth levels (RELs)
  - [0:27:03](https://youtu.be/UQJW9obxt34?t=1623) — MNQ REHs as targets if iFVG breaks
  - [0:29:24](https://youtu.be/UQJW9obxt34?t=1764) — intraday ES: relative equal highs tracked
- **Nuance:** RELs/REHs are probabilistic, not certain. Price may overshoot or undershoot; use them as "likely" zones, not hard stops.

=== [2026 #118 — ICT 2026 Market Commentary \ March 17, 2026 (ID AxLXI00L8zM)] — Relative Equal Lows / Highs
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Two price lows (or highs) that are very close to each other (within a "handle" or two), indicating a high-probability point for a raid (move in the opposite direction) or at least a significant level. On March 17, the market opened at 9:30, ran higher, then left a low at 24,962.00 and another low at 24,964.25—"relatively equal lows." This made the level a "high probability candidate for a raid or at least in the very minimum consider this a relative equal low because the low to the right of this one is slightly higher."
- **How to identify:** Compare two recent lows; if they're within a few ticks of each other, they're relatively equal.
- **How to use:** As a point of interest for a reversal or as a level to watch for stops.
- **Example in this video:** NQ March 17: relative equal lows at 24,962 and 24,964.25.
- **Timestamps:** [0:12:54](https://youtu.be/AxLXI00L8zM?t=774)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** "Relative equal" (within a few ticks) is different from an "exact" equal; used to identify high-probability reversal or raid levels.

- **Kind:** definition

=== [2026 #122 — ICT 2026 Market Commentary \ March 10, 2026 (ID 54kzPj03Rkg)] — Buy-Side Liquidity (BSL) vs Sell-Side Liquidity (SSL)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Buy-side liquidity = resting above current price (buy orders waiting for a rally to fill). Sell-side = resting below current price (sell orders from shorts wanting to cover). Identifying these pools and whether they'll be taken = the core of his algorithmic trading.
- **How to identify:** Look at the daily high (BSL) and daily low (SSL) of prior days' ranges. Also, intraday, look at incomplete runs or failed breakout levels that leave liquidity hanging.
- **How to use:** If market structure is bullish, you expect price to run to BSL and stop (or wick through and reverse). If bearish, expect SS. Entry: anticipate the liquidity run and size accordingly. Targets: the known liquidity pools.
- **Example in this video:** Intraday highs (25,000 146.75, 25,108 even) = minor buy side. Sell side at 24,892.50.
- **Timestamps:** [0:08:38](https://youtu.be/54kzPj03Rkg?t=518) — listing the levels; [0:11:01](https://youtu.be/54kzPj03Rkg?t=661) — explaining how algorithm uses sell-side info
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Not all highs/lows = major liquidity pools. Know your context and whether a level was "fought over" (many rejections) vs "breezed through."

=== [2026 #123 — ICT 2026 Market Commentary \ March 09, 2026 (ID CodF_0llnuk)] — Turtle Soup (Failed Breakout Reversal)
- **Kind:** entry model / example only
- **What ICT says:** Turtle soup = price trades below a recent low (fails to break out) and immediately reverses. Classic manipulation setup: run a low to stop out bears, then reverse. Not a buy signal itself; rather, context for where NOT to be short and WHERE to go long (back above failed low).
- **How to identify:** Recent swing low broken = turtle soup potential. Watch for immediate reversal back above that low.
- **How to use:** Do NOT short below the low when it's being tested (likely to fail). If it fails, trade long above it. Stop loss: below the low just tested. Target: buy-side liquidity or next key level.
- **Example in this video:** ES on March 09: traded below an earlier low (looked like continuation trade), but immediately bounced back up = turtle soup environment. Not shorting opportunity; a long opportunity using the failed break as confirmation.
- **Timestamps:** [0:27:47](https://youtu.be/CodF_0llnuk?t=1667) — "In other words, go below it, outside of it, and trade back up into it" = turtle soup definition
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Common in low-volatility or consolidation ranges where algos shake out weak positions before the real move.

=== [2026 #125 — ICT 2026 Market Commentary \ March 08, 2026 (ID JIs96_2rfkE)] — Relative Equal Highs/Lows & Old Highs/Lows (Liquidity Pools)
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:** Old highs/lows = liquidity resting at prior significant levels. Relative equal highs = price is making a similar high to a prior high but not higher (resistance). These are pools of buy-side (above, for shorts to cover) or sell-side (below, for longs to cover) liquidity. Algorithm will run these levels to "sweep" trapped traders' stops.
- **How to identify:** Mark on daily/weekly prior significant highs and lows. When price approaches them, mark as potential liquidity pool.
- **How to use:** Anticipate runs to old levels. Use these as target references. Stop placement: beyond old levels (to avoid being swept). If you're short and old high is being run, either exit or expand stop beyond the high (if risk allows).
- **Example in this video:** Dow: old high from prior month is a draw. Nasdaq August lows are a draw for first half of year. Dollar index: old high is a draw if risk-off scenario plays out.
- **Timestamps:** [0:11:05](https://youtu.be/JIs96_2rfkE?t=665) — discussing August lows as draw; [0:21:27](https://youtu.be/JIs96_2rfkE?t=1287) — "these lows here... uh again, this is March contract, so... I like these"; [0:28:35](https://youtu.be/JIs96_2rfkE?t=1715) — marking sell-side and buy-side liquidity
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Not all old levels matter. Significance = how recent, how violently rejected, and how many times price has returned to it. Recent highs/lows within 5–10 days are more relevant than years-old levels.

=== [2026 #127 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ February 27, 2026 (ID TFyk2jbQzdc)] — Sell-side delivery vs sell-side liquidity
- **Kind:** definition / clarification
- **What ICT says:** The run lower of price action is sell-side delivery, not sell-side liquidity. Sell-side liquidity is the resting stops below a low (the target); delivery is the leg of price moving down.
- **Timestamps:** [0:58:44](https://youtu.be/TFyk2jbQzdc?t=3524) — "This run is sell-side delivery, not sell-side liquidity"

=== [2026 #129 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ February 23, 2026 (ID NxwfXE6Zyns)] — Daily Draw on Liquidity as the bias anchor
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** His daily-chart annotation is where he thinks Nasdaq draws for the first quarter/first half of the year; he looks for reasons each day to justify why price may be gravitating there. Don't be afraid of being wrong on the daily; many daily candles will go against the idea. Constantly referring back to the daily chart prevents flipping bias intraday and second-guessing the higher-timeframe premise. Choose setups that line up with that bias (here shorts) to have the most advantages and avoid spraying buys and sells all day.

=== [2026 #130 — ICT 2026 Forex & Futures Commentary \ February 17, 2026 (ID z5ytUvVeUoQ)] — Relative Equal Highs/Lows & Liquidity Reversal
- **Kind:** definition / rule
- **What ICT says:** When price makes a high at approximately the same level as a prior high (within a few ticks), it's a "relative equal high." These are magnets for order flow because they're psychological levels. Price often fails at relative equal highs on retest (lower high = weakening), or uses them as springboards.
- **How to identify:** Mark on chart significant prior highs/lows. When price approaches the same level, note it as relative equal.
- **How to use:** At relative equal highs, expect either: (1) failure and reversal lower, or (2) breakout above. Don't sit at the level; use it to anticipate direction. If bearish bias, shorting at relative equal high is high-probability (shorts cover above, pushing it down).
- **Example in this video:** Nasdaq overnight session 7:00 a.m. ET: relative equal highs from prior trading. Price traded to them, failed, reversed lower. This is the draw used for the day's trading plan.
- **Timestamps:** [0:12:07](https://youtu.be/z5ytUvVeUoQ?t=727) — "relative equal highs... why is this important?"; [0:13:42](https://youtu.be/z5ytUvVeUoQ?t=822) — showing how market used this level
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Relative equal highs are psychological. They're not perfectly precise levels; use them with inefficiencies (FVGs, order blocks) nearby for confirmation.

=== [2026 #132 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ February 10, 2026 (ID E8EtGkn-x5w)] — Buy-side and sell-side liquidity as stops of the other side  (restated for beginners)
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** Highs/relative equal highs hold buy stops above them: anyone short places a protective buy stop there. Lows hold the protective sell stops of longs: that is sell-side liquidity. Liquidity is "the lifeblood of any market"; he looks for where the market will draw before volume bars print, whereas volume/footprint data are hindsight.
- **Timestamps:** [0:19:32](https://youtu.be/E8EtGkn-x5w?t=1172) — BSL above highs, SSL below lows; [0:24:30](https://youtu.be/E8EtGkn-x5w?t=1470) — liquidity as fuel and anchor points

=== [2026 #132 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ February 10, 2026 (ID E8EtGkn-x5w)] — Counterparty theory: why smart money shorts old highs
- **Kind:** definition
- **What ICT says:** When price takes out buy-side, smart money sells short into it, using the resting buy orders (breakout buyers and short stop-outs) as counterparties; they keep throwing sell orders to pair off as many as they can. They then need a lower price to get out: the most advantageous places are old lows and relative equal lows close by, where retail longs have their stops (sell stops that become market sells) and sell at a lower price. The lower target can also be a sell-side discount inefficiency. Every setup uses this counterparty logic. Retail does not spoof; retail traders pull orders and close before their full stop is hit, so depth-of-market numbers may not be there when price arrives.
- **How to use:** "Find the counterparty": you don't need to be right initially about direction; know the liquidity, the inefficiency in between, and the next draw. A trader long who dodged a stop by not being taken out but never banked profit will watch price wilt; when price is near the sell side, the setup is easy.
- **Timestamps:** [0:27:45](https://youtu.be/E8EtGkn-x5w?t=1665) — buy side taken, who sells short; [0:28:33](https://youtu.be/E8EtGkn-x5w?t=1713) — where they get out (old lows); [0:36:35](https://youtu.be/E8EtGkn-x5w?t=2195) — retail doesn't spoof; [0:39:47](https://youtu.be/E8EtGkn-x5w?t=2387) — handshake with existing interest, counterparty theory

=== [2026 #132 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ February 10, 2026 (ID E8EtGkn-x5w)] — Target: one tick below the sell-side liquidity pool; minimum 20 handles
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** For a beginner, don't get greedy: aim to exit one tick below the level you think is the sell-side pool (or one tick short of the target). Expect more protraction beyond it, but the beginner objective is "just below the line". Determine how much range there is; he wants 20 handles (or 20 pips in forex). On a micro that is about $2 per point (20 handles = $40, "the biggest 40 bucks you'll ever make" the first time, in real money).
- **Timestamps:** [0:37:23](https://youtu.be/E8EtGkn-x5w?t=2243) — dynamic range, don't get greedy; [0:38:10](https://youtu.be/E8EtGkn-x5w?t=2290) — one tick below the pool; [0:40:34](https://youtu.be/E8EtGkn-x5w?t=2434) — 20 handles/pips; [0:41:21](https://youtu.be/E8EtGkn-x5w?t=2481) — $40 on a micro

=== [2026 #136 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ January 29, 2026 (ID 8wHewi6N6dg)] — Draw on liquidity first ("where is it going?")  (aliases ICT uses: "where is it drawing to", "the secret ingredient", "reach for", cookie-cutter run)
- **Kind:** rule / philosophy (central lesson of the video)
- **What ICT says:**
  - The first and most important skill is knowing where the market is likely to go next. Until you know that, entries, stop loss and targets are "not even important yet".
  - Knowing the draw lets you frame risk (where your stop goes), which he calls the first real measure of understanding what you are doing.
  - Price is always doing one of two things: running to liquidity or running to an inefficiency, above or below the market. It is not "buying and selling pressure", indicators, or bookmap bubbles. He calls the repeating pieces "signatures", not patterns.
  - If you know the draw correctly you can trade "any old thing" (his "flip a quarter" line) with a realistic stop and still win.
  - Beginners should not try to pick entries at all at first. Only pick the direction/draw; afterwards, in hindsight, look back to see what PD arrays (FVG, breaker, Silver Bullet, market maker model) formed in the delivery.
  - The "cookie cutter" moves he lists, in rising difficulty: (1) run from an inefficiency to a liquidity pool; (2) run from a liquidity pool to an inefficiency; (3) run from one liquidity pool to another, e.g. a false breakout above an old high into a false breakout below an old low (he calls this the highest form of what he teaches, "not all that complex" but needs experience).
- **How to identify:** On the chart ask: where is the nearest premium (or discount) liquidity, and where is the nearest inefficiency? Price in a discount reaches up for the nearest short-term high / minor BSL or an unfilled gap.
- **How to use:**
  - Pick the smallest, easiest objective. Short-term high (minor BSL) is easier to find than an inefficiency because shorts will have trailed their buy-stop protection just above it.
  - Two-stage exit with multiple micros: partial at the minor BSL / short-term high, final exit (his word "terminus") at the low of the inefficiency above.
  - If the expected draw is not clearly there, do nothing; be content with it.
- **Example in this video:** NQ, pre-8:30 to 9:00 ET. Price had sold off overnight leaving lows jagged (no equal lows). A minor BSL sat at the lower of two relative equal highs. Above it was an upper-quadrant inefficiency. After the 8:30 news price ran to the short-term high and then up to the bottom (low) of the inefficiency, then sold hard. He calls that "low-hanging fruit" and says "you could be done".
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:09:45](https://youtu.be/8wHewi6N6dg?t=585) — first identification of minor BSL above the .375 level and the inefficiency (volume imbalance + SIBI) above it
  - [0:28:25](https://youtu.be/8wHewi6N6dg?t=1705) — "you already remove 90% of the struggle if you can define the range"
  - [0:52:58](https://youtu.be/8wHewi6N6dg?t=3178) — the three cookie-cutter run types
  - [1:39:32](https://youtu.be/8wHewi6N6dg?t=5972) — "if you know where the market's going, you can trade any old thing"
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He says you do not need price to take the range high or break the range low; the whole point is "disarming" the need to be right about how far it goes. This is the same "first lesson" he claims to have taught on his first live stream after the BabyPips days.

=== [2026 #136 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ January 29, 2026 (ID 8wHewi6N6dg)] — Jagged vs smooth levels (relative equal highs/lows as the draw)  (aliases ICT uses: made jagged, smooth levels, relative equal highs, relative equal lows)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:**
  - A side of the market where price keeps making lower low after lower low with no relative equal lows is "jagged". There is nothing smooth below to draw to.
  - After a jagged run, look for where the market left smooth levels (relative equal highs or lows). The market gravitates to the smooth level. He said he has taught this "this year and previous years".
  - A shift in market structure after the jagged drop, keyed off a sell-side-imbalance gap that then inverts, signals the turn toward the smooth level.
- **How to identify:** Count the lows in the leg down: if each is lower and none matches another, it is jagged. The relative equal highs above, where bodies converge (here near the .125 level), are smooth.
- **How to use:** Use the smooth level (or the nearest minor pool before it) as the draw after a jagged run; wait for 8:30 or 9:30 as the starting gun (see time section).
- **Example in this video:** NQ overnight: lows kept dropping with no equal lows (jagged). Relative equal highs sat above with bodies converging on the .125 line from yesterday's RTH opening range gap. He used this as the "I like that as a draw, staying inside the range, needing it not to trade there" idea.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:26:05](https://youtu.be/8wHewi6N6dg?t=1565) — "levels that are smooth after levels that have been made jagged"
  - [0:26:52](https://youtu.be/8wHewi6N6dg?t=1612) — explains jagged meaning nothing relatively equal on the way down
  - [0:46:33](https://youtu.be/8wHewi6N6dg?t=2793) — jagged lows vs smooth relative equal highs again
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** none contradicting earlier teaching; this reuses his relative equal highs/lows liquidity concept, framed as "jagged vs smooth".

=== [2026 #136 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ January 29, 2026 (ID 8wHewi6N6dg)] — Three tools: draw on liquidity, frame risk, entry mechanism  (aliases ICT uses: "three paint brushes", Bob Ross)
- **Kind:** philosophy / framework
- **What ICT says:**
  - All you need: (1) the draw on liquidity, (2) the ability to frame risk (where the stop goes), (3) an entry mechanism. Everyone looks at the same chart; what separates profitability is knowing what to do with it.
  - Over time you find the one PD array that is easy for you to recognise (Silver Bullet, order block, breaker, 2022 model, Reaper, etc.). If it is not present, do nothing and be content with that; the model did not tell you to act.
  - Do not abandon the model you were using to chase a new one because it changed; if the one you stopped using delivers, take responsibility.
  - Some students must use a breaker; others the Silver Bullet; do not force yourself into a mold. He can use different PD arrays and models on a 15 or 30 second chart all day without repeating a setup, but students cannot replicate that.
  - Entry mechanism he gave 1990s students: an order block, sell short on a stop (sell weakness), buy on a stop (buy strength).
- **Timestamps:**
  - [1:00:52](https://youtu.be/8wHewi6N6dg?t=3652) — do not force a mold; freedom to choose tools
  - [1:40:18](https://youtu.be/8wHewi6N6dg?t=6018) — three paintbrushes
  - [1:41:06](https://youtu.be/8wHewi6N6dg?t=6066) — when your PD array is not present, do nothing
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** Also says: do not email him about Nasdaq 24-hour trading; he says he will have "new weapons" (no detail).

=== [2026 #137 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ January 13, 2026 (ID Rwao8f6oD0Q)] — Trendline "phantom": ascending trendline as sell-stop liquidity, not support  (aliases ICT uses: trend line phantom, retail diagonal support fallacy, channel, pitchfork)
- **Kind:** refinement / opinion on retail concepts
- **What ICT says:**
  - An ascending diagonal "support" that retail traders see (as a channel or pitchfork) is a fallacy for him: the stops resting below the trendline and below a specific low are the liquidity price targets.
  - Retail buys the apparent breakout; they are trapped when price first dips to absorb the liquidity into the first presented FVG, then runs.
  - He says he does not normally draw the trendline; he draws it for teaching so students understand the internal dialogue. Also something about looking at three points where the line is trying to gravitate (caption unclear).
  - Price, time and liquidity agree: it is anchored to a time (the first presented FVG, relative to the 7:00 start) and a price/liquidity (the stops below).
  - Homework: re-listen to the live stream to confirm he said the trendline would be the liquidity and that this FVG was the first presented one. Review the next day after sleep, to see what you missed and to solidify the model.
- **How to identify:** Retail-looking diagonal support with obvious stops below a specific low (minor SSL).
- **How to use:** Expect a run down into the trendline liquidity / first presented FVG, then reversal in the direction of the higher-timeframe draw.
- **Example in this video:** NQ Monday 12 Jan pre-9:30: ascending trendline from the Asian session; liquidity resting below one low; price opens and trades down into the first presented FVG and absorbs it, then reverses.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:20:37](https://youtu.be/Rwao8f6oD0Q?t=1237) — "trend line phantom", retail diagonal support fallacy
  - [0:22:11](https://youtu.be/Rwao8f6oD0Q?t=1331) — stops below the trendline are what they target
  - [0:23:00](https://youtu.be/Rwao8f6oD0Q?t=1380) — liquidity below the low; market trades down into the first presented FVG
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** The "midpoint of the trend line phantom" and "three points" statements are caption-unclear.

=== [2026 #137 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ January 13, 2026 (ID Rwao8f6oD0Q)] — Daily bias, higher-timeframe draw, and "hold for the close" (Larry Williams)  (aliases ICT uses: higher time frame draw on liquidity, daily candle close, trading with the larger move)
- **Kind:** rule / bias
- **What ICT says:**
  - Frame direction from the higher time frame draw: here the daily relative equal highs around 26,133.5 were the draw (discussed about a week earlier). The best intraday moves are framed by daily bias, even though counter-trend shorts can work briefly.
  - Idea credited to Larry Williams: if you day trade, be on side with where the daily candle is likely to close and hold something into the close. Decide before you sit down whether the daily candle should close up, and look for longs, holding at least part of the position.
  - His own timing version: long at the 9:30 to 10:00 opening range or pre-market from 7:00 onward, then off-board something between 10:00 and 11:00 (profit-taking; London close; entering NY lunch).
  - Trading only on the side of the daily candle's expected extension lets you catch the big running moves; trading every minor fluctuation against that bias (e.g., a short from the Asian session BSL FVG) will not.
  - Time and price meet like a multiplication table (liquidity price on one axis, time on the other): the algorithm finds where time and price meet.
- **How to identify:** Daily chart: nearby old high/low or relative equal highs/lows or imbalance above/below price; recent liquidity events (e.g., sell side just run).
- **How to use:** Trade only in the daily-bias direction, partial off 10:00 to 11:00, hold remainder to the close if running.
- **Example in this video:** Daily bullish toward 26,133.5; Monday's session (12 Jan) went long from the first presented FVG at the 9:30 release.
- **Timestamps:**
  - [0:13:17](https://youtu.be/Rwao8f6oD0Q?t=797) — the question for the day: run to 26,133.5 or use CPI for a sentiment drop first
  - [0:24:35](https://youtu.be/Rwao8f6oD0Q?t=1475) — Larry Williams "hold for the close" idea
  - [0:25:21](https://youtu.be/Rwao8f6oD0Q?t=1521) — off-board something from 10:00 to 11:00
  - [0:26:54](https://youtu.be/Rwao8f6oD0Q?t=1614) — time and price like the multiplication table
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** He explicitly says it will "be in the book" (his three-book set) but is all on YouTube.

=== [2026 #138 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ January 12, 2026 (ID dt9mk4n5g_o)] — Smooth highs / smooth lows = relative equal highs / lows (engineered liquidity, retail S/R trap)  (aliases: "obvious level", "single high / single low" when only one)
- **Kind:** definition / refinement
- **What ICT says:**
  - He looks for "smooth" highs or lows within a time frame: clean, textbook-looking support/resistance (high, high, high) that retail is taught to trust. Smooth areas tend to be made jagged: price runs to the level, trades through it, and the cleanness is destroyed.
  - He does NOT trade at the level when it forms; he looks back across the session for it, because that is the trap. It is "engineered liquidity": price runs to an obvious level on purpose.
  - Retail logic is "diametrically opposed" to his: if a new trader would see it as support/resistance and he sees it as a likely run for liquidity, he puts it at roughly 90% likelihood of being right, and the last 10% is why you use stops.
  - His definition of the relative-equal-high structure: price runs to a level, a later high falls short of it (closest to current price is LOWER than the anchoring one), then the little "red herring" run. The failed second high is the retail "strong resistance" trap.
  - Why the liquidity is bigger there: new shorts entered at the failed high and existing short stops were moved down to that second high, so the pool just below the first high is larger and a stronger draw. This is how "bigger bubbles" are known without BookMap.
  - If there is no smooth high/low inside the range (e.g., a single swing high followed by continuation lower), then the single high/low (e.g., overnight high) is the target.
  - Odds of a run into the level increase "exponentially" if the smooth level sits at or around a quadrant level of the whole range (e.g., lower half of the 7 pm–5 am range).
  - Zoom out/compress ranges so the smooth edges stand out ("nose to the tree" analogy).
- **How to identify:** eye jumps to the level; flat highs/lows in a session; second one slightly under the first (for highs).
- **How to use:** expect a pre-market (7–9 am) run to it; pair with first presented FVG as the entry catalyst; target is run on the stops above/below.
- **Example in this video:** NQ overnight Sun Jan 11–Mon Jan 12: smooth highs formed Sunday around 10:00 pm ET; price then only fell overnight; a later, lower high failed to reach the first = the trap; at 7:00 am price displaced up and ran to them.
- **Timestamps:** [0:25:42](https://youtu.be/dt9mk4n5g_o?t=1542) — smooth highs, engineered liquidity · [0:57:34](https://youtu.be/dt9mk4n5g_o?t=3454) — the lower right high is "the trap" · [1:05:41](https://youtu.be/dt9mk4n5g_o?t=3941) — why the pool is larger · [1:42:54](https://youtu.be/dt9mk4n5g_o?t=6174) — beginner minimum
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** refines earlier "equal highs/lows" teaching: explicitly requires the second extreme not to exceed the anchor (see next concept).

=== [2026 #138 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ January 12, 2026 (ID dt9mk4n5g_o)] — Time then price: premium market → look for smooth lows next
- **Kind:** rule / mindset
- **What ICT says:** after the time window is complete and price has pushed to the high end of the range (premium), the best thing to look for is a relative equal low in that action, because the market is more likely to drop and knock out weak bulls (then maybe resume up), or reverse. You do not care which way it goes after the run; you are looking for low-hanging fruit, not trying to ride the entire move or be right.
- **Timestamps:** [0:41:39](https://youtu.be/dt9mk4n5g_o?t=2499) · [0:43:15](https://youtu.be/dt9mk4n5g_o?t=2595)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** the same logic is applied after the 7:00 am run: look inside the 2–7 am range for relative equal lows (London low bulls have made money) and the CE of the Jan 2 first-presented FVG.

=== [2026 #139 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts In Action \ January 06, 2026 (ID Jxl5UJk_ylc)] — Buy-Side Liquidity (BSL) Above Structural Highs
- **Kind:** entry model
- **What ICT says:** "I'm wanting the buy side is resting above the pre-market session highs. And then this high up here, which is overnight relative equal highs." The market is biased to run higher and collect stops/liquidity resting above these structural resistance points.
- **How to identify:** Identify overnight session highs (electronic trading, pre-market). Mark where relative equal highs formed. These become BSL pools above the current price.
- **How to use:** Use these points as targets for long entries. Enter on wicks or pullbacks into demand (discount areas), with the expectation that price will run up to collect these higher BSL pools.
- **Example in this video:** NQ on Jan 6: overnight relative equal highs were marked; ICT wanted to see price rally up to challenge and take out these levels. Entry was taken on a wick down into demand, with BSL above as the target.
- **Timestamps:** [0:03:03](https://youtu.be/Jxl5UJk_ylc?t=183) (identifying BSL above pre-market highs and overnight equal highs)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** BSL is not the same as a simple "resistance"—it's specifically liquidity pools where stop-losses and limit orders rest, waiting to be hit.

=== [2026 #140 — ICT 2026 Smart Money Concepts Lecture \ January 02, 2026 (ID 1X0A-Lgf9RY)] — Relative equal highs/lows as sweep candidates
- **Kind:** refinement (restates rule)
- **What ICT says:** what makes a pool a good candidate to be run: the right-hand extreme is slightly lower (for highs) / slightly higher (for lows) than the left one. For lows: "a low with a higher low to the right of it" is a high-probability sweep candidate; the market trades down through it. For highs: the equal highs were an old buy pool that price blasted through at the open before going to the premium inefficiency.
- **Timestamps:** [0:26:06](https://youtu.be/1X0A-Lgf9RY?t=1566) · [0:45:22](https://youtu.be/1X0A-Lgf9RY?t=2722)
- **Nuance / caveats / changes:** consistent with the anchor-point rule in L26-138.

=== [2026 #141 — From Vision To Execution (ID rXgEMIJ1fQg)] — Casting a vision / Draw on liquidity first  (aliases: "cast forth a vision", narrative, "the plot", "where is price drawing to")
- **Kind:** rule (core method)
- **What ICT says:**
  - Every trader is projecting the future of price; if you are not, you are gambling. The "don't predict" line is dismissed as nonsense, since the vision is exactly what you are doing.
  - The first primary skill is knowing where price is likely to go next (the draw on liquidity). Without knowing what price is reaching for, you cannot get in rhythm with it.
  - Veteran students do not try to work out the next two or three runs. They look at price at this moment and ask what it is likely to do now. If nothing is clear, they do nothing, which removes worry and fear of missing out.
  - Once the vision is set (e.g. "price is reaching for overnight lows") you keep it on the chart and "do not abandon the idea" until price proves you wrong and it is obvious.
  - Price can only be doing one of four things: drawing to sell-side liquidity, drawing to buy-side liquidity, running for a premium inefficiency above, or running for an inefficiency below. Only a geopolitical event breaks that.
- **How to identify:** ask "from the market price right now, where is it drawing?" Name the pool (here: relative equal lows from the overnight session) and then judge each candle by whether it serves that plot.
- **How to use:** the vision sets which candles matter and what is "permissible" for price. Ask what limits you would place on price if you controlled it. If you cannot build a narrative for why price should do what you want, you are forcing a setup.
- **Example in this video:** the Dec 2025 NQ day below. Vision: price rallied in pre-market to an old FVG, rejected, so it should go back down and take the overnight lows. The vision held from before 9:30 until the lows were taken in the late-morning macro.
- **Timestamps:** [0:05:37](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=337) vision = what every trader does, otherwise gambling · [0:06:25](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=385) vision = the draw on liquidity · [0:20:02](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=1202) "do not abandon the idea" until proven wrong · [0:40:00](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=2400) only four possible draws
- **Nuance / caveats:** he says this is hard for brand-new students and recommends coming back after three and six months of experience (see Process). He also cites Habakkuk 2:2–3 (write the vision down, make it plain) as the inspiration for the lecture [0:43:12](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=2592).

=== [2026 #141 — From Vision To Execution (ID rXgEMIJ1fQg)] — Overnight range lows as sell-side liquidity draw  (aliases: overnight lows, midnight-to-7:00 range, Asian/London lows)
- **Kind:** rule (routine)
- **What ICT says:** the easiest routine: mark off midnight ET to 7:00 AM ET and take the lowest low (and the highest high) of that window. If those lows are relative equal lows, "that's a loaded deal", it is where sell-side liquidity rests and price will want to seek it if the day is bearish. It does not always happen that day, "but that's how I classify it".
- **How to use:** it is the draw you cast the vision toward. Hold the vision (see Casting a vision) while the opening-range FVG, macros and small FVGs play out beneath it.
- **Example in this video:** overnight low was a relative equal low and a clear standout. Price took it later in the session after the 10:50–11:10 macro displacement, where the first run lower goes through the nearby sell side and then the overnight sell side.
- **Timestamps:** [0:17:40](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=1060) mark midnight to 7:00 ET lowest low · [0:19:14](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=1154) "that's the draw" · [0:34:24](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=2064) price dives through the overnight low
- **Nuance / caveats:** "Not all the time, not that very day." It is a classification of where liquidity is, not a guarantee.

=== [2026 #141 — From Vision To Execution (ID rXgEMIJ1fQg)] — Relative equal lows (and highs): the "slightly higher low to the right" rule  (aliases: equal lows, RELs)
- **Kind:** refinement
- **What ICT says:** two lows are relative equal when the one on the right is slightly higher than the one on the left. That is the pattern that makes a strong candidate for an algorithmic sell-side raid. If the right-hand low is instead lower than the left (not relative equal), the raid is less likely to be immediate; price can eventually trade through, but first retraces for an inefficiency above or a short-term high (an "efficiency premium" or buy-side pool) to the left.
- **How to identify:** compare successive swing lows; right low marginally higher than the left = relative equal. He says "that's how your eye is supposed to identify it".
- **How to use:** treat as a high-probability target when the plot is bearish. If you see the non-equal version, expect a deeper retracement before the run.
- **Example in this video:** (1) zoomed in during the opening-range consolidation: two lows, the right one slightly higher, flagged as a strong candidate for a sell-side raid; price later takes them. (2) PM session: minor sell side with relative equal lows (lows to the right slightly higher) just above an old AM-session RTH low.
- **Timestamps:** [0:23:59](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=1439) what makes them relative equal · [0:24:46](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=1486) the non-equal version needs a deeper retracement · [0:40:48](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=2448) PM relative equal lows
- **Nuance / caveats:** he has taught this "for years", so it is a restatement.

=== [2026 #141 — From Vision To Execution (ID rXgEMIJ1fQg)] — Partials  (aliases: taking partials at liquidity)
- **Kind:** rule
- **What ICT says:** he defends taking partials against people who say not to. The relative equal lows in the PM session are "perfect candidates" to take a partial if you are short from up above. After the speed develops (elongated candles below those lows), price then reaches for the next sell side (the old AM-session RTH low).
- **How to use:** take a partial at nearby, obvious liquidity (relative equal lows), then watch for acceleration (elongated candles through them) as confirmation that the further pool is the target.
- **Timestamps:** [0:41:36](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=2496) speed below the lows = reach for the next sell side · [0:42:24](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=2544) partials defended
- **Nuance / caveats:** no percentage for the partial is given.

=== [2026 #141 — From Vision To Execution (ID rXgEMIJ1fQg)] — Liquidity void (strict definition)
- **Kind:** definition / refinement
- **What ICT says:** a liquidity void is only a real gap where there is no trading at all, "no buying and selling in there". A suspension block, FVG or volume imbalance is not a void; each is an inefficiency with trading on both sides.
- **Timestamps:** [0:45:38](https://youtu.be/rXgEMIJ1fQg?t=2738)
- **Nuance / caveats:** tightens how the term "void" should be used in the playbook ("the only void that exists in price").
